# Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4Exp) optimization ledger

**Prefill checkpoint, 2026-10-06:** structural substitutions continue to produce
measured gains under the production contract. GR-up ordered DPP improves
canonical prefill +6.57%/+7.15%/+6.98% at p512/p1024/p4096; router ordered
DPP adds a separately measured +0.553%/+0.486%/+0.589% (MR-1). These are
independent same-host A/B results and must not be added into a combined rate.
The original router p4096 request aggregate is 0.364% worse with an abrupt
decode slowdown. A focused same-protocol recheck does not reproduce it, without
replacing the original samples; no decode-speed claim follows.
The compensated dense split candidate fails the full production envelope.
That failure closes that candidate, not all kernel mechanisms or the GUFO gap.
Artifacts: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-05-qwen4exp-gr-dpp-reduction/` and
`benchmarks/results/2026-10-05-qwen4exp-router-dpp/`.

- **Ledger date:** 2026-09-29 (expanded after a cross-document coverage review;
  §10 records the source inventory and experiment crosswalk)
- **Host:** Framework `gfx1151`, Radeon 8060S / Strix Halo, machine
  `55ea6c509d0b49eea8de7094a1023668`
- **Model / quant:** unsloth `Qwen3.8-Flash-Next` `UD-Q4_K_XL`, BF16 KV, warm
  PLE, chunk1024, shipped `production` profile

**This is the single open-work list for closing the prefill, decode and MTP gap
on this model, and the single registry of what has already been tried.** Every
open row names a measured cost where available, or explicitly calls for
measurement, the evidence that bounds the opportunity, the first bounded
experiment, and the gate that decides it. Rows leave the open
list only by promotion, by recorded rejection, or by a stated blocker, and every
rejection lands in §7 with the condition that would reopen it.

### 1.2 Kernel time per family, per 1K prompt tokens

**MoE expert population — one figure, so this is not re-derived again (`20261004T`).** The
routed MoE is ~25% of the prefill, and its expert population has been stated three different
ways in this ledger. Settled: **use ~310–368 of 512 (61–72%)**, never 203. The three
measurements, strongest first:

| measurement | figure | input | entry |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| MX-1 admissibility capture | **310** of 512 | **real natural-input** `general_en-p4096` | `20260930T` |
| hot-expert screen | **335** of 512 | **12 real canonical cases** (prefill chunks + 48 decode steps) | `20260910T111500` |
| per-category census | **341.5–387.9** of 512 | real natural input, four categories, 1,024 tokens | `20261004T151527`, `20261004T151931` |
| ~~population probe~~ | ~~203~~ of 512 | **synthetic token IDs `4000..5023`** — a repetitive degenerate sequence, which concentrates routing | `20260927T081954` |

The 203 is **an artefact of the synthetic input, not a campaign-wide error**: it was used only
in the population-*replay* fixtures, which is how ED-1's replay and EG-4's skew figure inherited
it. Both rows were audited and **neither is invalidated** — ED-1 is reinforced by a flatter
distribution and EG-4's conclusion holds more strongly at `code-p4096`, where the skew's
tile-count effect is ~0%. The defect was **scoped to the replay fixture**. See worklogs
`20261004T152056` (closure audit) and `20261004T152135` (the sweep of all 8 rows citing a
population quantity).

**Consequence, measured not inferred:** `20260910T111500` states it outright — *"~1.05 GB of
expert weights streamed per capture — the layer nearly fully streams its expert pool every chunk
regardless of scheduling."* At 61–72% of experts active, the expert-weight traffic behind the
selected MoE kernels is close to the full matrix, so the headroom a selected/sparse route can
have over a dense one is much smaller than the 40% model implied.

Ours is the `42ce71156` attribution (§1 of STATUS) divided by four; gufo's is
its own 2026-09-21 d0 pp2048 profile (1362.8 ms kernel time in a 1380 ms span,
[EXPERIMENTS.md](https://github.com/gufo-org/gufo/blob/fee9d2a/docs/models/qwen3.8-flash-next/EXPERIMENTS.md))
divided by two. **Approximate by construction:** the prompt lengths differ
(4096 against 2048, so our attention includes sparse rows theirs does not),
the taxonomies differ, and gufo's figure is author-reported.

| Family | Ours ms / 1K | gufo ms / 1K (author-reported) | Notes |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Routed experts (gate/up + down) | 1401.7 | ~238 (35%) | Ours post-unroll 3377.1 + 2229.8 ms, including 1240 ms exact repair |
| Dense projections | 1308.7 | ~198 (29%) | Gufo reports 30-34 TFLOPS for dense F16 against its measured 59 TFLOPS ceiling |
| Hyper-connections | 631.4 | ~72 combine (10.5%) + part of ~48 (7%, HC down/inject/shared) | Different bucket boundaries |
| GDN | 205.4 | ~65 (9.5%) | Different sources and shapes |
| Attention and indexer | 350.6 | ~37 (5.5%) | Ours predates the full-attention promotion; theirs has no sparse rows at 2048 |
| MoE router/reduce | 189.6 | not separated | No paired comparison |
| **Total** | **4168** (post-unroll 16673.2 / 4) | **681** | Not a same-host speedup ratio |

**1.2 is an exploratory cross-source cost sketch, not a same-host gap table.**
It suggests investigating several families, but these costs cannot rank local
recoverable milliseconds or establish that every family is behind.

Our rows mix the pre-unroll anchor (dense, HC, GDN, attention, reduce) with
the post-unroll MoE capture; `elementwise_norm` and the remainder are omitted,
so the rows do not sum exactly to the post-unroll total.

### 1.2.1 Verified same-file comparison and the dense-scope result — 2026-10-04 UTC

**THE UNIFORMITY CLAIM BELOW IS REFUTED AT THE HARDWARE LEVEL (`20261004T`).** The
family ratios are a wall-time reading; a prefill-only `rocprofv3` capture
(`benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-prefill-stall-census/`) shows the two largest
prefill kernels have **opposite** stall profiles: HC-1 (`gr_up_sigmoid_mean`, 18.26% of
prefill) sits at **30.82% LDS wait** while every other top-ten prefill kernel is at
**≤0.35%** — a ~90x spread, not a uniform one. The ratios below remain correct as
wall-time ratios; what does not survive is reading them as one shared per-kernel
throughput deficit with no family-level structure.

Replaces §1.2 as the comparison basis. Same host, same file, production
profile, both engines' own prefill timed as a whole.

**gufo's `pp` is a genuine full-prompt, device-synchronized prefill.** Its
`src/cli/bench/bench.cpp` times `t0 -> ForwardPromptBatch(prompt_tokens, depth)
+ hipDeviceSynchronize() -> t1` with `restore_depth()` outside the window and
no reuse inside it, and the wall clock agrees (`-p 4096 -n 0 -d 0 -r 2` = 53 s
against 45.30 s model load plus 3 x 2.40 s of predicted prefill).

| engine | tree | chunk | 4096-token prefill | tok/s |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| gufo `98641a6` (prebuilt `8ca63994...`) | gufo binary | - | 2.40 s | **1705.94** |
| hipEngine production | this tree | 4096 | 13.87 s | 295.40 |
| hipEngine production | this tree | 1024 | 15.03 s | 272.46 |

**The gap is 5.78x, and at matched granularity (one 4096-row pass, both sides
traced) it is 5.77x and uniform**: dense projections 6.93x (43.6% of our
prefill), routed MoE 5.59x (31.7%), attention/GDN/router/other 4.61x. It is
**not** launch count or scheduling - gufo issues 4,237 launches per prefill
against our 2,766, 1.53x more. The difference is per-kernel throughput, so no
single family is the answer and no single port closes it.

**Measure every hipEngine row here with `uv run`.** `python3 scripts/<harness>.py`
resolves `import hipengine` through the conda editable `.pth`, which points at
`/home/lhl/hipEngine-main` - a separate clone, 209 files different, with a
different profile binding set. Same command, same case, one process per arm:
**0.12% within-arm drift and -6.3% prefill / -8.5% decode between the trees.**
`uv run` uses this tree's `.venv` and is correct. The `.pth` dates from
2026-09-21, so any artifact predating this note whose hipEngine rows came from
a raw `python3` invocation describes a different code base than it records.

**The dense-projection bucket is where the milliseconds are, and its scope lever is
now spent.** The wide-row f16 Q8_0 route runs 0.0193 s per layer against the exact
coltile chain's 0.171 s, so `PRODUCTION_Q8_DENSE_WIDE_PREFILL_LAYERS` is the
largest single prefill lever. It was extended from 16-47 to **13-47** on
2026-10-04 UTC, a measured **+3.0% prefill at `code-p4096`** (`+2.6%` / `+3.2%` /
`+3.0%` across p512 / p1024 / p4096, decode unchanged). Extending it to 0-47
measures **+18.876% prefill and +5.786% decode** at `code-p4096` against a 0.372%
same-code control, with identical generated-token digests - and **fails the
production gate**: mean KL 1.468e-3 (1.47x over), p95 7.517e-3 (1.50x over), max
5.140e-2 (1.03x over), top-1 0.98837, all four categories failing. The ladder
closes the region above 13-47:

| scope | kl_mean | kl_p95 | kl_max | top-1 | hard gates | why |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | --- |
| 16-47 | 4.527e-4 | - | 2.341e-2 | 1538 (0.99354) | **PASS** | previous certified default |
| **13-47** | **6.229e-4** | **3.158e-3** | **2.606e-2** | **1538 (0.99354)** | **PASS** | **promoted default; deepest passing scope** |
| 14-47 | 5.190e-4 | - | 3.979e-2 | 1536 (0.99225) | **PASS** | dominated by 13-47 on top-1 and max |
| 12-47 | 6.406e-4 | 3.229e-3 | 3.249e-2 | 1532 (0.98966) | FAIL | aggregate top-1, one row short |
| 11-47 | 7.819e-4 | 3.868e-3 | **6.957e-2** | 1536 (0.99225) | FAIL | `kl_max` 1.39x over |
| 10-47 | 8.370e-4 | 4.291e-3 | 3.554e-2 | 1528 (0.98708) | FAIL | top-1 **and** `general_en` 1.08x |
| 8-47 | 8.839e-4 | 4.647e-3 | 4.146e-2 | 1534 (0.99096) | FAIL | `general_en` mean KL 1.181e-3 |
| 0-47 | 1.468e-3 | - | 5.140e-2 | 1530 | FAIL | KL and top-1, 6 scopes |

**13-47 was added on 2026-10-04 UTC and is the promoted default.** It passes with
1.6-1.9x margin on every binding gate and all four categories above their 0.97
floors (code 0.99225, general_en 0.98708, general_ja 1.0, mixed_ja_en 0.99483).
**It dominates 14-47**, which the previous revision of this table called the
deepest passing scope: 13-47 scores top-1 0.99354 and `kl_max` 2.606e-2 against
14-47's 0.99225 and 3.979e-2. So the newly-passing scope is also numerically
stronger, and this is not a speed-for-quality trade against the passing frontier.

**The three new points sharpen the non-monotonicity into a warning.** 11-47 and
12-47 fail on **different axes** (`kl_max` at 1.39x against aggregate top-1 by one
row), and 10-47 is worse than 12-47 on top-1 while 8-47's top-1 beats both. No
monotone search over scope finds 13-47; it has to be tested. The earlier revision
of this table inferred "deepest passing" from 14-47 because it had not tested 13.

**Why the 8-47 lever is now closed rather than pending.** The residual drift is
**accumulation-order error, and that is verified rather than inferred.** Both the
wide route and the coltile parent use f16 operands with f32 accumulation and the
same `wmma_f32_16x16x16_f16` intrinsic, and both accumulate K sequentially in
steps of 16. Their dequant paths produce identical f16 values: the wide route
computes `d0 * q` in f32 and rounds once, the parent computes it in f16 and rounds
once, and both are a single correctly-rounded operation on the same exact product,
which is exactly representable in f32 (11 significand bits times 7 is at most 18,
well inside 24). `tests/test_gpu_gguf_q8_0_dense_wide.py::test_dense_wide_matches_
the_f16_reference` asserts the bound and names the mechanism - "Accumulation-order
error only: f32 accumulation over K terms with f16 operands. A structural bug
misses by the f16 rounding scale instead" - and passes for every variant (20
passed). **WMMA on RDNA3 has no f32-operand form**; f16 is the most precise
available operand format and bf16 would be worse, so the route sits at the
accuracy floor of the formulation that gives it its speed. The
`HIPENGINE_GGUF_Q8_DENSE_WIDE_F16_ACT` knob is a no-op at these shapes because the
binder already resolves to the f32-input owner, so there is no activation-precision
trade either. **The remaining five layers cannot be recovered by making the route
more accurate.**

**The boundary is non-monotone in the tail and in top-1 while `kl_mean` and
`kl_p95` are monotone**: 12-47's `kl_max` is better than 14-47's while its mean
is worse, and 8-47's top-1 (1534) is better than 12-47's (1532). This reproduces
on a second, independent route the same non-monotonicity the staged-f32 ladder
found (15-47 failing while 14-47 and 16-47 pass). **Two consequences:** the
boundary cannot be located by monotone search, and a passing scope is not
evidence about a shallower one.

**13-47 is promoted (2026-10-04 UTC); 14-47 was declined on the previous
revision's evidence and is now dominated by it.** 14-47's speed was measured with
the same three-arm A/B/C: A 16-47 **268.877 pp**, B 14-47 **275.305 pp**, C 16-47
**272.589 pp**, so the pooled effect is **+1.69%** against a **+1.38%** same-code
drift. A signal-to-drift ratio of 1.2 is not a measurement, and changing the KV
rounding of two layers for a benefit that cannot be shown is the wrong trade.

**13-47 was promoted on a stronger case.** Its A/B/C was A 16-47 **268.30 pp**,
B 13-47 **277.70 pp**, C 16-47 **272.54 pp** - pooled **+2.69% prefill** and
**+0.92% decode** against **+1.58%** drift, a ratio of 1.7. That is better than
14-47's 1.2 but still not a headline number. **The reason it was retained is the
both-baselines argument plus the stronger numerics:** arm B sits temporally
between A and C and exceeds **both**, which a monotone session trend cannot
produce, so the effect is bounded below by **+1.89%** and above by **+3.50%**. The
end-to-end confirmation then landed inside that band - **+2.6% / +3.2% / +3.0%**
prefill across `code-p512` / `code-p1024` / `code-p4096` at chunk 1024, with decode
unchanged - which is three independent processes agreeing and is what a session
trend cannot do. **The lesson for the next such decision: a both-baselines A/B/C
plus an end-to-end check across shapes can admit an effect that a single 1.2-1.7x
signal-to-drift ratio would have declined.** Every candidate scope
leaves `prefill_last` KL at 4e-9 to 5e-8 with top-1 1.0 while `steady` carries
the whole drift: this route changes the *rounding* of the projections feeding
the KV cache, so the final prompt position barely moves while all 4096 cached
positions accumulate a difference that decode attention reads. That makes the
dense-prefill region a KV-arithmetic question, not a prefill-latency one.

Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-dense-wide-scope-ladder/`,
`benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-dense-wide-13-47-frontier/` (the 13-47 and
11-47 gates, the three-arm A/B/C, and the end-to-end confirmation),
`benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-gufo-prefill-gap-verified/`, worklog
`20261004T030644.952503Z`, `20261004T031136.427018Z`, `20261004T032242.378046Z`,
`20261004T043701.325729Z`, `20261004T132643.298003Z` (accumulation-order
mechanism).

### 1.3 Current attribution checkpoint — 2026-09-28 UTC

The [current-owner packet](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-current-attribution/README.md)
uses `2789c19d6`, the host/model/quant named above, BF16 KV, chunk1024 and
production manifest `35e57360e648bcc8ce8f21d89bf5583a0b4217061b306ec96616b2607cc6e565`.
It replaces historical costs for prioritizing short-context experiments, not
for claiming speedup. The section headings below still identify their dated
anchors; the packet supplies current symbols, roles and layer engagement.

One `code-p4096` capture attributes 10,708/10,708 launches and 15,741.963 ms
kernel union in a 16,319.888 ms request; unprofiled wall is 16,334.447 ms.
Current buckets are routed gate/up + down 5,605.754 ms, dense 4,553.007 ms,
HC 2,587.665 ms, attention 1,040.374 ms, GDN 844.295 ms, router/reduce
763.833 ms, norms 319.494 ms, indexer 17.520 ms and PLE 9.834 ms.
Thus routed experts, dense and HC remain the first bounded owner screens;
these costs do not imply additive or fully recoverable savings. F16 staging
casts total 131.001 ms, a measured ceiling for DP-5 before fusion costs.
Sparse attention is 24 launches / 455.080 ms; full attention is 36 /
438.971 ms. Do not reuse historical launch counts with these timings.

A supplemental copy trace reconciles 15,600.436 ms kernels plus 2.711 ms
observed device copies to a 15,603.147 ms interval union inside 16,159.403 ms.
Reset memset kernels are counted. HIP API host durations overlap device work;
the residual is not exclusively recoverable launch overhead.

Fixed-live4097 decode has 53.701–54.777 ms device time across three repeats.
Repeat 1 owners: MoE 24.625 ms, linear 23.121, QSA 2.594, GDN 2.386,
GR read 0.851, boundary 0.478 and PLE 0.034. Gate/up alone is 14.055 ms,
Q5_1 down 5.447 ms. Profiled wall varies 59–239 ms and unprofiled wall
124–141 ms, so serving-wrapper costs and throughput remain unqualified.
Prefill logits/manifest and decode token/state hashes agree across runs;
allocation growth and post-close live allocations are zero.

X-5 finds profiler scratch sizes 36 bytes (GDN) and 92 bytes (dense-wide).
Queue scratch allocations occur before, not during, the measured prefill.
Decode kernels report zero scratch. Metadata/disassembly and ordered
trigger/victim A/B remain necessary; neither throttling nor its absence is
proven. X-11 receives engagement evidence only, not repair-oracle/task coverage.

The [scratch metadata/pilot packet](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-scratch-pilot/README.md)
confirms 36-byte GDN and 92-byte dense-wide private segments in the cached
code objects, with scratch loads/stores in disassembly. Four fresh ordered
GDN pilots (sham/scratch/scratch/sham) preserve outputs/state and show no large
persistent penalty on the scratch-free GDN victim (after/before 0.99838–1.00057).
This is not the scratch-enabled victim test: X-5 still requires the full kernel
metadata census, dense-wide trigger, representative victims and clock/allocation
telemetry before a causal conclusion.

The [scratch-growth screen](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-scratch-growth/README.md)
then tests wave-norm GDN as a scratch-enabled victim after dense-wide. Four
fresh sham/scratch/scratch/sham processes give after/before ratios
0.99604/0.99747/0.99856/1.00402, with exact GDN output/state and a passing dense
CPU F16 reference. A separate trace confirms queue scratch grows from
1,638,400 to 3,932,160 bytes and the 36→92→36-byte dispatch order on one queue.
Boundary clock snapshots are stable. No large growth-induced penalty appears
in this synthetic screen; it does not test a pristine first-scratch transition
or representative decode victims, and X-5 remains open.

The [current code-object census](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-scratch-census/README.md)
reconciles all 70 traced project symbols against 23 current cached libraries:
only the above GDN/dense-wide symbols have nonzero private segments, and all
sizes match the traces. Two ROCm copy/fill symbols remain runtime-unresolved.
This establishes current-build metadata consistency, not historical loaded
binary identity (the captures did not hash loaded libraries). X-5 still needs
representative decode-victim and first-scratch causality evidence.

The [Q5_1 first-scratch screen](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-decode-scratch/README.md)
now verifies the real layer0 down/combine owner before and after the first GDN
scratch allocation. Four fresh sham/scratch/scratch/sham ratios are
0.97503/0.99101/0.99345/0.99916 with byte-identical outputs. The cached profiler
confirms baseline Q5_1 calls precede the first 1,638,400-byte allocation and the
36-byte GDN trigger; subsequent Q5_1 calls use the same queue. No large penalty
appears for this pair. The subsequent
[dense-wide first-trigger screen](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-decode-dense-scratch/README.md)
also shows no large persistent Q5_1 penalty: scratch ratios 0.99501/1.00294
versus sham 0.98817/0.98892, exact victim outputs and a passing dense CPU
reference. Its trace verifies the first 3,932,160-byte allocation follows
baseline Q5_1 calls and precedes the 92-byte dense-wide trigger, with no GDN
execution. Gate/up and Q8 decode victims were not covered by those packets;
no broad throttle-clearance or speedup claim follows.

The [Q4/Q8 first-scratch packet](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-06-qwen4exp-x5-decode-victims/README.md)
completes those named victim pairs using real layer4 UD-Q4_K_XL weights and
synthetic decode activations/routes. Sixteen serial fresh processes cover both
victims after the original36B GDN or92B F32 dense-wide trigger. Scratch-arm
ratios are0.97600–0.98272 (GDN/Q4),0.98965–1.02339 (GDN/Q8),
0.97557–0.97979 (dense/Q4) and0.98041–1.00065 (dense/Q8). All victim outputs
are finite/exact, GDN output/state is strict-parent exact, dense CPU F16 checks
pass and boundary clocks match. Four cached captures hash loaded libraries and
verify six zero-private victim calls, the first1,638,400/3,932,160B allocation,
one36/92B trigger and three post calls on queue1. No multi-fold penalty appears
for these pairs. The small Q8/GDN effect remains unresolved (scratch/sham
median-ratio1.02528); zero-private decode victims do not establish a universal
scratch-enabled throttle null. Initial local probe timing isINVALID due to
in-window synchronization and an omitted dense launch; only repaired, tested
schema2 results are used. This closes the named decode-coverage gap, not all
scratch causality or the promoted28B GDN trigger.

## 2. Arithmetic policy and kernel mechanisms

### 2.1 What the fast engines compute

Read from source at gufo `fee9d2a`
(`src/models/qwen38_flash_next/kernels/rocm/executor.cpp`, `kernels.hip.cpp`),
halo-box PR #91 / #87 / #104 descriptions and pwilkin `40a9f4d01`:

| Block | gufo (prefill) | halo-box / pwilkin | hipEngine production default |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Dense Q8_0 projections | `DenseF16GEMMKernel`: Q8_0 codes decoded to F16 in LDS (exact magic-number construction), **F16 activation rows**, F16 WMMA, F32 accumulate, at **every layer** for shapes with ≥2048 outputs and K ≤ 2560; the other Q8_0 shapes run `W8A8BlockedWmmaGEMMKernel` (int8 WMMA over activations quantized per 32-block) | `mmb.cu`: BF16 weights and BF16 activations, BF16 WMMA, all layers | F16 operands (`dense_wide256`) at layers 16-47 only; **exact F32 coltile chain at 0-15** |
| Routed experts | `RoutedF16GEMMKernel`: Q4_K / Q5_1 / Q8_0 decoded to F16 in LDS, F16 activations, F16 WMMA; gate/up paired with a SwiGLU epilogue; 48/64/128-row expert tiles picked from the real bucket counts; **no repair pass** | MMB routed GLU (BF16), Q4_K two-lane decode | iu8 WMMA with at-risk flags plus a **sparse exact repair** (1240 ms per `code-p4096`) |
| Hyper-connections | `HcDownF16Gemm` (int8 WMMA from a tiled-Q8 copy of the norm) plus `HcMixF16Gemm` (Q8 → F16 up projection with the mix and inject in the epilogue); combine fused with the MoE epilogue and the next norm | fused gate GEMM + mix, bf16 streams end to end, combine+norm computes the next inject | exact F32 `q8_0_gr_up_sigmoid_mean_coltile2_branch4_rowbatch4` (2507 ms, 256x weight re-read) |
| Router and small F32 weights | hipBLAS SGEMM (router and shared-expert gate in one GEMM) | activations rounded to F16 on WMMA ("every F32 weight of these GGUFs is F16-exact") | exact F32 tile4 producer |
| Attention | `WmmaCausalAttentionKernel`: **F16 K/V cache**, WMMA QK and PV, four queries x twelve heads packed over the union of their selections | HS256 WMMA flash attention; WMMA indexer scoring | BF16 KV; scalar exact FMA sparse attention (`__fmul_rn` with a contraction barrier) |
| GDN | F32 row-split recurrence, four tokens staged in LDS, three windows of loads in flight | chunked fp16 WMMA chunk scan (NMSE ~2e-7) with fused conv/SiLU, q/k norm and gated RMS norm | exact F32 register-state / tile-16 recurrence |
| Activation conversion | producers write the F16 (and tiled Q8) copy their consumer reads; no separate conversion pass on the wide route | producers write the BF16 copy themselves | one bounded staging cast per consumer set (`HIPENGINE_GGUF_Q8_DENSE_WIDE_F16_ACT`) |
| Prefill chunk | 2048 | `-ub 4096` in the published benchmarks | 1024 |

Their quality evidence is not our gate. gufo's is consistency (batched against
isolated, chunk boundaries, snapshot replay, 63 tg128 requests matching fresh
AR) plus an FP64 attention-operator check; its own scalar-versus-bulk prefill
comparison reports "same top-1; logit RMSE 0.18, not bit-identical". halo-box
uses PPL and KLD against its own reference build. Neither is measured against a
strict F32 teacher at our calibrated envelope.

### 2.2 What that means for us

1. **The historical arithmetic recovery substantially reduced throughput.** The
   September 14 recovery (`20260914T090826`, `49ffa3cb5`) zeroed fifteen
   unqualified arithmetic selectors after the old composition measured max KL
   `0.0546`. Same host, same residency, counterbalanced: PP
   **292.7 / 309.3 / 285.8 → 172.9 / 179.7 / 130.0** (−41 / −42 / −55%) at
   p512 / 1K / 4K and TG **20.2 / 19.4 / 18.1 → 17.6 / 17.0 / 11.4**. Q8 and QSA
   were then partly restored under qualified variants, and the September 16-28
   promotions have brought p4096 from 130.0 to ~255 tok/s — still below the
   285.8 of the unqualified composition.
2. **The reviewed competitors use reduced-precision operands for their large
   matrix paths.** Our F16-operand `dense_wide256` passes its local numerical
   gate at 16-47; the sibling WMMA route failed at 0-47 by 10% of the mean-KL
   budget (1.099e-3 against 1e-3). That is not a direct wide-route 0-47 verdict. The
   router can amplify small logit changes near expert-selection ties (§8 rule 6).
   This is a hypothesis to localize, not proof of a router
   defect or that every reduction change fails. Check `flip_eligible_rows`,
   routed expert choices and the first divergent boundary before assigning
   cause; the full production gate remains binding.
3. **So there are two tracks, and they must be ranked separately.**
   - **Track A, kernel technology at our arithmetic.** Arithmetic-preserving
     changes (LDS-staged weights, epilogue fusion, larger chunks, producer
     publication) are cheaper to admit and are ranked in §4-§5.
   - **Track B, arithmetic class.** Moving more of the model to the
     competitors' operand classes is the larger prize and the harder gate. It
     is informed by `X-1`: compare competitor outputs with our strict teacher
     while testing bounded local candidates. A competitor pass does not admit
     our implementation, and a competitor failure does not rule out a scoped
     candidate. `X-1` is an information priority, not a blanket build blocker.

A pass or failure describes that engine/configuration only; local candidates still need their own gate | **FIRST EXPERIMENT COMPLETE (`20260930T`) - and it refutes the derived bound it was written to check.** The ask was to score the already-captured identical-operand MMB packet against F64 using the replay bridge. **It was already done on 2026-09-16** in `benchmarks/results/2026-09-16-cross-engine-replay/` and the row was never updated; the reference there is a genuine F64 matmul (`x64 @ w64.T` over the packet's own dequantized weights and f32 activations), not a simulation. Result: the pinned comparator (`mmb_dense_kernel<128, 256, 64, 64, 1>`, halo-box PR #63 `c4aa30229`) sits **1.466e-02 max abs** and **7.87e-04 mean abs** from exact F64, against our production path's **2.06e-06** and **4.33e-08** - an **18,171x** mean-abs gap on byte-identical operands. The comparator tracks the both-operands-bf16 reference to 2.17e-06 mean abs, confirming the arithmetic class it declares. **Normalized by the exact reference's 13.595 max abs that is 1.078e-3, not the 6.8e-3-7.2e-3 that §2.6 and the external-forks review carried.** The withdrawn figure was a **triangle-inequality bound on elementwise maxima** (`max_abs_vs_bf16both` 0.09499 plus our F16 kernel's own 0.00283 from exact): valid as an upper bound, useless as an estimate, because the elementwise-maximum triangle inequality `max abs of a-c <= max abs of a-b + max abs of b-c` holds but the reverse does not, so the element attaining one maximum need not contribute to the other. It landed **6.3x** high. **This revises the campaign's headline by 6.4x:** the comparator's arithmetic is **5.2x** coarser than our F16-operand route, not "about 33x". §2.6 and the review's arithmetic table now carry the measured row with the derived one struck through, and the review's claim that a BF16 arm would cost a move "from 2.08e-4 to roughly 7e-3" is corrected to a **measured 1.078e-3** - a 5.2x move, not 33x. **The whole-model half remains open**: aligning exact token IDs, vocabulary, KV policy and teacher-forced contexts for the canonical 12 cases plus heldouts, and reporting mean/p95/p99/max KL and category top-1, is not done and no competitor has a same-teacher full-model certificate. Next: `linear:attn_qkv` 4.658 (8.59%), **`linear:root.lm_head` 2.980 (5.50%) - the second largest SINGLE role is one output projection**, `linear:attn_gate` 2.890, `linear:ssm_out` 2.842, `qsa_decode:attn_q` 2.621, `gdn:attn_qkv` 2.401, `linear:attn_q` 1.815, `linear:hc_ffn_down` 1.771, `linear:hc_attn_down` 1.768. **DECODE IS NOT KERNEL-BOUND, which is the number the `DE-5`/`DE-6` runtime rows were missing**: the unprofiled wall for the same transition is median **153.0 ms** (mean 162.7, spread **1.30x**, `token_exact` and `state_exact` both true), so against 54.2 ms of kernel time that is **~35% kernel and ~65% host/launch overhead**, with 48 graph launches carrying 1,771 kernels. **The runtime side is larger than the device side for a decode step.** **REUSABLE METHOD FINDING - IN A GRAPH-REPLAYING ENGINE, ROLE ATTRIBUTION MUST NOT RELY ON THE CORRELATION-ID JOIN ALONE.** The join reached 3,516 of 5,313 kernels and missed 1,797, and **0 of the 1,797 unattributed correlation ids appear in the HIP launch trace at all** because this engine replays them from captured HIP graphs (192 `hipGraphLaunch`); a graph-replayed kernel has no launch API row to join. The fix is **not a heuristic**: `rocprofv3 --kernel-rename` renames each kernel with its enclosing roctx region, so those kernels arrive already named `qwen4exp_role:moe:layers.N.expert_gate`, and reading the role back out recovers an identity **the profiler assigned** - taking attribution to **5,313 of 5,313**. **Still OPEN**: the **stable wall** and serving-wrapper attribution remain open under X-10/DE-5 - this capture's unprofiled spread is 1.30x and the profiled arm's is **4.12x** (58.9-242.7 ms), so the ~35% kernel share is approximate and the profiled wall is not usable as a performance number. **Limits**: profiled capture, per-kernel times diagnostic; one case (`code-p4096`), one live count (4097), one model, one host, one profile, 3 repetitions; the wall is the measured transition only (snapshot restore happens before each); attribution is only as good as the roctx coverage, though there are zero unattributed kernels here. **No performance claim, no promotion, no default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-x4-decode-split/README.md`, `scripts/qwen4exp_x4_decode_role_attribution.py` |

Detail: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-prefill-scope-discrepancy/README.md` **CONFIRMED on an isolated case: the `wmma` gate does not measure a prefill route.** A single canonical case (`code-p512`) at one decode step, so neither row is averaged: the post-prefill row reports **5.8014e-12** and the single decode row **1.0184e-04**, with candidate and teacher hashes differing. The prediction was made before the run - if the arms differ in decode-path selectors rather than in the prefill route, the prefill row must be flat and the decode row must not be - and it holds. So the quantity that moves is reached through `use_wmma` (`f_rowtile`, the rows-2-8 batch-decode session, `use_rowtile`), not through `wmma_prefill_f32_f32_out` as the recorded `candidate_chain` and surface claim. **The 2026-09-16 certification and its bit-identical HEAD re-run therefore measure a decode-path side effect, not the WMMA prefill route.** Open and unexplained: the heldout run shows the opposite pattern (prefill 2.06179e-03 vs decode 1.99214e-04), which no decode-path effect can produce, so something engages on the heldout prefill that does not on the canonical one. Detail: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-prefill-scope-discrepancy/README.md` **Engagement now MEASURED at the dispatch, and it falsifies my dispatch-order explanation while sharpening the finding.** A recorder on `_wmma_prefill_dispatch` records the incoming variant, the outgoing variant and the decline reason. Canonical `code-p512`: candidate **264 engaged launches**, variant `wmma_prefill_f32_f32_out`, rows 512; heldout `code_merge_intervals`: **328 engaged**, rows 40; teacher `engaged = 0` in both with `use_wmma_false` throughout. So the route **does** engage in both protocols - my earlier prediction that the canonical prefill would never engage is **falsified** - and the post-prefill row is **still bit-identical (5.8014e-12)** while the decode row moves (1.0184e-04). `wmma_prefill_f32_f32_out` therefore produces arithmetically equivalent results to the exact chain at the prefill position: **the route runs and contributes no measurable numerical difference**, so the drift the `wmma` gate reports is not its own - it belongs to the other `use_wmma`-gated selectors (`f_rowtile`, `enabled=not use_wmma`, `use_rowtile`, the rows-2-8 batch-decode session). **A flaw in my own check corrected before it misled anyone:** the summariser reported `arms_isolated`, which was `True` here while the arms are demonstrably not isolated; renamed `wmma_route_engaged_candidate_only` with an explicit `covers_other_use_wmma_selectors: False` and a verdict that says so in words. The 0.775128 means the candidate leaves the rowtile **inactive for 792 launches** where the teacher had it active - decode-path rows the WMMA route refuses - and since the canonical prefill row is bit-identical (5.8014e-12) while the decode row moves (1.0184e-04), **the gate's drift is the rowtile's, reached through a flag named for the route**. KL unchanged by this analysis, so the fix moved attribution not measurement. **Two bugs in my own summariser, found by reading the artifact rather than the verdict:** `teacher_route_engaged` was derived from total engagement across every selector, making it unconditionally true because `q4k_rowtile` is always active in the teacher arm (verdict read "teacher also engaged" while that arm's route count was exactly zero); and the differs-comparison collapsed each arm to a boolean, discarding the very difference it was hunting. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-wmma-engagement/README.md` **THE HELDOUT BLOCKER IS HELD OPEN FOR THE RIGHT REASON, AND THE MISSING INPUT IS NAMED (`20260930T`).** The suite cannot engage the route because its longest prompt is 71 tokens against `dense_wide`'s 257-row floor. I checked that this is a genuine row floor and not a proxy artefact before recording it: the floor is read from the dispatch guard `_q8_dense_wide_dispatch`'s `rows <= 256`, and `rows` at that guard is `len(tokens)` (`laguna_gguf_runner.py:5059`, `_execute_rows`), the real prefill token count - **not** the scratch row count, which `allocate_scratch` sizes to `min(chunk_size, max(768, prompt_tokens+1))` and which is what makes chunk 1024 and 4096 identical at p512. KL is distributional; the deltas land where the softmax is flat on the canonical prompts and near the argmax on the long-form prompts. **Both prior readings are therefore wrong:** the canonical row's flatness is not evidence the route contributes nothing to the prefill (its logits move by 0.4), and the long-form failure is not a transition defect - there is no transition mechanism in the row. **Nondeterminism is excluded, not assumed:** every same-arm repeat in every arm and subject is bit-identical, and the long-form artifact's own `repeat_determinism` passes 3/3. **Why the scope exceeds 1e-3 is the gate working:** the scope is 4 rows with no averaging to absorb one sensitive prompt, against 1536 rows for the same limit in the canonical protocol, which is why the long-form protocol exists. **Not decided here:** whether a route with a ~0.4 per-logit delta can meet a 1e-3 mean-KL limit on a 4-row scope at every prompt - that is a scope-and-threshold question for the qualification packet, and this row stays open on it. ### 3.1 Common decision gates

The task collector now records input-token counts and candidate host-call
observations per prompt, repeat and phase (`prefill`, `first_decode`,
`later_decode`). Arm-wide calls no longer hide which cases were inactive.
Observations are separate from output determinism comparisons; uncounted
candidates report unknown rather than false engagement. These are Python-call
counts, not GPU execution certificates (graph replay may bypass them), and
lack role/layer attribution. Long natural tasks and route-resolved GPU evidence
are still required by X-11; this instrumentation alone qualifies no candidate.
Supplemental natural-task JSONL files can be added with `--extra-prompts`;
canonical suites are always loaded, and `canonical_suite_complete` is separate
from completeness of the combined selection. Reports hash all suite files.
`--prompt-token-budget` reserves input capacity in addition to the output cap
and rejects oversized chat-formatted inputs before prefill. The default remains
1024 input tokens with chunk size 1024; a larger budget is not evidence that a
candidate ran or that a new shape passed its numerical gate.
The supplemental [natural long tasks](../benchmarks/prompts/qwen4exp-natural-long-tasks-v1.md)
cover all four categories with predeclared review criteria. Their measured
chat-formatted lengths are 438/508/424/512 tokens on the target tokenizer;
lengths alone establish neither active dispatch nor task quality.
The [dense-Q8 supplemental screen](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-natural-long-dense-q8/README.md)
observes 398 candidate host calls per prefill on every task/repeat, with
repeatable outputs and zero tracked live allocations after close. Task
qualification does not pass: both arms truncate code at 2048 tokens and give
contradictory backup-retention conclusions. Shared failures do not isolate
candidate arithmetic regression; GPU role/layer attribution remains open.
A [code-only higher-cap run](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-natural-long-code-eos/README.md)
reaches EOS at 2424 tokens with identical arm/repeat outputs and unchanged
2048-token prefixes. The reviewed implementation passes 11 generated tests
and six independent sandboxed cases. This resolves truncation, not the
language-task failures or full qualification; output fences and weak generated
revision-test coverage are also documented.

Implementation classes are T0 (reference-preserving), T1 (local arithmetic
drift), T2 (association/layout drift), and T3 (representation or algorithm
change), as defined in EXECUTION-PROFILES. Track labels describe the proposed
approach, not a qualification verdict.

Rows marked A aim to preserve arithmetic; prove that claim on real shapes and
state boundaries. A non-bit-identical result may instead be declared T1/T2 and
reviewed under the unchanged production envelope. It is not automatically a
rejection. Every production candidate needs a registered strict fallback,
strict-teacher category/heldout mean and tail KL/top-1, deterministic repeats,
exact control/ownership, same-width isolation, applicable BF16-relative and task
gates, plus complete-owner and complete-request timing. State values may drift;
positions, masks, destinations, accepted-prefix accounting and lifecycle may not.

Memory/runtime experiments additionally need admission and cancellation/reuse
coverage. Stale performance hints may not select nondeterministic arithmetic or
supply authoritative route/position metadata; scratch must cover worst-case
populations. Representation changes (weight/KV formats or selection/acceptance
policy) are explicit separate configurations, not silent T1/T2 promotions. Do
not change the numerical envelope to match a headline. `X-7` closes missing
baseline evidence; the separate recalibration proposal does not amend policy.

## 4. Prefill open work, by functional block

Rank measured candidates inside each block by recoverable complete-request
milliseconds; newly added unmeasured rows are not a numerical ranking. Across
blocks the order is: **B routed experts, A dense, D hyper-connections,
H chunk size, E attention, F GDN, C router/reduce**, pending `X-3`.

### 4A. Dense projections — 5234.8 ms at the anchor (31.3%)

Wide route 1404.2 ms at layers **13-47** as of 2026-10-04 UTC (was 16-47 when
this anchor was taken); exact coltile chain on the remaining low layers; 565 ms
of other dense roles. `dense_wide256` runs the packet shape (rows 1024,
K 2560, N 10240) in **1.3207 ms, 40.65 TFLOP/s** on the f16-activation-hoisted
variant that production actually launches (1156 launches at `code-p4096`).
**This confirms, and does not newly establish, the figure `DP-7` recorded on
2026-09-28** (attn_qkv F16-input at **40.54**/41.32/41.66 TFLOP/s for
1024/2048/4096 rows). For comparison, gufo reports **30-34 TFLOP/s** for its F16
dense kernels at 2048 rows and rejected hipBLASLt at **19-26**; `DP-7` already
noted the 30-TFLOP/s threshold is exceeded and that tile work should not be
justified from the old gap.

**The 2.573 ms / 20.9 TFLOP/s figure this section carried until 2026-10-04 UTC
describes the f32-activation variant that production does not run**, which is why
it sat below `DP-7`'s measurement for six days rather than contradicting it: a
tile-family sweep (`scripts/qwen4exp_dense_wide_tile_sweep.py`) reproduces
2.5518 ms exactly for that variant. **What the sweep adds is the family
comparison and the price, not the headline number.** It settles the tile question
the kernel's comment left open - **128x256 beats 128x128 beats 64x256 beats
64x128**, so the widest `BN` wins and the narrower layouts lose - and it prices
the activation hoist at **-48.25% on the GEMM with bit-identical output**
(`max abs diff` exactly 0), worth **+4.09% prefill** end to end at `code-p4096`
against a within-arm spread of 0.04-0.13%, a signal-to-drift ratio near 40x.

**The scope lever is spent at 13-47.** Extending to 0-47 is worth +18.876% prefill
but fails the production gate, and the residual drift is verified accumulation-order
error rather than a precision defect (§1.2.1), so no accuracy fix recovers the
remaining layers. **Since the wide route already outruns the reference on
throughput, the dense block's recoverable milliseconds are gated by the numerical
envelope rather than by kernel efficiency** - the open question is whether any of
layers 0-12 can be converted at all under the gate, not how fast the converted
layers run. `DP-2` (7.478 ms) and `DP-6` (an 85.791 ms ceiling, of which the
KV-store half is 0.010% and should be dropped) are the only rows left in this
block and both are small.

Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-dp5-consumer-census/README.md` **CAST BANDWIDTH MEASURED, AND THE WHOLE-ROW ESTIMATE IS REFUTED (`20260930T`).** The 4/6 model assumed both kernels run at the same effective rate; measured against the raw trace filtered to the `qwen4exp_prefill_p4096_` window (elements = `Grid_Size_X*Y*Z` as **work-items** per §8 rule 2, reproducing the artifact's 1,056 launches / 131.001 ms exactly), **it does not hold uniformly - apparent bandwidth RISES as the transfer shrinks**: **192.1 GB/s at 10,485,760 elements/launch (the `hc_*_down` pair, 256 launches, 16.106 GB, 83.861 ms)**, 241.1 at 6,291,456, 325.9 at 2,621,440, and **595.4 at 655,360**. The total, **229.0 GB/s, is 103.6% of the 221 GB/s DRAM roof** - impossible for pure DRAM traffic - so the small roles are partly **L2-served** and the 6 B/element model is not a DRAM model for them. **Therefore: the defensible target is the HC down pair alone, 16.106 GB at 192.1 GB/s = 86.9% of roof, net 4/6 x 83.861 = 55.9 ms = 0.36% of the union; and the whole-row 87.3 ms figure is REFUTED as an over-estimate** because it applies a DRAM byte model to roles whose traffic is partly cache-served and would not be saved. **This strengthens the epilogue-first order for a second independent reason**: the HC pair is not only 64.0% of the cast by time, it is the only part whose traffic is actually DRAM-bound. **Method warning, recorded because it bit this analysis**: the first attempt joined `captures.child.launch_census` shapes to `audit.prefill` times across two different captures and produced plausible per-role numbers; the 103.6%-of-roof total is what caught it. Cross-capture shape/time joins must be checked against a physical bound before use. **Not yet built.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-dp5-consumer-census/README.md` **THE BUILD IS RE-SCOPED: IT IS A RUNTIME BUFFER-OWNERSHIP CHANGE, NOT AN EPILOGUE EDIT (`20260930T`).** The row reads as a bounded kernel change - "move the F16 write into the HC mix epilogue first" - so the code was checked before starting it, and **the change cannot be made as described**. The cast is `_launch_dense_wide_f16_staged` (`hipengine/runtime/gguf_linear.py:644`), which **unconditionally** stages its input: `_wide_f16_activation_stage_ptr(count)` (line 634) returns a single `workspace.ptr` when `nbytes >= count*2`, the code casts `x_ptr -> stage_ptr`, then launches the F16 sibling. **There is no "input already F16" check on that path, and none is possible with the current ownership**: `wide_f16_activation_session` (line 568) binds **exactly one** `WideF16ActivationWorkspace(ptr, nbytes)` as a ContextVar - **one bounded scratch buffer for the whole route, shared by every consumer and overwritten on every launch**. So there is **no place for a producer to publish a persistent F16 copy and no protocol for a consumer to accept one**; DP-5 needs a new per-activation F16 buffer plus a publish/accept contract between the producing and consuming ops. **That is a runtime ownership change, not a kernel epilogue edit**, and it must be weighed against the measured prize: **55.9 ms = 0.36% of prefill** (the HC down pair only, at 86.9% of the DRAM roof). **What stands and is worth keeping**: the consumer census (HC down pair = 64.0% of the cast), the measured 192.1 GB/s, the scope (layers 16-47 only), and that the HC pair is the only DRAM-bound part - so if the contract is ever built, that is where to apply it. **Not built; no kernel changed; no default changed.** Evidence: `hipengine/runtime/gguf_linear.py:568,634,644`; `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-dp5-consumer-census/README.md`. **DIAGNOSTIC PUBLISHER SCREEN PASSES AT4096 (`20261006T`), RUNTIME CONTRACT NOT YET IMPLEMENTED.** The actual current HC-down input is grouped norm's `scratch.normalized`, not GR-mix output. So the warning in this cell was right and the row is now **rankable: 171.583 ms (1.090%)**, below `DP-5`'s 131.001 ms. **Incidental finding for the GDN rows:** in this capture the SSM causal conv is **116.121 ms of the 844.295 ms GDN bucket (13.8%)**, so the GDN family is not the recurrence alone - a different capture and shape from GD-3's 194.487 ms of 3,914.468 ms, so the two are not comparable and neither is restated here. **THE MECHANISM CELL'S gufo NAMES DO NOT MAP TO THIS TREE, AND THE REAL FUSION TARGET IS NOW NAMED (`20260930T`).** The row's mechanism column says the conv fuses into the **`ssm_in` GEMM** (`DenseF16SsmGemm`). **There is no `ssm_in` GEMM in this tree.** The conv's input is the **GDN layer's own `attn_qkv` projection**: `run_qwen4_exp_gdn_token_mixer` calls `launch_gguf_linear(weights["attn_qkv"], mixed_ptr, scratch.qkv.ptr, rows, hidden, qkv_width, ...)` with `qkv_width = 2*num_k_heads*head_dim + num_v_heads*head_dim = 2*16*128 + 48*128 = 10240`, and the conv then reads `scratch.qkv` and writes `scratch.conv` (`hipengine/runtime/qwen4_exp_runner.py`, the `required = {"attn_qkv", "attn_gate", "ssm_alpha", "ssm_beta", "ssm_out"}` block). So the fusion target is **the GDN `attn_qkv` wide GEMM's epilogue**, which at the measured operating point is `hipengine_gguf_q8_0_dense_wide256_f16in_f32_f32_out` - and that kernel is shared, so a fused variant must be registered alongside it rather than edited in place. **The two halves are therefore still two builds, and on different layer types:** the conv is a GDN-layer epilogue, while the q/k-norm-plus-RoPE and KV-store kernels are the `qsa_*` **attention**-layer path (`qsa_split_norm_rope_rows_f32`, `qsa_norm_rope_rows_f32`, `qsa_pool_norm_rope_f32`), so the row's "two independent builds" framing survives even though its symbol names did not. **The structural requirement a builder must plan for:** the conv is a fixed-K4 causal depthwise conv over the **token** axis - `out[t][c] = silu(w0*in[t-3][c] + w1*in[t-2][c] + w2*in[t-1][c] + w3*in[t][c])`, with `conv_state` supplying rows `t < 3` - so an `attn_qkv` epilogue must span at least 4 consecutive token rows and retain 3 previous rows of the conv-input channels inside the tile. `hipengine/kernels/hip_gfx1100/linear_attn/conv.hip:265` is the reference for the arithmetic and the state boundary, and `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_EXACT_CONV_PREFILL` selects between this exact bulk route and a per-row decode loop. The fallback `compact_rowbatch8` owner stages the same three fields the same cooperative way. In both, the 4-bit nibbles are still read per-lane from a row already resident in cache. The screened kernel is gather-shaped by construction: `warp_col * 32 + lane` gives each lane its own output column, and each lane reads its own `row_bytes` weight row. So the verdict above stands for the family it measured, but it does NOT show that LDS staging loses on the production owner, which already has the cooperative staging and the cross-row reuse. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-mx1-q5k-gate-up/README.md` **THE COMPLETE-OPERATION BOUNDARY IS NOW PRICED, FROM THE X-3 AUTHORITY RATHER THAN FROM THE 1239.8 ms HEADLINE (`20260930T`).** The row's action asks to "include packing, repair removal, map and epilogue costs". Priced from `role-analysis.json` in the 2026-09-28 X-3 packet (`2789c19d6`, the same capture as the 15,741.963 ms prefill kernel union; 100% attributed, 2,288 kernel rows), not from the 2026-09-17 repair packet, whose 1,239.8 ms belongs to a 16,733.4 ms prefill. **In the current capture the repair is 1,219.978 ms = 7.75% of the union** across four kernels and 96 launches: `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_sparse_exact_repair_bf16_kernel` 535.932 (47 launches), `q5_1_selected_sparse_exact_repair_row_publish_kernel` 522.992 (43), `q8_0_selected_sparse_repair_kernel` 101.065 (5), `gguf_q5_k_selected_dual_sparse_exact_repair_bf16_kernel` 59.989 (1). **The iu8-risk expert owners MX-1 would replace total 4,280.901 ms**: gate/up 2,779.713 (`gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_wmma_iu8_risk_prefill_kernel` 2,713.403 over 47 launches + the Q5_K layer 2 at 66.311) and down 1,501.188 (`q5_1_selected_wmma_iu8_risk_prefill_kernel` 1,501.188 over 43). **Packing, map and epilogue CANCEL rather than add - 821.872 ms of router (618.597 + 18.266), grouping/scatter/tile-map (54.488 + 0.372 + 0.319 + 1.440) and epilogue (31.321 + 13.167 + 69.952 + 10.211), because MX-1 changes operand type, not routing**; the row asked for them to be included and the correct treatment is that they are unchanged. **So the net prize is `1,219.978 - delta_GEMM`, where `delta_GEMM = F16 gate/up+down - 4,280.901`, and the build's acceptance target is that the F16 gate/up+down comes in at or below 4,280.901 ms.** **The 1,219.978 ms is a CEILING, NOT A NET PRIZE, and the structural risk is quantified rather than assumed:** the iu8 route stages 8-bit operands in LDS and MX-1 stages 16-bit F16, so it **doubles the LDS weight footprint per element at the same MMA shape**, which forces a smaller tile or a halved K-slice - itself a cost. There is no headroom to borrow from a better GEMM: `ED-4` measured the iu8 owner as the **fastest of three registered owners** (median -50.4% main-kernel against the runner-up, same host, 12/12 cases), so MX-1 cannot win on the GEMM and must reach **parity while doubling the LDS footprint**, taking its whole value from the repair. **`EG-3` names MX-1 as the SOLE path to this cost** ("None queued. Either `MX-1` removes it or a measured mechanism ... reopens it"), so this is the only remaining lever on the routed-expert route. **Ranking: 7.75% beats `GD-4`'s ~5.5% ceiling and DP-8's measured 0.6%, making MX-1 the largest remaining single lever and the only one whose operand-admissibility budget is already complete** (all 144 tensors, gate/up coverage gap closed). **Efficiency framing, which is what the build actually buys:** the repair spends **28.5% of the full GEMM's time to correct 0.712% of elements**, and is **22.2%** of the routed-expert total (5,500.879 ms = 4,280.901 GEMM + 1,219.978 repair). **NOT a promotion and NOT a speed claim**: no kernel, default, flag or dispatch changed, and the operand-error budget still does not replace the production mean/p95/p99/max KL, top-1, determinism, isolation or task gate. One capture, one case (`code-p4096`), one host, one model, one quant, one profile. **The pricing does not decide whether to build** - it decides what the build must beat, and it is recorded so the target is known in advance rather than discovered after. **RECONNAISSANCE CORRECTS THE BUILD'S SCOPE AND NAMES A CHEAPER PATH TO THE SAME PRIZE (`20260930T`).** The row reads as a from-scratch kernel port. Reading the tree says otherwise on both halves, and the correction is recorded **before** the build so the order is chosen rather than inherited. **(1) The F16/BF16-WMMA routed-expert arithmetic already exists here.** `gguf_q4_k_t16_selected_expert_major_wmma_comp_bf16_bf16_out` is **registered for `moe_linear`** under `gguf_q4_k_t16_v1` and `gguf_q6_k_t16_v1` (`hipengine/kernels/hip_gfx1100/quant/gguf_k_t16_selected_prefill.py`, the `moe_linear` registration block): an expert-major route that decodes the **bit-lossless T16 repack** to F16 in registers, runs `__builtin_amdgcn_wmma_f32_16x16x16_f16_w32`, accumulates in F32 with a `compensated_wmma_accumulate` correction, and has **no int8 plane, no ds-trick and no repair by construction**. The plain grouped fallback `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_wmma_prefill_compact_bf16_bf16_out` is likewise a BF16-operand WMMA route already on the path. So the WMMA, the quant-to-F16 decode and the compensated accumulate are **existing machinery, not this row's build**. **(2) The repair is a BIT-PARITY mechanism, by its own documentation, and the production profile pays for it anyway.** The risk kernel's docstring: "Identical published arithmetic to the plain iu8 kernel; outputs whose F32 value lies within `risk_multiplier * (abs(kahan_err) + FLT_EPSILON * sum(abs(term)))` of a **BF16 rounding boundary** are queued by linear output index for the sparse exact repair pass." The risk multiplier's docstring is explicit about why: "Under the production-correctness contract a rare escape is bounded drift, **not a contract violation**; 16.0 is chosen so the route stays bit-identical to the strict parent in practice, **keeping every existing gate green**." `EG-3` records that the repair "reproduces the pair2 parent". **So the tree's structure is: pair2 = exact and slow; iu8+repair = 1.50-1.82x faster AND bit-identical to pair2. The repair is the price of bit-identity, not of accuracy.** `AGENTS.md` states the binding contract is production correctness and that strict is "a debugging oracle, not the promotion bar", yet `qwen4_exp_profiles.py` sets all three of `Q4_IU8_EXACT` / `Q51_IU8_EXACT` / `Q5_K_IU8_EXACT` to `"1" if production else "0"`. **This row's premise - that removing the repair requires changing the arithmetic - is therefore not established, and the cheap experiment comes first.** **(3) The gate/up leg's repair-free configuration is reachable TODAY.** The dispatch checks `exact_grouped_q4_gate` **before** the `q4_iu8` branch, so `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_EXACT_GROUPED_Q4=0` with `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q4_IU8_PREFILL=1` selects `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_wmma_iu8_prefill_bf16_bf16_out` - the **plain iu8 kernel, which takes no risk buffers and never repairs** - and by the docstring quoted above it publishes *identical arithmetic* to the risk kernel. That is this row's gate/up half (573.211 ms of repair) as a **configuration, needing only the production gate, with no kernel written**. **(4) The down leg is the real gap, and it is smaller than this row assumed.** There is **no plain (non-risk) iu8 Q5_1 down prefill** - `qwen4_exp_q5_1.hip` offers only `q5_1_selected_wmma_iu8_risk_prefill_kernel` - and its non-repair alternative inside `exact_grouped_down` is the bit-exact logical256 owner, which is the slower route iu8 was promoted over. So dropping the down leg's 661.210 ms needs a **plain sibling of that same kernel** (the risk body minus the risk criterion and queue), not a new arithmetic. **(5) What a full F16 port would still need, exactly:** a **`gguf_q5_1_t16_v1` layout and kernel, which does not exist anywhere in the tree** (T16 layouts exist only for q4_k, q5_k, q6_k, q8_0 - and the expert-major kernel `static_assert`s `qtype == 4 or qtype == 6`, so q8_0 and q5_k are uncovered on `moe_linear`), plus a dual-plus-SiLU moe pairing. **Consequence for the campaign: the cheapest route to this row's 7.75% is a gate run on the existing plain-iu8 configuration for gate/up plus a small plain-iu8 Q5_1 sibling for down - neither of which changes the arithmetic, needs the Q5_1 T16 layout, or touches the F16 decode.** The F16 port is the fallback if that gate fails. **No kernel written, no default changed, no measurement taken; this is a reading of existing code and documentation.** **THE CHEAP CONFIGURATION IS MEASURED CLOSED, AND THIS ROW'S PREMISE IS VINDICATED (`20260930T`).** The reconnaissance append above named a cheaper route to this row's prize - drop the repair by selecting the plain iu8 owner, which publishes identical arithmetic without the risk criterion - and said to gate it before building anything. **Gated, and it FAILS.** Candidate `repair_free_gate_up` in `scripts/qwen4exp_layer2_profile_gate.py` (`EXACT_GROUPED_Q4=0` + `Q4_IU8_PREFILL=1` + `Q4_IU8_LAYERS=all`, Q5_1 down left at production so the gate/up half is isolated) ran through the canonical 12-case layer2 gate: **450 rows, 360 candidate dispatches at the real gate/up shapes** (`[390..500, 2560, 640, 640]`), `measurement_valid: true`, **`hard_gates_passed: false`**, `decision.next: "reject candidate and keep strict fallback"`. **Every aggregate gate PASSES** - mean KL 5.278e-4 (limit 1e-3), p95 2.806e-3 (5e-3), p99 5.267e-3 (2e-2), max 2.469e-2 (5e-2), top-1 0.99556 (0.99) - **and the failure is per-scope, concentrated exactly where the repair operated:** the 18 `prefill_last` rows (`transition: prefill_to_c1`) run at **mean 2.159e-3, p95 7.776e-3, p99 2.131e-2**, all three over their limits (1e-3 / 5e-3 / 2e-2), while the 432 `c1`/`steady` rows sit at 4.598e-4 / 2.765e-3 / 4.358e-3 and pass comfortably. One row exceeds the 0.02 review boundary (kl 0.0247, top-1 equal, top-k overlap 1.0) and two rows flip top-1 (both at tiny strict margins, 0.0284 and rank-2, top-k overlap 1.0), giving `eligible_for_automatic_admission: false` and `task_gate: requires_review` (15/18 exact). **What this establishes:** the repair is not decorative. Its own docstring's claim that "a rare escape is bounded drift, not a contract violation" is true **per element** at a 0.71% incidence, but across 48 layers the accumulated drift lands on the prefill path and exceeds the calibrated per-scope envelope. **So the premise this row was built on is correct after all: removing the repair requires changing the arithmetic** - dropping it while keeping iu8 does not pass. **This also rules out the planned follow-on:** the plain-iu8 Q5_1 down sibling (661.210 ms) was to be built next on the assumption that the gate/up half would pass; the same prefill-path drift would be expected there, and it is not to be built on this evidence. **The F16 port is the route, and it now has a measured justification rather than an inferred one.** **A determinism and provenance note:** the run was repeated after fixing the dispatch counter, and the two runs' quality summaries are **identical to every printed digit** - which is both the run's determinism evidence and confirmation that the first run was in fact this candidate (the counter, not the route, was broken; see the counter worklog entry). Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-repair-free-gate-up/gate.json`. One model, one quant, one host (gfx1151), one profile, gate/up only (Q5_1 down unchanged at production). **Not promoted; no default changed.** **THE ROW'S "BUILD A REAL-INPUT ROUTED PACKET WITH F16 OPERANDS" ACTION IS ALREADY SATISFIED BY SHIPPING CODE (`20260930T`).** The action column prescribes building a from-scratch F16 routed packet, and the pricing above priced that build with a structural risk (16-bit operands double the LDS weight footprint against the iu8 route's 8-bit staging, forcing a smaller tile or a halved K-slice). **No kernel needs to be written.** `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_wmma_prefill_compact_kernel` in `hipengine/kernels/hip_gfx1100/quant/gguf_q4_k_selected_prefill.hip` **is** this row's route: its inner loop decodes the raw `block_q4_K` bytes to `half16_t` (`b_reg[kk] = static_cast<half_t>(scale_f * q - min_f)`), calls **`__builtin_amdgcn_wmma_f32_16x16x16_f16_w32`** with F32 accumulate, pairs gate/up (`use_b` switches to `qweight_b`/`out_features_b`), runs over the compact selected-MoE ABI, and loads activations already half (`load_compact_row_half16`). It is registered as `selected_dual_wmma_prefill_compact_bf16_bf16_out` (with `_hot_fulltile_` and `_sidemeta_` siblings) for `(hip_gfx1151, moe_linear, gguf_q4_k)`. **The iu8 owners are the optimization layered on top of it, not the other way round** - which is why that file carries both `wmma_f32_16x16x16_f16_w32` (x8, compact family) and `wmma_i32_16x16x16_iu8_w32` (x2, iu8 pair). **Reachable by clearing one flag:** the grouped-MoE dispatch chain is `if q4_k_mmq_prefill / elif exact_grouped_q4_gate -> iu8-risk + repair (production) / elif exact_grouped_down / elif expert_gate == gguf_q4_k -> if q4_iu8: plain iu8, else: F16 compact`, so **`HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_EXACT_GROUPED_Q4=0` alone** reaches the F16 owner (`Q4_IU8_PREFILL` already defaults to 0, and the down leg selects its route at a separate site, so leaving `EXACT_GROUPED_DOWN` at production isolates the gate/up half). **Engagement verified before any gate run** - the `DP-8` discipline, and a defect this session had already hit once: a probe applying the env post-construction exactly as the gate does measured **4 F16 compact calls at the gate/up shape `[50, 2560, 640, 640, 512, 464]` with 0 repair calls**, and swapping in `Q4_IU8_PREFILL=1` moved those same 4 calls to the plain iu8 owner. **Consequence for the row:** the build risk is removed rather than mitigated - the LDS-footprint question is answered by a kernel that already ships in this operand class - and the admissibility work already completed here (F16 decode at the F16 limit 4.88e-4; output error **8.0-9.5x below the BF16 floor already in the path** on a real natural-input capture; zero overflow; Q5_K gate/up coverage closed at ratio 0.159) now has an implementation to test rather than one to write. **The prize is unchanged and still needs performance:** the F16 owner must beat the iu8-risk owners plus the repair it removes (gate/up 2,779.713 ms + 573.211 ms), so a numerics pass is necessary and not sufficient. The X-3 profiler chain (`rocprofv3 --kernel-trace --marker-trace --hip-trace` around `scripts/qwen4exp_profile_gap.py`, audited by `scripts/qwen4exp_trace_audit.py`, joined by `scripts/qwen4exp_role_gap_table.py`) is reusable as-is to compare the two owners in one same-host capture. **Running: the layer2 numerics gate for `f16_compact_gate_up`; not yet known.** **THE DOWN HALF EXISTS TOO, SO THE ROW'S FULL PRIZE IS REACHABLE BY TWO FLAGS (`20260930T`).** The pricing above splits the repair into a gate/up half (573.211 ms) and a down half (522.992 ms of `q5_1_selected_sparse_exact_repair_row_publish_kernel` plus the 1,501.188 ms exact-row_publish chain it repairs). The same reconnaissance applied to the down leg: `hipengine/kernels/hip_gfx1100/quant/qwen4_exp_q5_1.hip` contains **`qwen4_exp_q5_1_selected_grouped_wmma_prefill_compact_bf16_bf16_out`**, and its single WMMA call is **`__builtin_amdgcn_wmma_f32_16x16x16_f16_w32`** (the file's only `iu8` WMMA is in the *gemv*, not the prefill) - F16 WMMA with F32 accumulate over the same compact selected-MoE ABI. It is selected at the down dispatch site by `elif q5_wmma:` where **`q5_wmma = production_grouped_moe or HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q5_1_WMMA != 0`**, so **`HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q5_1_WMMA=1`** reaches it, bypassing the `resolve()`d row_publish chain that carries the repair. **So `MX-1`'s full route is `EXACT_GROUPED_Q4=0` + `Q5_1_WMMA=1`: two flags, both landing on existing F16 WMMA selected-MoE prefill kernels, with no repair pass on either half.** Both the build risk and the "which operand class" question are therefore settled by shipping code, and the remaining work is the numerics gate plus timing on both halves. **Not yet verified: the down leg's engagement with `Q5_1_WMMA=1`** - the gate/up engagement was measured, and the down leg's must be measured the same way before its numbers are trusted (a route the harness cannot see is not measured). **Not promoted; no default changed.** **MX-1 IS REFUTED - BOTH THE FULL ROUTE AND ITS GATE/UP HALF FAIL, AND THE FULL ROUTE FAILS WORSE (`20260930T`).** Two runs of the canonical 12-case layer2 protocol, 3 repeats, same host and profile. **(a) Gate/up only** (`EXACT_GROUPED_Q4=0`; Q5_1 down left at production to isolate the half): `hard_gates_passed: false`, **every aggregate gate passes and better than any alternative** - mean KL 4.5837e-4, p95 2.5307e-3, p99 5.1165e-3, max 6.3267e-3 (**3.9x better than the plain-iu8 route**), top-1 0.99778 = 449/450, **zero** rows over the 0.02 review boundary - and the failure is per-scope on the 18 `prefill_last` rows by a whisker: **mean 1.127e-3 (1.13x over), p95 5.067e-3 (1.013x over)**, with p99, max and top-1 all passing. **(b) The full route** (`EXACT_GROUPED_Q4=0` + `EXACT_GROUPED_DOWN=0` + `Q5_1_WMMA=1` - three flags, because the down dispatch tests `exact_grouped_down` **before** `q5_wmma` at 4603 vs 4751): `hard_gates_passed: false` and **worse on every metric** - mean **7.288e-4**, p95 **3.4452e-3**, p99 **1.1172e-2**, max **1.8181e-2**, top-1 **0.99333** = 447/450, and **four** failing scopes (`general_ja`, `mixed_ja_en`, `prefill_last`, `prefill_to_c1`) against the gate/up-only variant's two. On the binding scope it runs at **mean 1.848e-3 (1.85x over), p95 7.716e-3 (1.54x over)**. **Adding the F16 down roughly doubles the drift** (mean 4.584e-4 -> 7.288e-4, p99 5.1165e-3 -> 1.1172e-2, top-1 449 -> 447), so the down half cannot rescue the route. **The row is refuted on three independent grounds:** the full route fails the envelope; it fails worse than its own gate/up half; and it fails against a bar **production meets exactly**, since the iu8-risk route carries the repair precisely so its arithmetic is **bit-identical to the strict parent** (zero deviation by construction). **Together with `EG-3`/the `repair_free_gate_up` result (plain iu8, no repair: 2.16x over on `prefill_last` mean), two distinct operand classes have now been measured and both fail - and F16 is the tighter of the two**, which is what makes this decisive rather than merely negative. **The repair is load-bearing on the prefill path and the 1,219.978 ms is NOT recoverable by operand class.** Engagement was verified before both runs (gate/up: 360 dispatches at `[390..500, 2560, 640, 640]`, 0 repair; full: **47 F16 gate/up + 43 F16 down calls, 0 repair** - not 48 each because layer 2's gate/up is Q5_K and 5 down layers are Q8_0, matching the 94/43/2/5 census). The first down-leg attempt silently did not engage because `exact_grouped_down` wins that chain; the probe caught it. **What remains is not a kernel question:** the binding constraint is the 18-row `prefill_last` scope at a 1e-3 mean limit, which this row's own `prefill_last`-row work already flagged as "a scope-and-threshold question for the qualification packet". **These results do not resolve it and are not a pass under the current envelope.** If that threshold were ever decided differently, the gate/up-only F16 variant at 1.13x over is the nearest candidate - but it still requires the down leg left exact, keeping its 522.992 ms of repair. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-f16-compact-gate-up/` and `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-mx1-full-route/`. **No kernel written, no default changed, nothing promoted.** **THE ROUTE IS NOW CLOSED ON THE OTHER HALF OF ITS OWN ACCEPTANCE TARGET TOO: THE F16 OWNER CANNOT PAY FOR THE REPAIR EVEN IF THE NUMERICS WERE FREE (`20261004T`).** The numerics refutation above settles accuracy; the row's target is also a time bound - `F16 gate/up + down <= 4,280.901 ms` out of a 1,219.978 ms repair ceiling - and the main-kernel half of that comparison had never been measured. `scripts/qwen4exp_moe_owner_ab.py --route gate-up` now puts all three registered owners in one arms pair (production `selected_dual_wmma_iu8_risk_prefill_bf16_bf16_out`, its declared fallback `selected_dual_grouped_rowbatch8_out4_expertgrid64_bf16_bf16_out`, and the F16 route `selected_dual_wmma_prefill_compact_bf16_bf16_out`, which is what `EXACT_GROUPED_Q4=0` selects), real Q4_K geometry, 15 HIP-event repetitions, 2 primed launches, same host. **The F16 owner is slower in 8 of 8 cases** - balanced 512/1024/4096/10240 **+141.6 / +141.3 / +138.7 / +114.3%**, sparse 512/1024/4096/10240 **+138.3 / +139.8 / +106.9 / +53.9%**, median **+138.5%** - against a maximum iu8-risk repeat drift of 0.2229%, i.e. 240x the noise. **The most favourable case already exceeds the entire prize:** the smallest gap, +53.9%, applied to the gate/up half alone (2,779.713 ms) is **+1,498 ms**, more than the whole 1,219.978 ms of repair, before the down half is counted at all; the median case is +3,850 ms, 3.2x the prize. **The comparison is biased in the F16 owner's favour** (`max_risks=0` never charges the iu8-risk owner for its risk-queue writes), so the negative survives the direction of bias that matters. The tile passed to the F16 arm was read from the production call site (`qwen4_exp_runner.py:4430`, `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q4_TILE_M`/`_TILE_N` defaults) rather than chosen, which matters because a smaller `tile_m` would have manufactured this negative. The fallback reproduces its recorded loss (+19.6% to +394.9%, median +157.8%), the control showing the added arm did not perturb the harness. **So MX-1 fails both halves of its acceptance target, and the 1,219.978 ms is not recoverable by operand class on either ground** - it is not a kernel question. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-moe-f16-compact-owner-ab/gate-up-3arm.json` **THE DOWN HALF IS MEASURED, AND IT IS THE FASTEST DOWN OWNER - SO THE UNTESTED LEAD IS A DOWN-ONLY VARIANT (`20261007T`).** The two appends above price the route from the gate/up half and never measure the down owner's main kernel. Re-running `scripts/qwen4exp_moe_owner_ab.py` on the current tree (harness had a latent `padded_rows` `NameError` from the `8544227b7` refactor and no F16-down arm; both fixed) reproduces the gate/up result - F16 gate/up slower 8/8, median **+138.35%** against the committed +138.5% - and adds the missing down arm: `selected_grouped_wmma_prefill_compact_bf16_bf16_out` is the **fastest** registered down owner in **8/8** cases, median **-59.45%** (range -75.51% to -49.15%) against the A/B's pair2 primary, with the iu8-risk down at -36.22%. **The A/B primary is not the production down owner**: production runs the iu8-risk kernel via `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q51_IU8_EXACT=1` (the 2026-09-29 correction), and against that owner the F16 down is **-42.29% median** (range -49.54% to -32.71%). Absolute medians at balanced 512/1024/4096/10240 are **3.07/3.21/3.59/4.46 ms** against pair2's **6.90/7.22/9.80/17.12 ms**. **This does not reopen the full route** - it fails because the gate/up half alone is +138% slower than the owner it would replace, which already exceeds the whole 1,219.978 ms repair, and the full route fails numerics worse than its gate/up half. **What it changes is the remaining gate:** the only MX-1 configuration with a favorable main-kernel measurement whose envelope is unmeasured is the **down-only** variant (`HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_EXACT_GROUPED_DOWN=0` + `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q5_1_WMMA=1`, `EXACT_GROUPED_Q4` left at production). It has **not** been gated; the full-route gate showed the F16 down roughly doubles the accumulated drift when added to an already-drifted gate/up, so its envelope is unknown. **No promotion, no default change, main-kernel screen only (`performance_claim: false`).** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-07-qwen4exp-mx1-f16-owner-recheck/` **THE DOWN-ONLY VARIANT IS NOT DISPATCHABLE ON THE CURRENT TREE: THE F16 DOWN OWNER'S HOST TILE-ROW COUNT IS ZERO ON THE PRODUCTION GATE/UP PATH (`20261007T`).** A named T1 layer2 candidate `f16_compact_down` (`EXACT_GROUPED_DOWN=0` + `Q5_1_WMMA=1`, gate/up untouched) reaches the down owner's direct call and immediately raises `ValueError: compact_rows, num_experts, and wmma_total_rows must be positive` at `qwen4_exp_runner.py:4875` -> `qwen4_exp_q5_1.py:158`. The host-side `wmma_total_rows` is only read back when `q4_k_mmq_prefill or (not exact_grouped_down and not exact_grouped_q4_gate)` (`qwen4_exp_runner.py:4244-4284`); with gate/up left on the production exact route `exact_grouped_q4_gate` is true, so neither holds and the F16 down kernel receives 0 and rejects it before any arithmetic. The full route works only because clearing `EXACT_GROUPED_Q4` also clears `exact_grouped_q4_gate` and populates the count. Engagement is established (the counted alias is the frame that raises), so this is a runtime integration gap, not vacuous non-engagement; it is recorded as a blocker pending parent coordination rather than fixed in-unit. Derived: 52,428,800 outputs and **373,230 risky per call**; 40,960 compact rows per call; each repair re-reads a K=2560 Q4_K row = **1,440 B**, so **537.5 MB per call and 50.5 GB over all calls**, giving an **effective scattered bandwidth of 40.9 GB/s** at **35.2 ns per repair**. **40.9 GB/s is far below this part's memory peak and, combined with EG-3's finding that moving 3.3x fewer bytes made the kernel SLOWER, rules out a bandwidth limit and confirms the latency-bound dependent chain.** **The prize, quantified (which EG-3 did not state)**: q4_k repair 573.211 ms against a 2,713.953 ms fast path at a 0.7119% rate - ideal repair at fast-path efficiency would be 19.32 ms, a **29.7x disproportion**, saving **553.9 ms = 2.44% of the window**; the q5_1 route (661.210 ms repair) has an **UNMEASURED rate** and its 2.87% is an explicitly-labelled inference, so the **5.31% combined ceiling assumes both routes behave identically** and the defensible measured claim is **2.44%**. The repair does **0.71% of the work in 22.7% of the MoE GEMM time**. **MX-1 comparison**: MX-1 removes the repair entirely (up to 5.44%) but changes the arithmetic and needs the full production numerical gate; a repair-efficiency fix would keep the arithmetic and need no such gate, but EG-3's evidence says no known mechanism achieves it. One prompt, one model, one host, one profile, one repetition; the q5_1 rate is unmeasured; the "ideal repair" figure is a **bound, not a proposal**; the 50.5 GB assumes K=2560 for both halves. ### 4F. GDN — 821.6 ms (4.9%)

**Checked-index production leaf, 2026-10-06:** the post-router owner trace
measures GDN 805.945 ms/pass. A D128 wave-norm leaf narrows checked element
indexing without changing FP32 recurrence or norm association. Synthetic
complete-operation speedups are 1.0443x/1.0419x/1.0424x at 512/1024/4096
rows; private scratch 36→28 bytes/lane, spills 8→6, static VGPR 256 unchanged.
Exact replay, ownership, head isolation, CPU floor and cached trace pass.
Natural-model 216-call owner timing improves 1.0267–1.0441x; all 20
canonical/heldout full-logit/state/KV cases pass three repeats and lifecycle.
Tracked-clean `7a1dc7b22` twelve-case/three-pair engine A/B has 72 exact
trajectories and weighted prefill gains +0.149%/+0.303%/+0.227%; both order
strata are positive. Japanese p512 prefill is -0.008% with within-arm CV up
to 0.629%; three request cases lose, worst mixed p512 -0.546%. No all-case
request or decode-speed claim. Retain the independently measured exact
owner saving under the sub-window policy. UD production now selects the
narrow leaf only at Hk16/Hv48/D128, 2–4096 rows; wide-index outside/rollback
and strict primitives remain. Native binder smoke verifies actual dispatch.
[Evidence](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-06-qwen4exp-gdn-narrow-index/README.md).
