All four registered recurrence routes were measured directly on gfx1100 at a realistic shape (Hk16/Hv48/Dk=Dv=128), 5 reps after 1 warmup, per 1K tokens: **`tiled16` (the production route) 4.961 ms**, `register_state` 5.687, `columnwarps` 7.610, `serial_lds` 39.628. **The row's "our 205" matches NO route** - the worst registered route is 5.2x faster than it and the production route is **41x** faster - and **gufo's quoted 65 ms is 13x SLOWER than our production route**, so the row's direction is inverted: we are ahead, not behind. Scaling is exactly linear in tokens (2.898/5.665/11.278 ms at 512/1024/2048), so there is no fixed overhead. **The MECHANISM is confirmed:** every route sits at **1-9% of the 221 GB/s bandwidth roof** and 0.1-1.1% of the FP32 FLOP roof (traffic floor 0.448 ms, FLOP floor 0.053 ms at 1K), so none is traffic-bound and latency/occupancy/issue is the only remaining explanation - supported structurally by the register-state kernel holding **128 floats of state per thread** and carrying roughly **16 `__syncthreads()` per token**, against a column-warp sibling explicitly built with "no `__syncthreads` in the token loop". **THE COST GATE IS AN UNCONDITIONAL UPPER BOUND:** one layer's recurrence is 4.961 ms/1K, there are **9 certified GDN layers**, so ~22.7 ms at p508 against today's **2014 ms** p512 prefill = **1.1%** - meaning even an INFINITELY fast recurrence saves at most 1.1% of prefill, and a realistic 2x recovers ~0.55%. **Redirection, not a rejection of the mechanism:** the GDN family's cost is overwhelmingly OUTSIDE the recurrence (~3% of the family on the last recorded 750.68 ms trace), so if GDN is worth optimizing the norms/gates/conv/prepare/snapshot stages are where the time is - a different row with its own measurement. **Not claimed:** the family's current share of prefill (the 750.68 ms figure is from a 6.500 s prefill and has not been re-measured). One host, one shape, synthetic inputs, wall medians around a device synchronize (launch overhead included, negligible at 5-40 ms/call); no `rocprofv3` stall counters were taken, so "latency-bound" is inferred from distance to both roofs. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd3-recurrence-screen/README.md`, `scripts/qwen4exp_gd3_recurrence_screen.py` **CORRECTION (`20260930T`): THE CEILING ABOVE IS WRONG BY 4.5x - IT IS ~5% OF PREFILL, NOT 1.1%.** The error is the layer count: "9 certified GDN layers" is the **numerical qualification scope** (global layers 35-47), not the model's GDN layer count. The GDN-adjacent stages are small and separately classified (conv 13.625 ms = 0.35%, norm 16.178 ms = 0.41%), so **GD-4's ceiling is the same ~5% plus those two, ~5.5%** - the more complete version of the same idea, not a separate prize. **The mechanism is now confirmed by HARDWARE evidence rather than inference**: the trace records the recurrence dispatch at **`VGPR_Count` 256 (the hardware maximum)**, `SGPR_Count` 128, `LDS_Block_Size` 0, workgroup 128, grid 6144 - at 256 x 32 x 4 B = 32 KB against a 64 KB VGPR file per SIMD that permits **2 waves per SIMD** of a 64-wave-per-CU maximum, ~12.5% occupancy, so "latency-bound" is right and the cause is specifically **register-file capacity, not issue width** (the same conclusion QA-2 reached independently for QSA). Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-family-share/README.md`, `worklog/entries/20260930T013003.667156Z-lhl-qwen4exp-prefill-family-share-d3753c.md` |
| GD-4 | **Chunked GDN on fp16 WMMA** (halo-box PR #91: chunk scan with fused conv/SiLU, q/k norm and gated RMS norm; NMSE ~2e-7 against the sequential kernel) | Changes arithmetic in the recurrence, which carries state across the whole prompt | Screen state drift over 4K prompts and chunk/tail boundaries before a full production gate; `X-1` supplies context, not admission | B | CLOSED — BUILT, MEASURED, AND SLOWER; THE ESCAPE IT PROPOSED ALREADY EXISTS AT 85 VGPR AND 1.4-2.4x FASTER. **COST GATE MEASURED (`20260930T`): the ceiling is ~5.5% of prefill, and it is the same lever as GD-3, not a separate one.** A `rocprofv3 --kernel-trace` of the p512 prefill puts the **GDN family at 194.487 ms of 3914.468 ms kernel time = 4.97%**, and the single recurrence kernel `qwen4_exp_gdn_prefill_f32_kernel<true, true>` accounts for **all** of it. The stages this row's reference fuses on top are separately classified and small: **conv 13.625 ms (0.35%)** and **norm 16.178 ms (0.41%)**. So the fused chunk scan's ceiling is roughly **5.5%** - real, and 5x larger than the 1.1% first attributed to GD-3 (that figure was wrong; see GD-3's correction), but **not a separate prize from GD-3 and not where prefill time is**. **For scale:** MoE is **47.39%** and GR is **34.18%** of prefill - **81.6% between them** - with four individual kernels at **67.7%**, so GDN work of any kind competes for a ~5.5% slice. Two of those four are already on their optimal known path: `gguf_k_prefill_out_coltile_rowbatch_pipelined` (18.05%) is *already* the two-stage K-pipelined form, and `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_wmma_iu8_risk_prefill` (21.77%) is the fastest registered owner by `ED-4`. **Status: still OPEN on its own terms** (no state-drift screen has been run, and the arithmetic does change in the recurrence, which carries state across the whole prompt), but it should be prioritized below the MoE/GR families. The mechanism evidence is now hardware-grade: the recurrence runs at `VGPR_Count` 256 (the hardware maximum), i.e. ~12.5% occupancy, so the register-file-capacity argument that makes a chunked/WMMA reformulation attractive is measured rather than inferred. **STATE-DRIFT SCREEN RUN, AND THE ROW'S STATED BLOCKER IS CLEARED (`20260930T`).** The recurrence is affine in the state, so a chunked composition is *exact in exact arithmetic* and the only question is floating-point reordering. `scripts/gdn_chunk_drift_screen.py` screens it against `replay_gdn` (the same sequential reference the GPU-verification harness uses) at Qwen4Exp's shape over a 4K prompt: **fp32 state carry gives NMSE 4.6e-14 to 7.1e-14, flat in chunk size (32/64/128/256) and indistinguishable from fp32 round-off; an fp16 state carry costs ~4 orders of magnitude and is still tiny (NMSE 9.0e-11 to 8.0e-10), improving with larger chunks because fewer boundary roundings occur - and even the worst case is ~1000x better than the row's own cited reference (PR #91's "NMSE ~2e-7").** **No chunk/tail boundary effect:** 4095, 4096, 4097 and 4100 tokens all sit at NMSE ~7.2e-14. A RED test first confirmed the chunked form converges to the sequential one as the chunk grows (NMSE 4.6e-14 to 7.4e-14 with no growth in T), which is what makes the figures meaningful rather than a measurement of a different algorithm. **The screen also found the one thing a kernel author most needs, and it is not what the row expected:** computing `D_t/D_j` as a **ratio of prefix products** is numerically unsafe - `cum` decays geometrically and underflows to zero non-simultaneously, so while `cum[j]` has underflowed and `cum[i]` has not, the clamped ratio explodes, `L` blows up and the solve diverges (measured max_abs **1.05e1 at chunk 256 against 2.26e-01 at chunk 64** - larger chunks *worse*, the signature of an explosion). A **direct suffix product** `prod_{k=j+1..i} decay[k]` never divides, so every entry stays bounded by 1 and underflows monotonically; that form is committed and locked by `tests/test_unit_gdn_chunk_drift_screen.py`, which fails 5 ways when the prefix form is reinstated. **Remaining work is the kernel build, not more numerics** — BUT THE BUILD PLAN PUTS THE ROW OUT OF SINGLE-ITERATION SCOPE, AND THE ROW MUST NOT BE STARTED UNDER A "PORT IT" ESTIMATE (`20260930T`).** `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-buildplan/README.md` prices the build at **five components, and it is not a port of `tiled16`**: (1) an **fp16 WMMA GEMM primitive in `linear_attn/`**, which has **no precedent in the directory** - WMMA appears only inline inside `process_attention_tile` in `attention/laguna_flash_attention_prefill.hip`, and that use is attention-shaped rather than a callable GEMM helper; (2) the chunk-local scan with cooperative L in LDS, chunk 32, direct suffix products; (3) the chunk-axis state-propagation pass; (4) variant registration with `tiled16` as the strict fallback (this pattern exists); (5) **a second prefill test contract**, which is a governed change rather than a test edit - `tests/test_gpu_qwen4_exp_gdn_hip.py::test_qwen4_exp_gdn_prefill_is_exact_to_serial_decode` holds every existing prefill route **exact to serial decode**, and a chunked scan **cannot** satisfy that by construction because reassociating the recurrence's floating-point sums is the entire point. **The plan's own verdict is "a multi-session build, not a single iteration".** Two further constraints it surfaced that the row does not state, one of which silently destroys the advantage: L and QK are **value-column independent**, so computing them per-warp instead of cooperatively costs **16x** - it turns the priced **1.05 TFLOP/s (2%-of-peak) break-even** into a **~15 TFLOP/s (25%-of-peak)** bar; and the `tiled16` grid is `(num_v_heads=48, col_blocks=8)`, so collapsing to one block per v-head to remove the L redundancy leaves **48 blocks against 96 CUs - half the device idle** - which is why filling the device needs the second kernel. **Governing operating point is chunk 32**, not the feasibility artifact's chunk 128: chunk 128 is 3-5x over the 64 KB per-workgroup LDS limit under every staging choice, while chunk 32 has the **lowest break-even in that artifact's own table** and an fp16 NMSE of `7.961e-10` that is still **251x better than the cited reference's ~2e-7**. **So the row stays OPEN with its numerics fully specified and its real risk named** - bringing WMMA into a scalar-FMA family (component 1) and the second kernel's state handoff (component 3) - and it should be scheduled as a multi-session build, below the MoE/GR families. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-buildplan/README.md`, `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-feasibility/README.md`. **BUILD FEASIBILITY PRICED, AND IT JUSTIFIES THE BUILD (`20260930T`).** GD-3's screen established the recurrence is not traffic-bound but did not establish what a chunked scan would cost. From GD-3's own measured sequential rate (`tiled16`, **4.961 ms per 1K tokens per layer**) and GD-4's already-passed drift screen: the sequential route does **3 x 128x128 = 49,152 MACs per token per v-head** (memory `k.S`, state update `k (x) delta`, output `q.S`) = **4.832 GFLOP per 1K tokens per layer, achieving 974 GFLOP/s = 1.60% of the 61 TFLOP/s fp32 peak**. **The chunked scan adds only `C/384` extra FLOPs** - the L and QK `C x C` matrices, `C^2 x 128` MACs per chunk per head against the sequential route's three 128x128 products per token: **+8.3% at chunk 32, +33.3% at chunk 128, +66.7% at chunk 256**. **Break-even throughput is 1.05-1.62 TFLOP/s = 1.7-2.7% of the fp32 peak** - so the chunked schedule needs only to be about **twice** as efficient as a route sitting at 1.6% of peak, while exposing **C x more parallelism** along the token axis, which is the axis the sequential route has none of. **Chunk 128 is the recommended operating point**: +33.3% FLOPs, 1.30 TFLOP/s break-even, and fp16 state-carry NMSE **1.824e-10** - 5.4x better than chunk 32, because the drift screen found fp16 NMSE *improves* with larger chunks. **What this does NOT show**: that the chunked scan wins. It shows the arithmetic cost is small enough that a win is possible; whether the schedule reaches the 1.3 TFLOP/s bar is what the build must demonstrate. **Correction of my own earlier estimate**: an early pass in this session put the chunked scan at "16x more FLOPs" by comparing against a single 128x128 product rather than three; the true ratio is `C/384`. **Counter constraint re-confirmed**: PMC counters cannot establish the mechanism - `RDNA3-TUNING-GUIDE.md` §4.9 records that every `rocprofv3` counter collection on this stack hangs before the profiled application starts and that `timeout` cannot kill it (SIGTERM handler blocks; use `timeout -k 10`). GD-3 was right to avoid them. **Ceiling: the GDN recurrence is the whole family - 194.487 ms of 3,914.468 ms (4.97%) at p512, and the `gdn` role is 844.295 ms of the 15,741.963 ms union (5.36%) in the X-3 p4096 window - so removing it entirely is worth ~5% of the request and a 2x gain ~2.5%.** Arithmetic model and committed measurements only; **no kernel built and no chunked schedule timed**. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-feasibility/README.md` **BUILD PLAN DERIVED, AND THREE OF ITS FOUR CONSTRAINTS ARE NOT IN THIS ROW (`20260930T`).** Working the design against the validated reference (`scripts/gdn_chunk_drift_screen.py::_chunk_local`) and the existing ABI surfaced constraints that decide whether the kernel can reach the priced break-even. **(1) L and QK are value-column independent** - they depend only on k, q and the decay - so every warp of a v-head needs the same shared data and it **must be computed cooperatively into shared memory**. Computing it per-warp redundantly costs **16x: +66.7% at chunk 16 and +133.3% at chunk 32, against +4.17% and +8.33% cooperative.** This is the difference between the priced **1.05 TFLOP/s break-even and a route that must reach ~15 TFLOP/s** - it converts a 2%-of-peak bar into a 25%-of-peak bar, and it is not diagnosable from timing alone. **(2) The LDS operating point**: staging kn, qn, L, ratio and delta costs 26.3 KB at chunk 16 and 56.6 KB at chunk 32 (both fit the 64 KB workgroup limit; chunk 64 is 129.2 KB and does not). **Chunk 32 is primary** - it fits and its fp16 state-carry NMSE 7.961e-10 is better than chunk 16's, because the drift screen found fp16 NMSE *improves* with larger chunks; chunk 16 is the fallback if LDS-limited occupancy binds. **(3) The block decomposition must change, and this is the structural work**: `tiled16`'s grid is `(num_v_heads=48, col_blocks=8)`, so **8 blocks per v-head would each recompute the shared L** - exactly the 8x redundancy constraint 1 forbids - while collapsing to one block per v-head gives only **48 blocks against 96 CUs, so half the device idles**. Filling the device needs chunk-axis parallelism, i.e. **a two-kernel design with a chunk-level state-propagation pass. This is not a one-kernel port of `tiled16`.** **(4) The suffix product is an in-kernel requirement, not just a reference one**: the kernel's `ratio` matrix *and* the state-carry weights `ratio[-1,:]` must be direct suffix products, or the solve diverges (measured max_abs 1.05e1 at chunk 256 vs 2.26e-01 at chunk 64 - larger chunks worse). **BUILD PLAN ONLY - no kernel written, compiled or timed.** Recorded so the build does not rediscover it. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-buildplan/README.md` **OPERATING POINT CORRECTED TO CHUNK 32 (`20260930T`).** Two of this campaign's own artifacts disagreed: `2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-feasibility` chose **chunk 128** as "the balance" while `2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-buildplan` chose chunk 32. **The LDS arithmetic settles it against chunk 128**: it needs **320.0 KB of workgroup LDS with full fp32 staging and 192.0 KB with fp16 staging, against the 64 KB limit - 5x and 3x over.** The maximum LDS-resident logical chunk is **32** (fp32 staging) or **48** (fp16 kn/qn/L/ratio, fp32 delta). The feasibility artifact picked its operating point on the **numerics axis alone** - fp16 NMSE improves monotonically with chunk size - **without checking residency**, so a builder acting on it would have started at chunk 128 and hit a 5x overrun. **But chunk 32 is right on that artifact's own terms too**: it has the **lowest break-even in its own table** (1.05 TFLOP/s against chunk 128's 1.30), is the only LDS-resident operating point, and its fp16 NMSE 7.961e-10 is still **251x better than the cited reference's ~2e-7**. The extra orders of magnitude chunks 64-128 buy are orders **nobody asked for** - admission asks whether the route clears 2e-7, and chunk 32 clears it by 2.4 orders. **Governing operating point: chunk 32**, with chunk 48 available if `delta` is also staged in fp16 and occupancy does not bind. **BUILD COST: NOT A PORT (`20260930T`).** The row reads as a port of a known technique; it is not. **Five components, one with no precedent in the `linear_attn` family and one of which changes a test contract rather than adding to it.** (1) **An fp16 WMMA GEMM primitive in `linear_attn/`** - **no precedent**: WMMA appears only in the attention family, used inline inside `process_attention_tile` in `attention/laguna_flash_attention_prefill.hip` (intrinsics at lines 230 and 360), attention-shaped rather than a callable generic GEMM helper; the GDN family is **entirely scalar-FMA**. (2) The chunk-local scan. (3) The chunk-axis state-propagation pass. (4) Variant registration with `tiled16` as the strict fallback. (5) **A new GPU test envelope, which conflicts with the existing one**: `tests/test_gpu_qwen4_exp_gdn_hip.py::test_qwen4_exp_gdn_prefill_is_exact_to_serial_decode` holds every existing prefill route **exact to serial decode**, and a chunked scan **cannot satisfy that by construction** - it reassociates the recurrence's floating-point sums, which is the entire point (the drift screen measured the cost: fp32 NMSE ~5-7e-14, indistinguishable from round-off; fp16 state carry 7.961e-10 at chunk 32). So (5) is **adding a second envelope-based prefill contract alongside the exact one**, which `docs/EXECUTION-PROFILES.md` governs rather than a local test edit. **This is a multi-session build, not a single iteration**; the arithmetic and numerics are now fully specified and the residual risk is concentrated in (1) bringing WMMA into a scalar-FMA family and (3) the second kernel's state handoff. **STATE-DRIFT SCREEN RETRACTED - IT MODELLED THE WRONG RECURRENCE (`20260930T`, self-corrected).** `scripts/qwen4exp_gdn_chunk_scan_drift_screen.py` computes `state = gate*state + outer(k, v)` and `out = q @ state`. **That is gated LINEAR attention. GDN is the gated DELTA rule**, whose production kernel computes `delta = (v_in - sum_c k~_c * S_cv * decay) * beta` and `S_next = S*decay + k~ * delta` - it subtracts the state's own prediction from `v` before writing, and the screen has **no `beta` term at all** (its signature is `chunked(q, k, v, gate, chunk, ...)`). **Measured, not argued:** on identical random inputs at the model's shape, `NMSE(screen_sequential, gdn_prefill_recurrent_segments) = 1.009735e+00` - the two recurrences differ by 100% of the signal magnitude (screen out RMS 1.505e-02 vs oracle 8.277e-03, max abs difference 8.345e-02 against an oracle max of 7.590e-02). So **every number from that screen - 1.106e-07 state NMSE, flat in chunk size, no tail penalty, no compounding, the 1.3x fp16-intra cost - describes chunked gated linear attention and says NOTHING about GD-4.** The artifact, its README and the changelog entry are marked retracted. **What survives from that work:** only the methodological finding that its own f32-reassociation control caught a formulation bug, and the general fact that pure f32 reassociation sits at fp32 round-off. **GD-4'S NUMERICAL SCREEN IS NOT DONE.** The correct screen must exercise the triangular/WY form the row's reference uses - `delta[t] = beta[t]*(value[t] - P[t]*key[t]@H0 - sum_{s<t} R[t,s]*(key[t]@key[s])*delta[s])`, the form already implemented and validated in `hipengine.kernels.cpu_reference.gdn_prefill_chunkwise_wy_segments` - and the only in-tree model-level evidence about that form is GPF-8's KL 0.056522 at FP32. The next entry's re-scoped decision experiment is therefore the ONLY route to an answer, and it starts from an unretracted negative precedent rather than a passed screen. The row's prescribed first experiment was to screen state drift over 4K prompts and chunk/tail boundaries before any build. **No chunked/scan GDN kernel exists in this tree** - every registered route is a sequential chain variant - so the screen was run on the arithmetic: the same gated recurrence computed in chunks with fp16 operands, fp16 intra-chunk accumulation and an f32 chunk carry, against the sequential f32 chain, at the real geometry Hk16/Hv48/Dk=Dv=128, 3x kv-repeat, 4096 tokens, decay gates log-uniform in (1e-3, 1). **State NMSE 1.106e-07**, out NMSE 1.56e-07, max abs dS 6.32e-05. **Flat in chunk size** (1.10599e-07 / 1.10582e-07 / 1.10516e-07 / 1.10602e-07 at chunk 64/128/256/512), so a larger chunk - the efficiency-relevant direction - costs no accuracy. **No tail penalty** (chunk 384 with a 256-token tail gives 1.105e-07, the same as every divisible size) and **no compounding over the prompt** (1.138e-07 / 1.147e-07 / 1.099e-07 / 1.106e-07 at 512/1024/2048/4096), which were the two specific hazards this row named. **The reference's claim reproduces in this model's regime**: halo-box PR #91 reports NMSE ~2e-7, measured here at 1.1e-07. **The dominant error is operand rounding, not reassociation order** - pure f32 reassociation sits at fp32 round-off (1.9e-13 to 1.5e-11, the control that validates the formulation), fp16 operands move it six orders, and fp16 intra-chunk accumulation on top costs only a further **1.3x** (1.106e-07 vs 8.462e-08), so the fused fp16 accumulation the kernel needs is nearly free relative to the rounding it already implies. **So the row proceeds to the build rather than closing.** **[RETRACTED - see above.]** The ~5.5% ceiling is unchanged and this remains the same lever as `GD-3`. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-drift/`, harness `scripts/qwen4exp_gdn_chunk_scan_drift_screen.py`. **IN-TREE PRIOR ART FOUND (`20260930T`): THIS MECHANISM WAS ALREADY BUILT, VALIDATED AND REJECTED AS `GPF-8`.** `docs/GGUF-PREFILL-OPTIMIZATION.md` carries a complete candidate record for an **eight-token FP32 chunkwise/triangular-WY GDN prefill** (`chain_wy8`, default `chain_lds32_direct`) that is the same mechanism this row proposes. It is not mentioned anywhere in this ledger - `grep -c GPF-8` returns 0 - so the row reads as an untried idea when it is not. **What GPF-8 establishes in favour of this row:** the chunkwise form DOES escape the register-capacity bound that GD-3 confirmed by hardware counter (the production route sits at `VGPR_Count` 256, the hardware maximum, ~12.5% occupancy). GPF-8 compiled at **48 VGPR, 28672 B LDS, 0 scratch per thread** with workgroup 256 - a 5.3x reduction in register pressure, so the mechanism is validated in-tree rather than hypothesised. It also passed its resource and family gates (34 CPU oracle tests, 33 focused GPU tests, one-token byte-exact both plain and segmented, and the synthetic 30-layer 512-token GDN family at 47.491 ms against a predeclared 66.0 ms ceiling). **What GPF-8 establishes against this row - and this is the part that matters:** it was **byte-exact at one token**, so its kernel was correct, and at full chunks its primitive stayed inside the frozen tolerance (atol 5e-4, rtol 5e-3) - **yet the model-level gate still failed at KL 0.056522 against a 0.05 limit, top-1 445/450 = 98.889% against 0.99**, and only 5 of 18 free-running 128-transition trajectories stayed exact. So the chunkwise reassociation alone, at FP32 with no fp16 anywhere, was enough to fail the model gate while every primitive-level check passed. **That is the same failure mode as `MX-1`**, which sits 8.0-9.5x below the BF16 floor already in the path and still failed the 18-row `prefill_last` scope. **This row adds fp16 operands on top of that reassociation** - the screen above measures fp16 operands plus fp16 intra-chunk accumulation at 1.106e-07 NMSE against pure-f32 reassociation's 1.9e-13, six orders worse - so its numerical risk is strictly greater than a candidate already known to fail. **THE SPEED CASE IS NOW MEASURED, AND IT IS STRONG (`20260930T`, second pass).** GPF-8's artifact records the candidate family at **47.491 ms** but not the baseline; `docs/GGUF-PREFILL-OPTIMIZATION.md`'s GPF-8 section carries it - *"the complete 30-layer GDN family must fall from **210.501 ms** to roughly 66 ms or less"* at the clean 512 trace. **So the chunkwise/WY kernel took the GDN family from 210.501 ms to 47.491 ms: a 4.43x speedup on the exact stage this row targets, beating its own predeclared 66 ms ceiling.** That is direct in-tree evidence that the mechanism delivers on speed; the earlier "cannot be cited either way" caveat is WITHDRAWN. **Its rejection was still correct and still about speed plus numerics:** the whole-model 512 prefill reached 2003.399 tok/s against the 2412.320 llama.cpp HIP floor, 16.951% below (4K passed at +1.116%), and KL 0.056522 failed independently. So the GDN-family win did not by itself clear an external whole-model floor - a fact about that floor, not about the kernel. **Restating this row's prize with the measured 4.43x instead of the ~5.5% ceiling:** the qwen4exp GDN family is 194.487 ms of 3914.468 ms p512 prefill (4.97%), so a 4.43x on the family is 194.487 -> 43.9 ms, saving **~150.6 ms = ~3.85% of prefill**. The 5.5% figure is the ceiling (family to zero); 3.85% is what this mechanism has actually demonstrated on a sibling model. **The entire risk is therefore numerical, not structural** - and that is precisely what the re-scoped decision experiment below tests. **Scope difference that must not be hand-waved:** GPF-8 was Qwen3.6-35B-A3B `gguf_q4_k_m` on the `qwen35_gdn_prefill_*` family; this row is Qwen3.8-Flash-Next `qwen4_exp_gdn_prefill_f32_kernel<true, true>` at a different shape (Hk16/Hv48/Dk=Dv=128, 3x kv-repeat). The mechanism transfers; the numbers do not. **CONSEQUENCE - THE FIRST EXPERIMENT IS RE-SCOPED:** building the fused fp16 WMMA scan first would be the expensive way to learn what a decision experiment can settle. The cheap decisive step is to port the EXISTING CPU oracle's chunkwise/WY arithmetic (`hipengine.kernels.cpu_reference.gdn_prefill_chunkwise_wy_segments`, already validated against an independent token-serial f64 definition at chunk sizes 1/2/3/8/16 plus a 17-token tail) into a plain unfused FP32 GPU kernel for the qwen4exp shape, and run the production envelope on THAT before writing any WMMA. If a correct FP32 chunkwise kernel fails the envelope here as it did at GPF-8, the fp16 fused build is dead and the ~5.5% is not recoverable by this route; if it passes, the fp16 WMMA build is justified with the numerical question already answered. The CPU oracle and its fixture (`tests/test_unit_gdn_chunkwise_wy_cpu.py`, `tests/fixtures/gdn_chunkwise_wy_tiny.json`) are already in-tree, so the oracle half of the RED test exists. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-07-15-gfx1100-gguf-gdn-chunkwise-wy8-rejected.json`  **CORRECT SCREEN RUN (`20260930T`): THE REASSOCIATION IS NUMERICALLY FREE - THE ENTIRE COST IS THE FP16 OPERAND CLASS.** `scripts/qwen4exp_gdn_wy_chunk_drift_screen.py` implements the triangular/WY form in fp32 (verified against the committed oracle at 6.4e-16, single-chunk and multi-chunk with the carry exercised; the committed oracle's own wy8-vs-serial agreement is 1.556e-14) and measures its drift against the serial form's, both scored against the float64 oracle, at seq 512 / Hk16/Hv48/Dk=Dv=128 / beta in [0.1,0.9] / decay in [0.7,0.999]. **In fp32 the WY form drifts at 0.65-0.80x the serial form's OWN fp32 drift and gets BETTER as the chunk grows** (out NMSE: serial 5.359e-14; wy chunk 8 4.265e-14 = 0.80x; chunk 16 3.856e-14; chunk 32 3.610e-14; chunk 64 3.477e-14 = **0.65x**). **So the WY reassociation cannot explain GPF-8's KL 0.056522** - that failure must come from the **direct-conv Q/K/V input path** GPF-8 bundled with the recurrence change, which one-token byte-exactness does not establish over a long sequence and which the artifact never separated by measurement. **The entire cost is the fp16 operand class, and chunking REDUCES it:** serial fp16 operands 1.297e-07 out NMSE against wy fp16 chunk 64's **8.637e-08 = 0.67x the serial fp16 form**, and in state 4.423e-08 against **6.093e-10 = 73x better**, structurally because the WY form computes the chunk's state contribution as one sum where the serial chain applies 512 successive fp16 roundings to the carried state. **WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE BUILD.** (1) The numerical risk this row was carrying is **not the chunking** - it is exactly the risk of running the recurrence with fp16 operands, which is `MX-1`'s F16-operand class, and MX-1's F16 route failed the `prefill_last` scope while sitting 8.0-9.5x BELOW the BF16 floor already in the path. (2) **GPF-8 already demonstrated the speed at FP32** - 210.501 -> 47.491 ms on the 30-layer 512 GDN family, 4.43x, at 48 VGPR against the production route's 256 - so the structural win (chunk-level matmuls, lower register pressure) is what bought that, NOT the operand format. (3) **The recommended build is therefore the chunked WY form with FP32 operands**: it keeps GPF-8's measured 4.43x and this screen shows its arithmetic drift is BELOW the current production path's, while fp16 WMMA adds a speed increment whose size is unmeasured here and adds the entire numerical risk. (4) **Keep the existing input path** - since the reassociation is free in fp32, GPF-8's model-gate failure is evidence against the direct-conv change, not against chunking. **Limits:** synthetic inputs and a NumPy model of the arithmetic, not the real kernel; drift is not KL (GPF-8's own numbers are the caution - a primitive inside frozen tolerance still failed the model gate, and the `prefill_last` scope has now failed two non-exact routes, so the fp16 arm must be gated and not assumed); the 4.43x is GPF-8's on Qwen3.6-35B-A3B q4_k_m at C=8, not qwen4exp at this shape. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-wy-chunk-drift/`. The superseded `qwen4exp_gdn_chunk_scan_drift_screen.py` and its artifact are deleted and banner-marked respectively. **THE RECOMMENDED FP32 BUILD IS DONE, AND IT IS SLOWER THAN EVERY ROUTE THAT MATTERS (`20260930T`, lane reading corrected `20261001T`).** `qwen4_exp_gdn_prefill_wy_f32_kernel` (chunk 8, 256 threads, 128x32 state tile in LDS, FP32 operands, same two-stage `core_norm_gate` shape as the other prefill owners) is **parent-parity correct** against the production route - core max abs 5.96e-07 (3.8e-7 of ref max), state max abs 5.59e-09 (1.9e-7) - and independently agrees with an fp64 serial GDN recurrence (2.9e-05 on output). **Speed, 7 reps after warmup, medians, gfx1151, all seven routes in one session:** `wy_chunked` **2.620 / 6.202 / 13.545 ms** at 512 / 1024 / 2048 tokens against `tiled16` **2.177 / 5.063 / 11.828**, `wave_norm` **2.763 / 5.413 / 10.731** and `tiled16_multi` **1.157 / 3.150 / 7.741**. The build is therefore **1.20x / 1.22x / 1.15x slower than the non-multi tiled16 route it was screened against**, **1.05x faster at 512 but 1.15x / 1.26x slower than `wave_norm` - the route the UD-Q4_K_XL production lane actually dispatches**, and **2.26x / 1.97x / 1.75x slower than `tiled16_multi`**. Largest rep spread 6.1%, most under 2.6%; a first 5-arm run reproduced the `tiled16`/`wy` columns within 1.3%, and `tiled16_multi`'s 1.157 ms at 512 matches the promotion artifact's own 1.112 ms. **The 1.19x MAC count explains the slowdown:** the serial route does three 128x128 products per token per v-head (49,152 MACs); the chunked route keeps those three (key/query state and the chunk-final state, 49,152) and adds 9,216 - the `C x C` key/key and query/key dots (8,192: each of the four value-column blocks recomputes them in full, and half of that square is the discarded upper triangle), the triangular solve (448) and the output combination (576). 58,368 / 49,152 = **1.19x**, against a measured 1.15-1.22x; a cooperative lower-triangle implementation would add only 2,176 MACs (1.04x), and the built kernel carries **169 VGPR**, so it does not deliver the occupancy either. **THE ROW'S 256-VGPR PREMISE IS NOT STALE - IT IS TRUE FOR THE LANE THE TRACE MEASURED, AND AN EARLIER REVISION OF THIS SECTION WAS WRONG.** That revision read the family-share trace as a stale, pre-promotion trace and quoted 56 VGPR as the production route; both were errors. **The lane split:** the `gguf_ud_q4_k_xl` production profile applies `PRODUCTION_ARITHMETIC_RECOVERY_FLAGS`, which zeroes `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL`; the runner then takes the register path, and `GDN_REGISTER_PREFILL=1` + `GDN_WAVE_NORM=1` (both production bindings) resolve it to `qwen4exp_sigmoid_wave_norm_prefill`, the `<true,true>` kernel at **256 VGPR**. The family-share trace is exactly this: **72 dispatches of `<true,true>`, zero tiled dispatches, bound env `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL=0`, model root `UD-Q4_K_XL`** - so the 4.97% family share and the 256-VGPR figure describe the UD lane's own route. Compiled from `.amdhsa_next_free_vgpr` on the current source (rocprofv3 reports the allocated counts, i.e. these rounded up - `wy` 169 -> 176 VGPR, 28576 -> 28672 B LDS): `wave_norm` **256 / 0** (UD production), `register_state` **256 / 0** (no profile), `columnwarps` **46 / 0**, the non-multi `tiled16` **56 / 16640** (**no profile selects it** - this is the instantiation the earlier revision mistook for production), **`tiled16_multi` 85 / 16640 (q4_k_m production)**, and the new `wy_chunked` **169 / 28576**. **The larger finding is that the escape already exists and is cheaper.** `tiled16_multi` is **1.39-2.39x faster than the UD lane's `wave_norm` route at a third of the registers**, and it is already registered and already the `q4_k_m` production selection - it is gated off on UD by arithmetic recovery (not bit-exact; NMSE 1.8e-14), and on `q4_k_m` the composition fails the calibrated envelope for a pre-existing reason with an attribution control that fails worse (`2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gdn-multicolumn-q4km`). **So this row's mechanism is not the cheapest escape on either lane:** on UD the exact route in use must be beaten and a chunked reassociation changes arithmetic; on q4_k_m a cheaper built route already exists. The remaining work is arithmetic admission for an existing kernel, not a chunked/WMMA build. **One measurement trap recorded:** the production prefill wrappers apply `core_norm_gate` in place after the recurrence, so a new recurrence kernel writing raw `core` looks ~100% wrong against them (measured 1.067 against a ref max of 1.563) while its state matches to 5.6e-09. **Limits:** one host (gfx1151, not the W7900 default), synthetic inputs, wall medians around a device synchronize, no `rocprofv3` stall counters; the lane gates are quoted from their own artifacts, not re-run here. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-01-qwen4exp-gd4-wy-kernel-built/` |

If the +0.228 s / +0.126 s effects are inside noise as the Evidence column asserts, the row should be CLOSED; if they are real, the prescribed experiment is not an experiment. Either way the row is not actionable as written. One of those two columns needs correcting before it is worked.** **SETTLED BY READING THE SOURCE OF THE TWO FIGURES (`20260930T`).** They come from `benchmarks/results/2026-09-16-disabled-family-recoverable-time/README.md`, a nine-family sweep on `code-p4096` whose fallback median prefill is **23.648 s**: rank 5 `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL` 23.420 s (**+0.228 s, +0.96%**) and rank 6 `GDN_PEER_PREFILL` 23.522 s (**+0.126 s, +0.53%**). A ~20% improvement inside a ~5% family is ~1% of a wall; both statements describe that same effect. **The row's real defect is that it names a KERNEL for a GATE's measurement.** `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL=1` is the gate that admits the tiled16 owner: the runner consults `qwen4_exp_gdn_tile16_prefill_selected` only inside `if colwarps_prefill:`, and that predicate returns the **tiled16** route for the envelope shape, using the columnwarp parent only for non-envelope shapes and for the `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_GDN_TILE16_PREFILL=0` opt-out (`hipengine/runtime/qwen4_exp_runner.py:286-308`). Closed with no experiment to run, which is what the row's own First-bounded-experiment column said all along.|

### 4G. PLE — 9.9 ms in the warm anchor

Warm page-cache PLE is not a dominant prefill owner. The embedding table is
host-mapped, not wholly GPU-resident; “resident weights” must not erase the
cold/new-row serving case. Journey `R13`–`R15` and external `E7` remain separate,
unranked work until that workload has an exposed cost.

| ID | Mechanism / evidence | First bounded experiment and deciding gate | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PL-1 | **Copy elision, sorted dedup and persistent reads** (`R13`, `R14`); historical cold gains came with warm regressions | Measure gather, dedup/scatter, I/O and H2D separately on declared warm and cold/new-row category inputs. Compare the existing mmap path with one changed copy/read mechanism; inventory weight pinning/residency, startup fragmentation and KV-versus-page-cache budget, and charge host RSS/page-cache pressure and first-token wall. Advance only for a declared serving scope with a complete-request benefit, not another warm sweep or unchanged direct-to-ring layout | OPEN — workload-dependent, not a warm-kernel priority **FLAG WITHDRAWN — IT WAS A CELL MIS-ASSIGNMENT, NOT A MALFORMED ROW (`20260930T`).** The §11 census audit claimed this row's *First-bounded-experiment* column reads "OPEN — workload-dependent, not a warm-kernel priority" and therefore "holds a STATE where an experiment belongs". **That text is in the `State` column**, which is where a state belongs; the experiment column holds a complete, bounded experiment — separate measurement of gather, dedup/scatter, I/O and H2D on declared warm versus cold/new-row inputs, one changed copy/read mechanism against the existing mmap path, with the residency and RSS/page-cache inventory and the deciding gate all named. The row has four cells against its four-column header (ID, mechanism/evidence, first bounded experiment and deciding gate, state), so the mapping is unambiguous and the audit's column reference was simply wrong. **The row is well-formed and stays actionable.** Its stated experiment remains correct as written: advance only for a declared serving scope with a complete-request benefit. |
| PL-2 | **Asynchronous PLE/UMA staging** (`R15`, `E5`, Wilkin §3) | First measure exposed copy/wait time. If material, test one bounded double-buffer path with delayed consumers, wrap, cancellation, c2, graph pointer generations, allocation-root lifetime and ordered output consumption. Include buffer bytes in admission; reject if synchronization merely moves elsewhere | COST-GATED OUT — the exposed copy/wait gate fails **COST-GATED OUT (`20260929T`).** The gate is "first measure exposed copy/wait time"; measured, and it fails. Two independent measurements, neither needing the model: `scripts/qwen4exp_ple_h2d_cost.py` times the pinned H2D at the real shapes - prefill 512x2560 fp32 = 5.00 MiB / **67.60 us** / 77.6 GB/s; decode 1x2560 fp32 = 10.00 KiB / **16.97 us** / 0.6 GB/s (latency-bound) - giving **2.240 ms per request** (128 decode + 1 prefill, 6.25 MiB). In-engine attribution via `--ple-telemetry --case-id code-p512` (fresh run, current code): gather+dequantize **0.974 s**, staging copy 0.409 ms, H2D (decode only) 11.444 ms. **Verdict: addressable = H2D 2.240 ms + staging 0.409 ms = ~2.65 ms against the 9,055.58 ms production code-p512 wall (2026-09-07 baselines) = 0.029%.** Even perfect overlap recovers 0.029%, and one decode copy is 0.027% of a 61.75 ms decode step. The dominant PLE term is **gather/dequantize at 0.974 s (~370x the whole copy cost)** - CPU work on the mmap'd table that async staging does not touch; the 2026-09-02 artifact reached the same ranking independently. **No candidate path built**, per the gate's own "if material" condition. Also found and fixed a telemetry gap: the **prefill** PLE copy did not call `record_h2d`, so telemetry understated the request H2D payload **4x** (1,310,720 = 1.25 MiB -> **6,553,600 = 6.25 MiB** = 5,242,880 prefill + 1,310,720 decode, matching the independently measured shapes exactly); the added call is guarded like the decode path's, so it is a no-op with telemetry off and production is unaffected. `performance_claim: false` - this measures the existing synchronous copy and rejects a candidate, not an alternative. **Instrumented walls are noisy and excluded from performance evidence** (gather_dequant moved 0.974 -> 1.635 s between identical telemetry runs; h2d_wall moved 11.444 -> 11.988 ms for an added 67.60 us copy) - the deterministic **bytes** are the reliable evidence. One shape, one host. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-ple-staging-cost/README.md`|

### 4H. Chunking and runtime

| ID | Mechanism / measured cost | Opportunity and limit | First bounded experiment | Track | State |
