| RT-1 | **Prefill chunk 2048 / 4096** (was `CC-1`). Competitors run 2048 (gufo) and 4096 (halo-box benchmarks) | Dense half measured −23.5% (325 ms) at 4096; revised MoE estimate 0.95–1.07 s and total ~1.3 s (STATUS §5), not the older additive 1.0 + 0.6 s MoE estimate. The September 15 engine A/B measured +3.94% on 4K on a composition ~32% slower. 2048/4096 explicit options and mandatory scratch accounting were qualified on the September 15 composition; optional sidecars, graphs, verification and today's F16 activation workspace still need admission evidence | Re-measure 2048 and 4096 against today's default with real per-expert populations; measure together with `DP-7` (wide-tile TFLOP/s at larger M). Re-run the gate at the new chunk (`X-6`) | A | OPEN — largest Track A lever outside the MoE rows; **the serving half is now PROMOTED (see the last segment)**. `R11` **RE-MEASURED AGAINST TODAY'S DEFAULT (`20260929T`): chunk 4096 is **-4.94%** on `code-p4096`.** Radeon 8060S gfx1151, UD-Q4_K_XL, production profile, cached build, three repetitions per arm, one process per arm: chunk 1024 **15.625 s -> 4096 14.854 s, -771.9 ms**. The separation between medians is **5.70x** the larger within-arm spread and the arms do not overlap, so the effect is established at this sample size; a 1-rep screen also covered 2048 (-4.52%) and gave 4096 -5.77%, which the median **supersedes**. **`token_id` and `logits_sha256` are identical on every arm at every repetition**, with `fell_back_to_strict=False` - chunk size changes scheduling, not arithmetic. **Cost: peak allocation 84.44 -> 89.40 GB (+4.96 GB, +5.9%)**, an admission trade. **NOT promoted:** two concrete blockers - the numerical gate must be re-run at the new chunk size (`X-6`), and `RT-3` records that short-request costs historically kept 1024 as the default, so the right form is a shape-based policy rather than a blanket flip. The September 15 +3.94% engine A/B is neither confirmed nor contradicted (different date, changed composition) and is not presented as such. One case, one host, prefill only. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk-remeasure/README.md` **FIRST PROMOTION BLOCKER CLEARED (`20260929T`): the X-6 gate re-run at chunk 4096 PASSES and both arms reproduce the chunk-1024 certification BIT-IDENTICALLY.** `--route wmma --layers 16-47 --prefill-chunk-size 4096 --decode-steps 128 --repeat-runs 3`: teacher `550bb9b832bc7ba2...` and candidate `65370710c0b9c40f...` are the same hashes the chunk-1024 run recorded (`550bb9b8` / `65370710`), mean KL 3.795524e-4 against 3.79552e-4, `hard_gates_passed: true`, p95 1.762e-3, max 1.530e-2, top-1 0.99354, `repeat_deterministic: true`, `canonical_numerical_qualification_claim: true`. So the route arithmetic AND its drift are **invariant to the prefill chunk size** over 1024->4096, not merely close. **Checked against the false-pass mode first, since this gate previously returned KL exactly 0.0 with `measurement_valid: true` on a route that never ran:** engagement is recorded rather than inferred - `candidate_route_engaged: true`, `teacher_route_engaged: false`, `wmma_prefill` candidate rate 0.373585 vs teacher 0.0, **792** `wmma_prefill_f32_f32_out` launches, `selectors_observed_in_one_arm_only: []`, KL nonzero, teacher genuinely the cleared exact chain. **Does NOT clear:** the second blocker (`RT-3` short-request behaviour and the +4.96 GB allocation) and `X-7`'s remaining profile blockers, which the gate still reports and which the chunk change neither creates nor worsens. Still not promoted. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk-remeasure/README.md` **SECOND BLOCKER DOES NOT REPRODUCE (`20260929T`): there is NO short-request cost.** `code-p512` at chunk 1024 vs 4096, three reps per arm: median 2.0074 vs 2.0114 s, a +0.20% difference that is **0.08x** the larger within-arm spread with the arms overlapping, and `token_id`/`logits_sha256` identical. **Peak allocation is byte-identical (83,857,109,608 B)**, not +4.96 GB. Mechanism, from the code: `allocate_scratch` sizes every owner at `rows = min(chunk_size, runner.max_sequence_length)` and the harness sets `max_sequence_length = max(768, prompt_tokens + 1)`, so `code-p512` gives `max_seq_len` 768 and both chunk sizes resolve to 768 rows - the same execution - while `code-p4096` gives 4097 and they resolve to 1024 vs 4096, which is the +4.96 GB. So the workspace is **already shape-aware** and for any prompt <= 1023 tokens the two chunk sizes are byte-for-byte identical in time and memory. **Correction to my own earlier reasoning:** I claimed a shape-based policy would have to make the *allocation* shape-aware and that this was a larger change than `RT-3` assumes - wrong, the allocation already is; I had `min(chunk, max_seq_len)` right and wrongly assumed `max_seq_len` was constant across runs. **What remains is a single memory-admission question**: can the host afford a 89.40 GB peak on long prompts, where the +4.96 GB is paid by the prompt that gets the -4.94%. `RT-3`'s short-request objection does not reproduce here. Still not promoted. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk-remeasure/README.md` **QUALIFICATION TO MY OWN MEASUREMENT (`20260929T`): the "default" in this packet is the harness's, not the server's.** Everything measured above compares **1024 -> 4096**, because 1024 is the benchmark protocol's value and what the recorded attribution command passed. But the engine has more than one chunk-size default: `EngineLoopConfig.max_prefill_chunk_tokens` (`DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS`) is **256** and governs the serving/CLI path, while `EngineLoop.__init__`'s `prefill_chunk_size = 1024` is only the *no-config fallback* - line 1907 sets `self.prefill_chunk_size = int(resolved_config.max_prefill_chunk_tokens)`, so a supplied config overrides it with 256, and the CLI's own help says "default: 256". So the serving-relevant comparison is **256 -> 4096** (16x the chunk, not 4x) and it is **UNMEASURED**. The -4.94% is therefore a valid result for 1024 -> 4096 under this harness protocol and is **not** evidence for the size of the production change; nothing here should be cited as "the server would be 4.94% faster". The X-6 gate result is unaffected in kind - route arithmetic and drift are invariant to chunk size over the range tested, which does not depend on which default is in play - but the speedup figure does. Next measurement if pursued: 256 -> 4096 on the same case and protocol. Still not promoted. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk-remeasure/README.md` **SERVING-RELEVANT COMPARISON MEASURED (`20260929T`): the win against the serving default is far larger than against the protocol value.** Since 256 - not 1024 - is what the serving path defaults to (`EngineLoopConfig.max_prefill_chunk_tokens`), the governing comparison was measured: `code-p4096`, three reps per arm, chunk 256 median **23.8886 s** vs chunk 4096 **14.6882 s** = **-9.2004 s (-38.51%)** (min-to-min -38.42%). **Arms do not meaningfully overlap**: 4096's slowest rep (14.7967) beats 256's fastest (23.8427) by 38%; the naive range-overlap test trips only because the 256 arm has a 1.32x outlier (31.5102 s) extending it upward. Honest counterweight: that outlier inflates the 256 arm's stdev to 3.6037 vs 4096's 0.0524, so `abs(median delta)` is only 2.55x the larger stdev - weaker by that statistic than the 5.70x for 1024 -> 4096 - but min-to-min and worst-vs-best are unambiguous. **Numerics: the chunk size is NOT invariant here** - `token_id` identical (248068) but `logits_sha256` DIFFERS (e717076f vs 7270a972), so this is an arithmetic change relative to today's serving default, not a pure scheduling one. Not a gate regression: X-6 passes at 4096 and hashes bit-identically to the 1024 certification, so 4096 is the certified configuration. Note the route IS invariant between 1024 and 4096 (identical hashes) but NOT between 256 and 4096 - both measured, and neither generalizes to "chunk size does not affect numerics". Memory: peak 83.20 -> 89.40 GB = **+6.20 GB**; GTT is 120 GB here, leaving 30.6 GB headroom (25.5%). **Limits: ONE case in ONE category** - the 12-case 4-category suite run is the evidence that would generalize this, and until it lands the -38.51% is single-case. Prefill only; one host, and the +6.20 GB admission cost is host-specific. **No default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-serving-chunk/README.md` **FULL SUITE MEASURED (`20260929T`): 12/12 cases faster at chunk 4096, all completely separated, `token_id` identical 12/12, and `logits_sha256` identical in ZERO of 12.** All 12 fixture cases (4 categories x 3 sizes), three reps per arm, both chunk sizes, 24 invocations all exit 0. Effect is monotone in size and flat across category: p512 -28.2/-28.9/-28.7/-29.7%, p1024 -35.0/-33.4/-33.5/-34.2%, p4096 -37.6/-36.5/-35.4/-37.2%; within-size spread under 1.6pp, no category an outlier. Peak delta +0.77/+1.24/+6.20 GB by size, tracking `min(chunk, max_sequence_length)`. **So the arithmetic changes in EVERY case** - this is not a scheduling change and cannot be certified by bit-identity. It IS certified by the canonical numerical gate at chunk 4096 over this same 12-case fixture: `canonical_numerical_qualification_claim: true`, kl_mean 0.00038 / p95 0.00176 / p99 0.00542 / max 0.0153 against 0.001/0.005/0.02/0.05, top1 above floor. Stated precisely: that artifact records `profile_qualification_claim: false` with route-level blockers (category-heldout, strict-teacher/BF16-relative, exact control/isolation, task-quality, route engagement) that were **already open before this work** - not introduced by the chunk size and not cleared by it. **PROMOTION DECISION: NOT a blanket default flip, and the blocker is concrete.** `DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS` is GLOBAL across every model the engine serves and the workspace is `min(chunk, max_seq_len)` for all of them, while the +6.20 GB is measured for ONE model on ONE host - so a blanket 4096 multiplies the prefill workspace everywhere and turns a working configuration into a possible OOM on a lower-memory host. Bounding facts this establishes: (1) the historical short-request objection is DEAD (a 512-token request is unaffected in time and memory - the workspace is already shape-aware), so the only remaining constraint is peak memory; (2) there is no scoped hook - this model has no geometry-keyed chunk entry (the `GGUF_PREFILL_n` pattern) and the runner has no memory-aware admission guard, so today the choice is a global flip or new mechanism. **Concrete next step if pursued: memory-bounded chunk selection** (largest chunk the host can admit, falling back toward 256) - a real change with its own test/gate burden, not a constant edit. Limits: one host, one model, prefill only, numerical-not-profile gate. **No default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-serving-suite/README.md` **DECISIVE COMPARISON MEASURED (`20260929T`): 256 -> 1024, both arms in ONE run (REFACTOR forbids cross-run chunk comparisons), all 12 cases, three reps per arm, 24 artifacts all exit 0. **12/12 faster, 12/12 completely separated, `token_id` identical 12/12, `logits_sha256` identical 0/12.** p512 -29.0 to -29.4%, p1024 -32.9 to -34.4%, p4096 -32.9 to -35.7%; flat across category. **The marginal trade is the finding:** at `code-p4096`, 256 -> 1024 is **-34.85% for +1.24 GB**, while 256 -> 4096 is -37.61% for +6.20 GB - so 1024 -> 4096 buys **2.76 further percentage points for 4.96 GB more**. 256 -> 1024 captures **93% of the win at 20% of the memory**, so **1024 is the promotion candidate, not 4096**. Peak delta is flat at +1.24 GB for p1024/p4096 because the workspace is `min(chunk, max_seq_len)`. **Chunk 1024 is the CERTIFIED configuration** - the X-6 gate's numerical certification is at chunk 1024 and hashes bit-identically at 4096 - so this promotion lands on the configuration the gate actually certifies. `logits_sha256` still differs from 256 in all 12 cases, so it is an ARITHMETIC change relative to the current serving default. **PROMOTION BLOCKER - and it is NOT the memory cost, which at +1.24 GB is small:** `max_prefill_chunk_tokens` lives in `EngineLoopConfig` beside `fair_prefill_burst_chunks`, `round_prefill_token_budget` and `round_decode_row_budget`; its CLI help reads "Maximum prefill chunk tokens per loop tick" and dispatch caps it again at the round budget. It is a **C4 scheduler knob bounding per-tick prefill work so decode is not starved**, not a model-shape parameter. Raising it 4x makes each prefill tick up to 4x longer; the measured -29% to -36% is PREFILL THROUGHPUT and says nothing about DECODE LATENCY UNDER CONCURRENCY, which is what the tick bound exists to protect. That is unmeasured, and it is the prerequisite REFACTOR already names ("qualify ... c2 behavior"). So: prefill throughput for 256 -> 1024 is MEASURED; the serving default is BLOCKED on the interleaving/fairness measurement. A prefill-only or batch path is directly supported. Limits: one host, one model, prefill only, no c2/c8 claim. **No default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-256-vs-1024/README.md` **CHUNK-INVARIANCE CLASS NARROWED (`20260929T`): the class is exactly {1024, 2048, 4096} - CHUNK 256 IS OUTSIDE IT.** Reading the published `logits_sha256` across all three RT-1 packets (no re-run): **chunk 1024 vs 4096 is identical on 12 of 12 canonical cases**, an independent three-point run on `code-p4096` confirms 1024 = 2048 = 4096 (`7270a972da43ff46...`), and **chunk 256 vs 4096 differs on 12 of 12 cases**. So this row's "chunk size changes scheduling, not arithmetic" claim is **correct within the >= 1024 class and FALSE at 256** - it should be read as scoped, not wrong, since its own arms were 1024 and 4096. **`token_id` IS invariant across all four chunk sizes including 256** (12/12), so the 256 divergence is **sub-argmax**: it does not change the chosen token on these cases but is a real arithmetic difference that could change a near tie. **Chunk 256 is not numerically interchangeable with 1024+** - any comparison mixing the two (a baseline at 256 against a candidate at 1024, or a gate run at 256 quoted for a 1024 deployment) compares two configurations, the same protocol-confound class the external review checklist C1-C6 warns about. **Likely mechanism (HYPOTHESIS, not established)**: chunk size sets the per-chunk row count and several kernels are selected by row/M thresholds (dense wide-256, coltitle/rowbatch families, staged routes); a threshold between 256 and 1024 rows would select a different kernel and hence a different reduction order. No dispatch trace was taken. **This also predicts the X-6 gate passes at chunk 4096**: the certification is at 1024 and 1024 vs 4096 is bit-identical on all 12 cases - a prediction the chunk-4096 gate run tests directly. Attribution from existing artifacts, not a new run; one model, one quant, one host, one profile; equality is of the hashed published logits, not intermediate tensors; the boundary between 256 and 1024 is not narrowed further. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-chunk-invariance-class/README.md` **BLOCKER SET REDUCED TO FOUR NAMED ITEMS; PROMOTION STILL CORRECTLY BLOCKED (`20260929T`).** AGENTS.md promotes measured exact non-regressive wins "unless there is a concrete blocker recorded in a durable worklog entry" - and there is one: `docs/REFACTOR.md` names what enabling automatic selection requires. **THE MECHANISM ALREADY EXISTS, IS TESTED, AND IS DELIBERATELY UNWIRED** - this changes the remaining work from "build a policy" to "enable and qualify a policy": `Qwen4ExpPrefillWorkspace` owns prefill scratch/inputs independently; `_allocate_extra_prefill_workspace(chunk_size)` prepares an inactive runner-owned owner; `_configure_prefill_workspace_selection(workspaces, *, reserve_bytes)` selects among prepared owners, allocates nothing, rejects owners beyond staging capacity, and restores borrowed fields on failure; per-request selection is `next((w for w in choices if len(tokens) <= w.prefill_chunk_size), choices[-1])`; `tests/test_unit_qwen4exp_prefill_owner.py` covers ordering, duplicates, foreign owners, staging capacity and the reserve check; `scripts/qwen4exp_owned_workspace_check.py` verifies the extra owner allocates exactly its prefill-only budget and that prefill preparation does not replace decode state. **`_configure_prefill_workspace_selection` HAS NO CALLER OUTSIDE TESTS** - REFACTOR.md calls it "a private, default-disabled selector" and the check script's report reads "automatic selection is not enabled". **CLEARED THIS SESSION (with measurements, not arguments)**: (1) **numerical qualification at the new chunk** - the X-6 chunk-4096 gate passes **bit-identically** to chunk 1024 on all 12 cases, all five KL metrics to the last digit, 12 trajectory hashes, 3 repeats; (2) **arithmetic neutrality / preserve strict setup** - chunk 1024 vs 4096 publishes **identical `logits_sha256` on 12 of 12 cases**; (3) **the short-request loss** - p512 chunk-4096 cost is **+0.46% mean, SMALLER than the within-arm spread**, i.e. indistinguishable rather than a loss, and p1024 is -0.39%; (4) chunk-4096 accounting was already recorded repaired. **STILL OPEN, precisely named**: (a) **"measure short/long requests on the SAME PREPARED OWNERS"** - the specific gap, since every measurement so far including this row's and RT-3's used **one process per arm**, so the selection path has never been exercised end-to-end on a prepared pair; (b) qualify full state/lifetime and c2 behavior on the selection path; (c) account for all owners and host staging in public construction (the selector's reserve check is explicitly not public memory admission); (d) allocation and lifetime gates for the selector. **NOT PROMOTED - the reason is a recorded concrete blocker, not an unmeasured risk**, and the blocker set is now four named items down from an open-ended "short-request costs keep 1024 as the default". Status synthesis, not a new measurement; one model, one quant, one host, one profile; the cleared items are cleared for the measured scope (canonical 12 cases, one prompt family) only. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-blocker-status/README.md` **THE SHAPE POLICY IS UNNECESSARY - THE LAST MEASUREMENT-SHAPED BLOCKER IS RESOLVED BY REMOVING IT (`20260929T`).** REFACTOR.md's fourth requirement was "measure short/long requests on the SAME PREPARED OWNERS" - every prior measurement including this row's and RT-3's used ONE PROCESS PER ARM, so the selection path had never been exercised end-to-end on a prepared pair. **Measured now: one runner, two prepared owners (base 4096 sets the staging capacity, extra 1024), one process, selector configured via `_configure_prefill_workspace_selection([base, extra], reserve_bytes=4 GiB)`, and the owner that served each request RECORDED BY A PROBE INSIDE `_prefill_chunked_impl`** - the function the selector calls AFTER swapping fields - so the served owner is observed, not inferred. **The selection works and selects correctly for both shapes**: short (512 tok) served by **1024**, long (4096 tok) by **4096**. **Then the decisive A/B: the same short prompt with the selector turned OFF, forced onto the large base owner.** Result: selector on **3.2068 s** vs off **3.1730 s** = **-1.06%, and that difference is 0.017x the within-arm spread** - two orders of magnitude inside the noise. The honest statement is **no measurable benefit**, not "the small owner is worse". **The policy is also arithmetic-neutral here**: the short request's `logits_sha256` is **IDENTICAL** on the 1024 and 4096 owner (`fefecd92...`), consistent with this row's 12-of-12 chunk invariance. **The extra owner costs 1.555 GiB and returns no measured benefit.** **WHY THE PREMISE FAILS**: REFACTOR.md justifies the shape policy as "preserving the smaller owner's short costs", which assumes a smaller workspace makes a short request cheaper. It does not - the workspace is **SCRATCH**, its row count is `min(chunk_size, max_sequence_length)`, and the kernels' work scales with the **ACTUAL TOKEN COUNT**, not with the size of the scratch they are handed. A 512-token prefill does 512 tokens of work whichever owner supplies the buffers. This is the stronger version of the mechanism this row already found from the other direction (for prompts at or below the smaller chunk, both sizes resolved to the same `min(chunk, max_seq_len)`); here the owners genuinely differ (1024 vs 4096 rows against a 4352 sequence) and the short request is STILL unaffected. **WHAT THIS CHANGES - IT REMOVES A BLOCKER RATHER THAN CLEARING IT**: the shape policy was the reason a blanket chunk increase could not be promoted, so if short requests are unaffected by owner size there is nothing to preserve and **the simpler single-owner configuration is AT LEAST AS GOOD as the two-owner policy** - without the extra 1.555 GiB, without the borrow/restore path, and without a selector needing qualification. **RT-1's promotion therefore does NOT need a shape policy; what remains is the memory-admission question for a single larger owner, a capacity question rather than a policy one.** Limits: one short case and one long case only; the 1.06% is inside the noise so it supports "no measurable benefit" and nothing stronger (the short arm's own spread, 1.99%, exceeds the effect); one host, one model, one quant, one profile; GTT is 120 GB here and the base owner peaks near 89 GB, so a lower-memory host could not prepare both owners at all; the selector was enabled only inside that process, no runtime default changed and no production wiring added. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-same-owner-selection/README.md`, `scripts/qwen4exp_rt1_same_owner_selection.py` **PROMOTED TO THE SERVING DEFAULT (`20260930T`): THE RECORDED BLOCKER IS FALSIFIED BY THE CODE IT WAS ABOUT.** `DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS` 256 -> 1024, so the serving/CLI path now runs the chunk size this row measured and the X-6 gate certifies. **The blocker above says** "`max_prefill_chunk_tokens` ... is a **C4 scheduler knob bounding per-tick prefill work so decode is not starved** ... Raising it 4x makes each prefill tick up to 4x longer". **That premise is wrong.** `_run_token_budget_round` bounds per-tick prefill work with `round_prefill_token_budget` and only splits that already-bounded budget into chunks of `min(prefill_chunk_size, prefill_budget)`: at 256 the engine processed 1024 budgeted tokens as **4x256**, at 1024 it processes the same 1024 as **1x1024**. **Per-tick work is identical, so there is no tick to lengthen** - a cap below the round budget cannot make the engine fairer, it can only multiply per-chunk overhead, which is the whole of the measured win. Corroborating and independent: `ResidentEngineLoop.__init__`'s own `prefill_chunk_size` default was **already 1024**; only `EngineLoopConfig.max_prefill_chunk_tokens` was pulling it down to 256, so the two knobs were inconsistent and this aligns them. **Numerics: chunk 1024 IS the certified configuration** - the X-6 certification is at chunk 1024 and hashes bit-identically at 4096 - so the promotion lands on what the gate certifies. **Cost +1.24 GB at 4K**; the workspace is `min(chunk, max_seq_len)`, so p512 resolves to the same 768 rows either way and short requests are unaffected in time and memory. **SCOPE - what this does NOT claim:** the -29.0% to -35.7% is a **prefill-harness** measurement (`qwen4exp_profile_gap.py --mode prefill`, 12 cases, 3 reps/arm, one process per arm), **not** a decode-latency-under-concurrency result, and **no c2/c8 measurement was taken**. A serving-path A/B was **attempted and could not run here on either backend**: `gguf_live_server_bench.py` requires `--model` to be a single file so the 4-shard directory cannot load, `hip_gfx1100` died with `HIP error 98: invalid device function` even without the cached-build gate, and `hip_gfx1151` (the backend `QWEN4_EXP_BACKEND` names) timed out waiting for the engine service - so **no serving-path timing is claimed anywhere**. `token_id` is identical to 256 on 12/12 but `logits_sha256` differs on 12/12, so this is an **arithmetic change against the old default**, not a pure scheduling one; it is admissible because 1024 is the gated configuration, not because it is bit-identical to 256. Pinned by `tests/test_unit_engine_loop_prefill_chunk.py` (cap must equal the round budget, per-round token count unchanged by the cap, strictly less fragmentation) plus the updated `test_engine_loop_cli_env_defaults_match_docs`; 432 tests green across the affected bundle. **RT-3's shape policy is not needed for this** - a two-owner policy measured 1.06% *slower* at 0.017x the within-arm spread - so what remains for a larger chunk is memory admission, not policy. Evidence: `worklog/entries/20260930T011826.957349Z-lhl-qwen4exp-rt1-serving-chunk-default-3c5235.md`, `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-256-vs-1024/README.md` **THE CERTIFICATE THIS PROMOTION RESTS ON IS NOW STALE (`20260930T`), and the promotion itself is NOT invalidated by that.** The promotion cites "admissible because chunk 1024 is what the X-6 numerical gate certifies", and X-6's certification no longer reproduces at HEAD - at either chunk size (see X-6's last segment). Two things this does and does not change. **Does not change the promotion's own result:** RT-1 measured a WALL change and a chunk-invariance property, and the invariance half is independently RE-CONFIRMED at HEAD (chunk 1024 and 4096 bit-identical on both hashes and all five KL metrics), so `DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS 256 -> 1024` still moves the serving default onto a chunk size whose arithmetic is bit-identical to the one the gate certified. **Does change the route's admission:** the `wmma` 16-47 dense-prefill route is the production default for the wide dense-prefill shapes and its recorded numerical certificate - mean KL 3.796e-4, `measurement_valid: true` - is the thing that admits it; at HEAD the same configuration returns mean KL 4.388e-4 with one row over the 0.02 review boundary and `measurement_valid: false`. All hard gates still pass with 2.28x / 2.14x / 1.84x headroom, so this is a review requirement, not a demonstrated regression. **The open action is a re-gate of the route at HEAD (and a bisect of `035185fb2..HEAD` for which production-default change moved the shared baseline), not a revert of RT-1.** **The chunk-4096 half of this row is not numerically blocked.** `artifact-wmma16-47-chunk4096.json` runs the full canonical 12-case / 4-category protocol (1548 rows, 128 steps, 3 repeats, chunk 4096, determinism passed) and is **bit-identical to the chunk-1024 run on both hashes** (`294e6c08d5b2c315` / `10fd0ae50b6eb9ce`), on every KL moment, on top-1 (0.994186) and on the one over-review row - the same `general_en-p512` step 469. So chunk 4096 is screen-only for exactly the reason chunk 1024 is, and it introduces no numerical cost of its own. That satisfies this row's stated limit ("ONE case in ONE category - the 12-case 4-category suite run is the evidence that would generalize") and is stronger than the limit asked for: 4096 is not merely admissible across all four categories, it is indistinguishable from 1024. What remains for the promotion is the pre-existing outlier review above and the +4.96 GB memory admission, not numerics. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk4096-gate/` **CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN REMAINING-BLOCKER STATEMENT (`20261003T`): THE PER-TICK PREFILL BOUND IS THE REAL BLOCKER, NOT MEMORY ADMISSION - AND THE 4096 SEGMENT ABOVE DROPPED IT.** The paragraph above reduces the 4096 step to a capacity question - "a single memory-admission question: can the host afford a 89.40 GB peak" - and that is not what gates it. **The executed chunk depends on the scheduler policy, and the DEFAULT policy is not bounded by the round budget.** `EngineLoopConfig.prefill_decode_policy` defaults to **`protect_decode`** (`hipengine/generation/engine_loop.py:101`, `:1883`, `:1832`), and on that path one round runs exactly one prefill chunk of `self.prefill_chunk_size` (= `max_prefill_chunk_tokens`) via `next_prefill_work(chunk_size=self.prefill_chunk_size)` (`:2245`), with no round-budget cap applied. So **raising the cap to 4096 DOES raise the executed chunk to 4096 on the default path, and because one round is one chunk, per-tick prefill work quadruples** - as does the worst-case delay before a concurrently decoding request gets its next step. The `min(prefill_chunk_size, round_prefill_token_budget)` split (`_run_token_budget_round`, `:2255`/`:2266`) applies **only** under `prefill_decode_policy == "token_budget"`, where the cap alone is indeed inert and the round budget must rise with it - which lengthens the tick for the same reason. `tests/test_unit_engine_loop_prefill_chunk.py:26` pins `DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS == DEFAULT_ROUND_PREFILL_TOKEN_BUDGET` as a cross-policy consistency pin, **not** as a statement that the round budget bounds the default path. **Either policy reaches 4096 only by quadrupling per-tick prefill work**, which is exactly the objection this row already raised in its 256 -> 1024 segment and then dropped from its 4096 segment. **Two consequences follow.** (1) **A larger workspace owner cannot deliver the -4.94%.** This row's "concrete next step if pursued: memory-bounded chunk selection" and REFACTOR's "the one genuinely useful survivor is the capacity question (what peak can the host admit for a single larger owner)" both assume owner admission is the lever; this row's own `RT-3` measurement refutes it - the workspace is **SCRATCH** and kernel work scales with the ACTUAL TOKEN COUNT, not the size of the scratch handed to it (two-owner A/B, 1024 vs 4096 rows against a 4352 sequence: 3.2068 s vs 3.1730 s, 1.06% SLOWER on the small owner at 0.017x the within-arm spread, identical `logits_sha256`). The owner does not set the chunk at all - the cap does - so a 4096-row owner with a 1024 cap still executes 4x1024, which is the measured-slow configuration, and the selector that would pick owners is already parked for removal for exactly that reason. (2) **1024 -> 4096 is a REAL fairness trade; 256 -> 1024 was not.** The landed 256 -> 1024 promotion is admissible precisely because per-tick work was argued unchanged (1024 budgeted tokens as 4x256 vs 1x1024 - "there is no tick to lengthen"), and **that argument does not transfer**: reaching a 4096 chunk - by the cap on the default path, or by the round budget under `token_budget` - quadruples per-tick prefill work and therefore the worst-case delay before a concurrently decoding request gets its next step. This row already states the requirement correctly in its 256 -> 1024 blocker segment ("Raising it 4x makes each prefill tick up to 4x longer ... DECODE LATENCY UNDER CONCURRENCY ... is unmeasured, and it is the prerequisite"); the 4096 segment above dropped it. **The -4.94% itself is NOT retracted**: `qwen4exp_profile_gap.py:717` calls `runner.prefill(ids)` directly and therefore bypasses `_run_token_budget_round` entirely, so it is a valid direct-prefill/batch-path result and this row's "a prefill-only or batch path is directly supported" is its correct scope - it is simply not reachable through the engine loop without paying the tick cost. **The missing experiment is therefore NOT a capacity probe but a decode-interleaving / peer-fairness measurement at a 4096 prefill chunk** (on the default policy: raise `max_prefill_chunk_tokens`; under `token_budget`: raise `round_prefill_token_budget` with it) against 1024, same host and model, a decoding peer plus a long prefill - which is the same "no peer-fairness measurement" gap `RT-3` already lists. Code-reading finding, not a measurement; no code, default, flag, test or artifact changed. Evidence: `worklog/entries/20261003T152724.639601Z-lhl-rt-1-chunk-4096-is-gated-on-the-round-prefill-bu-3e7462.md`, corrected by `worklog/entries/20261003T152924.135144Z-lhl-correction-the-round-budget-does-not-bound-the-d-67c4d6.md` **THE PEER-FAIRNESS OBJECTION IS FALSIFIED BY MEASUREMENT (`20261004T`): RAISING THE CAP 1024 -> 4096 COSTS THE DECODING PEER NOTHING.** The segment above makes the missing experiment a decode-interleaving / peer-fairness measurement and predicts that quadrupling the executed chunk "quadruples the worst-case delay before a concurrently decoding request gets its next step". **Measured on the default policy (`protect_decode`), gfx1151 APU, UD-Q4_K_XL, production profile, cached build, `max_sequence_length=4097` per RT-3's protocol, two rows on one engine loop (a 4096-token `code-p4096` row and a decoding peer): the prediction does not hold.** **A) A continuously decoding peer (400 tokens, ~20 s) with the long prefill submitted mid-stream**: peer ITL median **49.662 -> 49.608 ms (-0.11%)**, ITL max **54.978 -> 54.753 ms (-0.41%)**, prefill wall **35.268 -> 35.250 s (-0.05%)**. **B) A second request queued behind the long prefill before the first tick** (both arms comparable because the loop's own FIFO decides): peer TTFT **16.860 -> 16.841 s (-0.11%)**, prefill wall **15.999 -> 16.012 s (+0.09%)**. **Engagement is recorded from inside the run, not inferred**: `loop_prefill_batch_chunks` goes `[1024, 1024, 1024, 1024, 16]` -> `[4096, 16]` and the call count 5 -> 2, so the cap did reach the scheduler and did change the tick decomposition 4x - the exact change the mechanism predicted would cost fairness. **WHY THE MECHANISM IS WRONG, not merely unmeasured**: `protect_decode` protects decode by **priority**, not by bounding the tick - while a decode row is due the loop schedules decode, so a continuously decoding peer is never interrupted and instead **starves the prefill** (B's prefill did not start until the peer's 400 tokens finished at +19.4 s; the 35.3 s prefill wall against ~16 s solo is the prefill absorbing that contention). And `Qwen4ExpResidentServingRunner.prefill_batch` hands the runner the **whole prompt** once the last slice arrives (`row.runner.prefill(row.prompt_ids)`), so the executed GPU prefill is one 4096-token call at either cap - the cap changes how many loop ticks accumulate it, not how much work any tick does. **So the cap is a scheduling knob that does not change the executed chunk.** **AND THE GENERATOR CHUNK IS THE KNOB THAT DOES - MEASURED IN THE SAME PACKET (`20261004T`): RT-1's WIN IS REACHABLE THROUGH THE ENGINE LOOP AT -5.74% / -5.80%, AND THE PEER BENEFITS RATHER THAN BEING HARMED.** Same protocol, same `--order queued`, **two independent repetitions per arm, one process each**: `g1024_l1024` prefill wall **15.992 / 15.998 s** with workspace `[1024]` and `prefill_token_ids` 8,192 B, against `g4096_l1024` **15.075 / 15.070 s (-5.74% / -5.80%)** with workspace `[4096]` and **32,768 B**, and `g4096_l4096` **15.001 / 15.072 s**. **Separation is far beyond this row's own bar:** within-arm spread is **0.005 s (0.03%)** for both arms, the arms **do not overlap** (slowest `g4096_l1024` 15.075 s is 5.74% faster than fastest `g1024_l1024` 15.992 s), and the median delta is **183x** the larger within-arm spread against the 5.70x required for the original claim. **The cap adds nothing on top of the chunk:** at repetition 2, `g4096_l4096` (-5.79%) and `g4096_l1024` (-5.80%) are indistinguishable and `g4096_l4096` is the *more* variable arm (0.071 s vs 0.005 s spread). Engagement is observed, not inferred: `_prefill_workspaces[*].prefill_chunk_size` `[1024]` -> `[4096]`, `prefill_token_ids.nbytes` 8,192 -> 32,768, traced session prefill `prefill_chunk_size` 1024 -> 4096. **The peer's TTFT improves 6.43% / 6.44%** (16.854 / 16.864 -> 15.770 / 15.777 s) because it queues behind a shorter prefill, its **ITL median is unchanged** (49.19 / 49.24 -> 49.09 / 49.15 ms, -0.2%), and its **worst single gap roughly halves in both repetitions** (147.9 / 155.5 -> 75.1 / 71.9 ms). So the -4.94% is **not** confined to the direct-prefill/batch path, and the workspace route carries no peer-fairness cost. **THE REAL BINDING POINT, and the earlier failure in this packet was a harness defect rather than a property of the engine:** patching `Qwen4ExpGGUFTextGenerator.__init__` alone does not hold the chunk, because `LLM` reaches the generator through the registry factory which sets `_implicit_profile_chunk_size = prefill_chunk_size is None` (True whenever no chunk is passed, which is always on this path) and `qwen4_exp_profiles._bind_default_chunk` then runs *after* construction and resets `generator.runner.prefill_chunk_size` to 1024 - silently reverting the override while the allocated workspace stayed at 1024 rows, which is the `copy size 32768 exceeds device buffer size 8192` failure. Neutralising `_bind_default_chunk` makes the chunk survive to the workspace allocation. **That constant is where a production promotion must move.** **Still open, and deliberately NOT argued here: the +4.96 GB peak the 4096-row workspace costs on p4096** (RT-1/RT-3). This segment establishes the win is real, reachable and free of peer-fairness cost; it does not make the admission case, and the task that produced it forbids promoting on one. **THE ADMISSION CASE IS NOW MEASURED, AND IT IS A REAL COST BUT NOT A BLOCKER (`20261004T`; the first revision of this segment called it a blocker and that OVERSTATED the evidence - see the correction below): THE WORKSPACE IS SIZED BY THE MODEL'S NATIVE CONTEXT, NOT THE PROMPT, SO AT c2 CHUNK 4096 COSTS +10.63 GiB (+11.02%) AND ROUGHLY HALVES THE ABSOLUTE HEADROOM.** `Qwen4ExpPrefillWorkspace.allocate_inputs` sizes every prefill input buffer as `rows = min(prefill_chunk_size, max_sequence_length)`, and that `max_sequence_length` is the **runner's** - set from the model's native context in `__init__`, where the base workspace is allocated, **before any request-level context applies**. This row and RT-3 measured at `max_sequence_length = max(768, prompt_tokens + 1)` (their harness tied context to prompt length), which is why RT-3 concluded the workspace "is already shape-aware" and short requests "byte-for-byte identical in time and memory". **It is context-aware, not prompt-aware.** `scripts/qwen4exp_chunk_memory_probe.py` (Tier-1 allocation probe, no full prefill, clean tracked revision, cached build, one process per arm, ~52 s each, native context 262144, GTT 120 GiB): **c1 86.64 -> 91.95 GiB peak (+5.31 GiB, +6.13%; free 33.36 -> 28.05 GiB)** and **c2 96.42 -> 107.05 GiB peak (+10.63 GiB, +11.02%; free 23.58 -> 12.95 GiB, 19.7% -> 10.8%)**. The delta is entirely scratch - `explicit_components_bytes` moves only 99,739,496,800 -> 99,802,411,360 at c2. **The cost is that the promotion roughly halves the absolute headroom at c2 native context; it does NOT exhaust it, and no OOM was produced. Calling it a blocker overstated the evidence.** **Correction to this segment's first revision:** it reported "leaves 84-168 MB of scratch margin" as the blocker. That figure is the probe's ALLOWANCE margin, `declared_allowance - observed_scratch`, which is a budget-saturation signal rather than host headroom - the observed scratch does saturate its declared allowance (240 MiB unused of 14.33 GiB at chunk 4096, against 4578 MiB of 8.00 GiB at chunk 1024, constant across context), but that is not an admission number. The admission number is peak vs GTT, above. **Corroborated independently in this packet's own fairness runs**, where `LLM.prepare()` was passed `max_sequence_length=4097` and the session still reported **262144**: `prefill_token_ids` (rows x 8) read 8,192 B at chunk 1024 and 32,768 B at chunk 4096. **So a blanket production chunk-4096 is a real reduction in headroom for every deployment rather than a cost borne only by long prompts - but it is NOT the measured blocker this segment first claimed**, and it does **NOT** retract the speed win, which stands at -5.74% / -5.80% through the engine loop with no peer-fairness cost. **Together they are the case for the capacity guard this row already named, and the context sweep now gives that guard its envelope:** because the workspace is `min(chunk, context)`, the workspace delta is constant at 3072 rows for any context above 4096, so the lever is that a smaller context frees KV memory - c2 chunk 4096 peaks at **98.14 GiB / 21.86 GiB free at 131072**, **93.68 / 26.32 at 65536**, **91.45 / 28.55 at 32768**, so **at 32K context chunk 4096 leaves MORE absolute headroom (28.55 GiB) than chunk 1024 does at native context (23.58 GiB)**. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-native-context-chunk-memory/README.md`, `worklog/entries/20261003T193019.939334Z-lhl-qwen4exp-native-context-chunk-memory-7e9073.md` **DECODE SIDE NOW MEASURED (`20261004T`): the promoted chunk-1024 default costs 1.15% decode and buys 9.06% prefill.** `RT-1` was promoted and qualified on prefill-only evidence, as its own state cell and the scoreboard's chunk section both say. A/B/A/B/A/B in one session at `code-p4096`, 150 s cooldowns, 1 warmup + 3 repetitions, one process per arm: chunk 512 **249.76 pp / 18.120 tg** (within-arm spread 0.051% / 0.127%) against chunk 1024 **272.38 pp / 17.911 tg** (0.433% / 0.479%), every arm fully separated on both columns. **The decode cost is eight times smaller than the prefill benefit, so the promotion stands on both axes.** The same comparison run sequentially reads -7.1% decode, because a non-interleaved `code-p4096` A/B on this host drifts **8%** - interleaving takes within-arm repeatability from 8.0% to 0.05-0.48% and is mandatory at this shape. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-chunk-decode-trade/`, worklog `20261004T100141`. | **THE 1024 -> 4096 STEP IS NOW TAKEN, THROUGH A GUARD RATHER THAN A BLANKET FLIP (`20261004T`).** RT-1 recorded "Not promoted. No default changed. Two concrete blockers": (1) re-run the numerical gate at the new chunk size, and (2) RT-3's short-request concern, with the right form being "a shape-based chunk policy". **Both are now settled.** (1) was already closed by this row's own `X-6` segment - the gate at chunk 4096 reproduces the chunk-1024 certification bit-identically, logits hashes included. (2) was closed by RT-3's `20261004T` segment, which found the short-request cost was a harness artifact and that the right lever is a **capacity guard**; that guard is now implemented and is what takes the larger chunk. **The chunk-1024 gate also reproduces independently in this unit:** the layer2 gate with `--candidate production_baseline --prefill-chunk-size 4096` gives mean KL 3.039e-4 (gate 1e-3), p95 1.785e-3, p99 4.271e-3 (2e-2), max 7.771e-3 (5e-2), top-1 0.99556 (448/450, zero mismatches outside the flip-eligible set), `hard_gates_passed: true`, `scope_failures: []` - every figure identical to the recorded production@1024 gate, and token ids bit-identical across strict and production at both chunk sizes (6 samples per arm). **The speed is larger on the canonical bench than RT-1's prefill-mode figure:** +8.43% prefill and +1.68% decode at `code-p4096` against RT-1's -4.94% prefill-only. Different harnesses, same direction; the canonical figure is the one the production path is measured with. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-prefill-chunk-4096/`.

No per-start timing-selected arithmetic; re-gate each dispatched scope | A | PREMISE FALSIFIED — the trade is memory-only **PREMISE FALSIFIED; THE TRADE IS MEMORY-ONLY (`20260929T`).** RT-3's rationale is "the September 15 4096 option improved 4K but **short-request costs kept 1024 default**". Measured: that short-request cost is **not reproducible today**. **Mechanism:** scratch is sized `min(chunk_size, max_sequence_length)` and the harness sets `max_sequence_length = max(768, prompt_tokens+1)`, so the chunk setting changes nothing until the prompt exceeds the chunk: p512 -> 768 vs 768 rows (**identical**); p1024 -> 1024 vs 1025 (**+1 row**); p4096 -> 1024 vs 4096 (**4x**). **Measured** (`code-p1024`, 3 reps/arm, production, cached, one process per arm, chunk the only difference): chunk 1024 **3.7671 s** / 84,337,158,344 B vs chunk 4096 **3.8196 s** / 84,338,566,196 B = **+0.0525 s (+1.39%)** and **+1,407,852 B (+0.00167%)**; `token_id` 248068 and `logits_sha256` **identical**, `fell_back_to_strict=False`. **The +1.39% is NOT an effect**: the within-arm ranges are 0.0735 s and 0.0748 s, so the median gap is SMALLER than either range and the arms overlap - reported as indistinguishable. **Across all three measured points**: p512 indistinguishable wall / **byte-identical** allocation; p1024 indistinguishable wall / **+1.34 MiB**; p4096 **-4.94%** wall / **+4.96 GB**. The transition is sharp and lands exactly where the model predicts. **Conclusion: the shape-based TIME policy the row asks for is not the right form** - there is no time/memory tradeoff below the chunk size because nothing changes there, and a blanket chunk 4096 costs short requests **nothing measurable**. The lever is a **capacity guard**: take the larger chunk when the allocation is admissible, fall back when not - simpler than a length-bucketed policy and what the evidence supports. **THIS PACKET'S OWN PREMISE IS CORRECTED (`20261004T`): THE WORKSPACE IS CONTEXT-AWARE, NOT PROMPT-AWARE, SO "SHORT REQUESTS ARE UNAFFECTED IN MEMORY" IS A HARNESS ARTIFACT.** The p512 byte-identical result held because this harness set `max_sequence_length = max(768, prompt_tokens + 1)`, tying context to prompt length; `allocate_inputs` sizes rows as `min(prefill_chunk_size, max_sequence_length)` where that `max_sequence_length` is the **runner's**, fixed from the model's native context at construction. In a real serving session the context is native 262144, so `rows = min(chunk, 262144) = chunk` for **every** session and the larger chunk is paid at session construction regardless of traffic. Measured (Tier-1 probe, native context, GTT 120 GiB): c1 **86.64 -> 91.95 GiB peak (+5.31 GiB)** and c2 **96.42 -> 107.05 GiB (+10.63 GiB, +11.02%)**, free falling **23.58 -> 12.95 GiB (19.7% -> 10.8%)**. **So the capacity guard this row recommends is still the right lever, and the context sweep gives it an operating envelope** - c2 chunk 4096 at 32K context leaves 28.55 GiB free, more than chunk 1024 does at native context. **But the cost is not the blocker this row's sibling segment first called it**: 12.95 GiB free is not an OOM, and no failure was produced. See RT-1's `20261004T` segment and `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-native-context-chunk-memory/README.md`. **NOT done (real gaps):** no 2051/2052 case measured (the canonical fixture has p512/p1024/p4096 only, so the exact boundary the row names is a PREDICTION not a measurement); no separate repair-queue / F16-workspace / c2-peak accounting (only aggregate peak); no peer-fairness measurement (**now measured - see `RT-1`'s `20261004T` segment: raising the cap 1024 -> 4096 leaves peer ITL at 49.6 ms median and peer TTFT unchanged, both within 0.11%**); no ragged-final-chunk exercise; **no policy implemented and nothing promoted** (default unchanged); one prompt family, one host. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt3-chunk-inert/README.md` **STRENGTHENED BY A DIRECT A/B ON GENUINELY DIFFERENT OWNERS (`20260929T`): THE SHAPE POLICY HAS NO BENEFIT EVEN WHERE THE OWNERS DO DIFFER.** The finding above shows the short-request cost is not reproducible, but at p512 the two chunk sizes resolved to the SAME `min(chunk, max_seq_len)` (768 rows), so that measurement could not separate "the policy is unnecessary" from "the two arms were identical". **A two-owner A/B separates them**: one runner, two prepared owners whose row counts genuinely differ (**1024 vs 4096 rows against a 4352 sequence**), the selection path exercised end to end, and the same short prompt measured with the selector ON (served by the 1024 owner) and OFF (forced onto the 4096 owner). Result: **3.2068 s vs 3.1730 s = 1.06% SLOWER on the small owner, at 0.017x the within-arm spread**, with **identical `logits_sha256`** - no measurable benefit either way. **Mechanism, stated directly**: the workspace is **SCRATCH**; kernel work scales with the ACTUAL TOKEN COUNT, not with the size of the scratch handed to it, so a 512-token prefill does 512 tokens of work whichever owner supplies the buffers. This is the same conclusion as the `min(chunk, max_seq_len)` observation but established where the owners are NOT identical, so it is the stronger evidence and the two are independent. The extra owner also costs **1.555 GiB**. **So the length/capacity-bucketed policy this row asks for is not merely the wrong form for TIME - it is unnecessary**: a single larger owner is at least as good, and the only real lever remains the capacity guard named above. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-same-owner-selection/README.md`. | **THE CAPACITY GUARD IS IMPLEMENTED AND PROMOTED (`20261004T`) - the row's own named lever, which had been measured three times and never built.** RT-3's last recorded state was "the lever is a capacity guard: take the larger chunk when the allocation is admissible, fall back when not" with "no policy implemented and nothing promoted (default unchanged)". It is now built: `hipengine/generation/qwen4_exp_gguf.py` gains `_select_prefill_chunk` over `_AUTO_PREFILL_CHUNK_LADDER = (4096, 2048, 1024)`, the qualified production factory passes `None` when the caller gives no chunk, the constructor records the choice as `admitted_prefill_chunk_size`, and `_bind_default_chunk` binds that value so the pre-binding allocation and the bound runner chunk agree. **Both callbacks are pure arithmetic on the residency plan, so the whole ladder is evaluated before any weight is materialized** - a fallback costs no device memory and no time. Verified end to end through `resolve_runtime_profile(...).construct_generator(...)` with the real model: `admitted=4096`, `runner_chunk=4096`, `context=262144`, `profile=production`. **Two new measurements the row did not have:** (1) the step is not a prefill-for-decode trade - `code-p4096` production, 6 samples/arm, interleaved A/B/A/B gives chunk 4096 against 1024 as **+8.43% prefill AND +1.68% decode** (279.28 -> 302.81 pp, 18.127 -> 18.431 tg, spreads <=1.13%), where the earlier 1024-against-512 trade recorded -1.15% decode and so set the expected direction; (2) **chunk 2048 is free** - the scratch reservation is `max(4 GiB per-runner floor, mandatory + wide-f16 staging)`, so 2048 sits under the floor at an unchanged 8.59 GB and 4096 crosses it at 15.39 GB (+6.80 GB), which is why 2048 is the correct fallback rung rather than a dead intermediate. Max context is 262144 at every rung. A same-session asymmetry worth keeping: in the **strict** profile chunk 4096 is *slower* (70.56 vs 72.35 tok/s, -2.48%), so the gain belongs to the production tile path, whose efficiency grows with the row batch, and is not a property of chunking as such. Advance only with exact visibility/ownership and complete-request benefit | A | ATTRIBUTED — the KV reset is 29.182 ms at 128K and 99.6% wasted; 220 GB/s is the hardware memset rate **ATTRIBUTED (`20260929T`).** `scripts/qwen4exp_kv_reset_cost.py` times exactly the K/V memset pair that dominates `Qwen4ExpGGUFResidentModelRunner.reset`, over a capacity ladder, without loading the model. Per-token footprint **24,576 B** (12 QSA layers x 2 kv heads x 256 key length x 2 x 2B bf16), read from the GGUF metadata. Result (median of 7, sync both sides): 1024 -> 48.0 MiB / **0.238 ms**; 4096 -> 192.0 MiB / **0.885 ms**; 16384 -> 768.0 MiB / **3.709 ms**; 65536 -> 3,072 MiB / **14.619 ms**; 131072 -> 6,144 MiB / **29.182 ms**. Wasted bytes for a 512-token request: 50.0% / 87.5% / 96.9% / 99.2% / **99.6%**. **Two findings.** (1) **It is already bandwidth-bound, so the memset has no headroom**: effective bandwidth is flat at 211-228 GB/s across a 128x size range, ~86% of this APU's ~256 GB/s LPDDR5X peak - no memset tuning makes it meaningfully faster, the ONLY lever is clearing fewer bytes. (2) **The waste is nearly total at realistic capacities**: a 512-token request at 128K capacity pays 29.182 ms to clear 6 GiB of which it wrote 24 MiB (0.4%); even at 16K the waste is 96.9%. **Establishes** the cost, its scaling, bandwidth and waste fraction as measured rather than estimated. **Does NOT establish a saving**: this measures the current contract and makes no claim about an alternative; clearing only written ranges or validated overwrite-before-read must still satisfy exact visibility/ownership, preserved recurrent initialization, poison-tested unused storage, and correct shorter-after-longer / sparse-tail / rollback / cancellation / c2 behavior. Not end-to-end (`performance_claim: false`); boundary excludes the recurrent zero, index resets and position writes. The historical 0.24-0.74% is a complete-request percentage at some other capacity and is NOT restated here as a millisecond figure. Limits: one host, one model's 24,576 B/token footprint, memset only. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-kv-reset-cost/README.md` **THE CHEAPER ALTERNATIVE IS RULED OUT: 220 GB/s IS THE HARDWARE MEMSET RATE (`20260929T`).** RT-4's attribution left two possible fixes - make the **memset** faster (no contract change, no correctness burden), or clear **less** (this row's proposed contract change with its poison/short-after-long/sparse-tail/rollback/cancellation/c2 burden). **Route 1 is now closed by measurement.** `hipMemset` over a capacity ladder (7 reps after 2 warmups, device-synchronized): 64 MiB **218.3 GB/s**, 768 MiB **220.1**, **6 GiB 218.7**, 12 GiB **220.1** - **flat across a 12,000x size range**, with the only deviation at 1 MiB (96.2 GB/s) where launch overhead dominates and the rate is LOWER. The 6 GiB point is the same volume this row clears at 128K and reproduces its recorded **220.77 GB/s to within 0.3%**; the best measured rate is **0.997x** it. **So 220 GB/s is this part's achievable `hipMemset` rate, not a property of how the reset calls it, and there is no memset-speed headroom.** **What this settles: clearing less is the ONLY lever**, so this row's correctness burden is not avoidable by a cheaper alternative and the remaining work is what the row already says it is. It also makes **29.182 ms at 128K a FLOOR for the full-clear design**, not an artifact of a suboptimal call - any future "the reset should be faster" claim needs a different mechanism than a faster memset. **Limits**: one part (a different memory subsystem would differ); `hipMemset` only, so a custom kernel, a different memset implementation or a DMA path was NOT tested and the claim is precisely "the `hipMemset` the runner uses is at this part's achievable rate", not "no memset-like operation can exceed 220 GB/s"; the reset path was not timed end to end, so this row's boundary stands; allocated buffers, no model loaded. No runtime change, no promotion, and no claim about the contract change's value. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-kv-reset-memset-bandwidth/README.md` **IMPLEMENTED AND MEASURED, DEFAULT-OFF PENDING THE EXACTNESS GATE (`20260930T`) - and the row's recorded 6,144 MiB / 29.182 ms at 128K is 2x TOO LARGE.** `reset()` now has a bounded path: `Qwen4ExpDenseAttentionState.written_cache_bytes()` returns `round_up(position, block_size) x bytes_per_position` from the HOST-resident `position_host` (no device read), and `clear_written_cache()` memsets only `[0, bound)`. **Soundness is why this is T1 and not a reassociation:** `allocate()` memsets the whole cache and KV writes only ever land below `position` (the block table is the identity `arange(blocks)` and is never mutated), so every byte at or above the bound is ALREADY ZERO and clearing it is pure waste. **Measured at native capacity** (`scripts/qwen4exp_de5_reset_split.py --capacity 131072`, `code-p1024`, 64 decode steps, 12 attention states, `unsloth-Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-UD-Q4_K_XL` on the W7900): reset **15.689 / 15.519 ms -> 1.133 / 1.741 ms**, i.e. **14.17 ms saved per request, 11.0x**, leaving only `state_zero` (0.55 ms) and the index resets (0.02-0.04 ms). **Decode is unaffected in both regimes** (first-eighth 192.26/143.87 vs 194.99/143.81; last-eighth 51.80/49.66 vs 51.98/49.73), the expected signature of skipping only already-zero bytes. **The traffic model is independently validated:** the probe reports `kv_bytes_total` **3,221,225,472 B (3,072 MiB)** over 12 key + 12 value caches of 134,217,728 B each and predicts 14.64 ms at 220 GB/s; the measured full clear is 15.17-15.33 ms, within 5%. **THE 2x CORRECTION:** the row's own footprint formula (`12 QSA layers x 2 kv heads x 256 key length x 2 x 2B bf16` = 24,576 B/token) gives `131072 x 24,576 = 3,221,225,472 B = 3,072 MiB`, NOT 6,144 MiB - and this probe reaches the same 3,072 MiB from the allocated buffers with no reference to the row. Halving the row's 29.182 ms gives **14.59 ms**, matching this run's 14.64 ms prediction. **The row's per-token footprint, its 220 GB/s rate and its 99.6% waste fraction all stand unchanged**; only the absolute byte and millisecond figures were doubled. **Unit evidence:** `tests/test_gpu_qwen4exp_kv_reset_bounded.py`, 5 tests, written RED first (`AttributeError`) then GREEN, including the poison test (proves the clear is BOUNDED, not a renamed full memset) and the shorter-after-longer stale-KV test the row names explicitly. **EXACTNESS PROVEN AND PROMOTED TO THE DEFAULT (`20260930T`).** The binding evidence is PARENT PARITY, not the strict comparison: `scripts/qwen4exp_layer2_profile_gate.py` run twice, `production_baseline` (no flag) and `bounded_kv_reset` (flag on), **both under the production profile**, so the flag is the only variable. They produce an **identical `candidate_logits_sha256` (`260979d6...`)** - and both runs produce an **identical `strict_logits_sha256` (`e5ae994e...`)**, which is the control showing the harness itself is reproducible, so the match is evidence rather than coincidence. Both are `measurement_valid = true`. **A single earlier run comparing the candidate against the STRICT arm showed differing logits hashes; that is the gate's intended strict-vs-production arithmetic difference and says NOTHING about this flag** - the strict arm runs under the registered strict profile while candidates run under production, so the strict-vs-candidate comparison is confounded for a T1 candidate by construction. **Independently confirmed at the byte level:** `scripts/qwen4exp_rt4_kv_compare.py` reads every K/V cache back to host and diffs the raw buffers - **byte-identical at all 12 attention states across three arms (flag off, off, on)**, with the flag-off arm also matching across separate processes. The invariant `last_nonzero < bound` holds at every state (**1,064,959 vs 1,310,720**), i.e. the highest written byte sits exactly at `position` and the bound clears a further 240 blocks of margin. **A phantom difference in an earlier arm comparison was a race in my own probe, not the engine:** `_read` called `copy_device_to_host` before `device_synchronize()`, so a copy issued while the last decode step was in flight captured partially-written state (lower non-zero count, different hash, no arithmetic change). Moving the sync before the copy made the outlier vanish and the arms byte-identical. **PROMOTED:** `_qwen4_exp_bounded_kv_reset_enabled()` now defaults ON; `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_BOUNDED_KV_RESET=0` restores the full-capacity clear for rollback or bisection. The unit file is 6 tests (5 bound tests plus a flag-contract test locking default-on with explicit falsy opt-out). `docs/REFACTOR.md` carries the removal condition. **Also not exercised:** rollback, cancellation and c2 - the bound is re-derived from `position` on each prefill, which is an argument, not a measurement. One host, one model, one quant, one case, one capacity. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-rt4-bounded-kv-reset/README.md` |

## 5. Decode open work, by functional block

**The budget every row below is spending (`20261004T`).** The canonical bench's
decode wall is **47.656 ms/step at p512 and 57.694 at p4096**, of which the
profiled kernel sum is **46.1927 and 47.1298 (+2.0%)**. So the wall's growth
across 8x more context is **10.04 ms/step (+21%)** while device kernels grow
0.94 ms/step, and the **non-kernel remainder grows 1.47 -> 10.56 ms/step (7x)**.
The remainder is host-side and the step is `max(host enqueue, device work)` with
the two within ~1% of each other, so **a host-only or device-only win is capped
at roughly half its nominal saving**. See `DE-11`, which is where the decode
attribution now lives, and which withdraws the "decode is host-enqueue bound"
reading published earlier the same day. Rows DE-1 through DE-10 price **device
kernel families** and remain valid as such; none of them is the growing
remainder.

**Prerequisite: `X-4`.** The last split (`R6` in the historical performance
campaign, not `R06` in the journey inventory; September 9, p4096
fixed-live4097): device 49.3 ms/token, of which MoE 18.09 (gate/up 5.68,
down/combine 5.60, shared expert 6.19, router 0.33, quantize/cast 0.29),
linear 24.73 (attn_qkv 5.05, lm_head 3.25, attn_gate 3.12, ssm_out 3.10,
attn_q 1.98, GR down 3.63, GR up 2.08), GDN 2.44, QSA 2.69, GR epilogue 0.86.
The September 14 recovery then disabled `Q4_DP4A64` and the other decode
selectors (TG 18.1 → 11.4 at 4K before QSA ordered decode was restored), so the
split no longer describes the default. Target: gufo's 25.87 tok/s is 38.7 ms per
token against our ~56-61.

the row's 2.0x/5.8x figures belong to the warp256 variant, not the reverted logical128 dp4a. **Two distinct next units follow:** (1) re-run the profile gate on the decode dp4a logical128 owner under the current production envelope (a gate run, no kernel work); Neither is licensed by this analysis.  **Limits:** gate+up and down mappings are high confidence (kernel names carry both quant and role; 47 and 43 kernels/step over 48 layers match one per layer, the shortfall expected from this model's DYNAMIC quantization); the router-GEMV identification is medium confidence (rests on it being the only fp32 dense GEMV and on `ffn_gate_inp` being exactly fp32, not on a name match). **No timing claim** - the artifact inherits the source capture's own limit that its per-kernel times are diagnostic. **MECHANISM ANSWERED (`20260929T`): the gap is INSIDE the kernels, not between them.** `x4decode_marker_api_trace.csv` carries the `qwen4exp_decode_live4097_rep{0,1,2}` ROCTX ranges, so the kernel trace segments into exactly **1,771 dispatches per rep**, reproducing the attributed kernel sum: rep0 54.721 ms, rep1 54.671 ms, rep2 54.173 ms - a **1.0% spread** - while the step SPAN varies 2.8x (227.470 / 225.228 / 81.685 ms). **The per-family figures are sums of kernel DURATIONS** (the 30 families total exactly 54.218 ms), so **inter-kernel gaps are not in them**: the MoE block's 24.5504 ms is GPU-resident execution time, and **launch/tail overhead is EXCLUDED as the explanation** - the 2.77x gap is inside the kernels, which is what the row's structural lever targets. **Independent corroboration of the DE-5 correction:** stable kernel work under an unstable wall, with the median inter-kernel gap swinging 23x between reps (53.60 / 58.46 / **2.28** us; total gaps 172.749 / 170.558 / 27.512 ms) - a stable kernel sum under a varying wall is the signature of a harness wall that is not a serving-path number. **Limits:** the gap statistics describe THIS PROFILED HARNESS, not the engine's steady-state scheduling - a 53.60 us median inter-kernel gap is far above normal device-side latency and varies 23x across reps, so **no gap figure here is an engine property**; the conclusion drawn is only the negative one that gaps are not what the 24.5504 ms consists of. **PROMOTED (`20260929T`) - the decode dp4a logical128 linear is now the production default, and the row's own two follow-up units are answered for unit (1).** Unit (1) was 're-run the profile gate on the decode dp4a logical128 owner under the current production envelope (a gate run, no kernel work)'. **Gate run: PASS on every binding axis.** Numerical mean KL **2.44e-4** vs the 1e-3 gate, p99 3.67e-3 vs 2e-2, max **6.14e-3** vs 5e-2, top-1 **0.9911**; determinism 3/3 exact on all 18 prompts; isolation clean (both arms return to 0 bytes); task quality cross-validated 11/11 on the `code` category with 0 disagreements, and the four prose divergences are synonym-level or article-drops that leave the decisions verbatim identical. The only non-passing signal anywhere was strict generated-ID equality (`task_passed: false`), which `AGENTS.md` explicitly does not make the promotion bar and which is why the gate reports `requires_task_review` rather than `failed`. **This is the same row that recorded 'these are different arithmetic outcomes and must not be treated as one class': the logical128 dp4a passes at max KL 6.14e-3 while the warp256 dp4a variant failed at 0.121-0.431, so the re-admission is licensed on its own evidence, not by generalising the warp256 failure away.** **End-to-end, measured by per-kernel join rather than by wall.** The owner `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_silu_logical128_t64` falls **14.155 -> 6.908 ms/step (2.049x)** and the decode kernel sum falls **54.603 -> 47.359 ms/step, -13.27%**; the non-owner sum is **40.448 vs 40.451 ms** (0.007%), so the change is confined to its own kernel. The 2.049x reproduces the shape screen's 2.0x. Engagement is proven from the trace, not inferred: **0 dp4a dispatches in the baseline kernel trace and 210 in the candidate**, plus a differing warmup state hash (`3466313123...` vs `418c337476...`). **The end-to-end WALL is deliberately not claimed**: its within-arm spread (0.0134 s) exceeds the between-arm difference (0.0042 s) and the median moves +2.9% in the wrong direction, so the wall cannot resolve a ~7 ms/step change on a ~145 ms step that is mostly inter-kernel gap - consistent with this row's own finding that the kernel sum is stable to 1.0% while the step span varies 2.8x. **Post-promotion verification on a clean tree at `c66db0264`:** the default path with NO override binds `Q4_DP4A64=1`, runs the dp4a owner at **5.404 ms/step** with a 1.480 ms/step exact residual (43 of 48 layers are in `Q4_DP4A64_LAYERS`), reproduces the candidate arm's warmup state hash exactly, and totals **47.390 ms/step**. **Mechanism of the promotion:** `Q4_DP4A64` moved into `PRODUCTION_Q8_QSA_RESTORED_FLAGS`; the base profile already set it to `"1"` and the blanket arithmetic-recovery sweep was the only thing suppressing it. **Unit (2) - the exact-activation warp256 variant - REMAINS OPEN and is NOT licensed by this result.** One model, one quant, one live count, one host. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-de1-dp4a-e2e/e2e.json`. **AND THE PROMOTION CLOSES ALMOST ALL OF THIS ROW'S OWN IDENTIFIED HEADROOM (`20260929T`).** The bandwidth gate localized 10.091 ms/step of headroom by charging the gate+up owner's 888.83 MB/step at the in-block comparator's 184.66 GB/s. Re-charging the promoted owner: **62.79 GB/s -> 164.48 GB/s**, i.e. **34.0% -> 89.1% of the comparator**, and the gap falls from **2.94x to 1.12x**. The floor at the comparator's rate is 4.813 ms/step, so the headroom falls from **9.342 to 0.591 ms/step** - **93.7% of it consumed**, leaving 0.591 ms/step = **1.25% of the new 47.390 ms/step kernel sum**. **Consequence for this row's remaining work: the dp4a logical128 route captured the prize the row was aiming at, so unit (2) (an exact-activation warp256 variant) is now chasing at most ~0.6 ms/step on this owner rather than the ~10 ms/step that motivated it.** That does not close unit (2) - it was never licensed by the headroom figure alone, and the row's actual ask was an EXACT-activation structure, which this promotion is not - but it materially changes its ranking.  **TAIL RESOLVED BY A BOTH-ARMS-AGAINST-STRICT RUN (`20260930T`): THE REGRESSION IS THE CANDIDATE'S, AND THE SUITE THAT CARRIES IT FAILS BOTH ARMS.** Every prior measurement compared the candidate against the incumbent; this one compares **both arms against the strict teacher** in one process (`--packet dual --teacher`, 18 cases / 1494 rows / 82 steps), which is the only basis that can attribute the tail. **It is the candidate's:** `heldout_mixed_review` candidate **0.26119** against the incumbent's own **0.06017** vs strict (**4.34x**), `mixed_ja_en_review` **0.19023** vs **0.05364** (**3.55x**). **And the flip is NOT a near-tie**, which corrects how this row previously described it: the single top-1 flip on `heldout_mixed_review` is at **step 63 of 83 with margin 0.954472**, where the candidate's step KL is **0.261188** against the incumbent's **0.039213** (6.7x worse); the minimum step margin anywhere in that case is 0.1076. **The discriminating fact is that the 18-case suite fails BOTH arms:** against strict the candidate is mean **0.001164** / p95 0.003536 / p99 0.019565 / max **0.261188** / top-1 0.993307, and the INCUMBENT is mean **0.001054** / p95 0.003732 / p99 **0.024299** / max **0.078490** / top-1 0.994645 - so the incumbent exceeds the 0.05 max ceiling on **3 of 18** cases (`mixed_ja_en_review` 0.05364, `heldout_mixed_summary` 0.07849, `heldout_mixed_review` 0.06017) and fails p99 as well. Neither arm passes, so these bars cannot rank them and this fixture is a stress suite rather than an admission basis - the same conclusion an earlier run reached on the canonical 12-case fixture, now reproduced on the larger suite. **The candidate is not uniformly worse:** relative to the incumbent against the same teacher it is worse on max (3.33x) and mean (1.10x) but BETTER on p99 (0.81x) and p95 (0.95x), so its whole envelope failure is the single step-63 outlier. **A trap found while setting up the localization, recorded because it is easy to hit again:** the runner tests `(not dp4a_layers or gate_layer in dp4a_layers)`, so an EMPTY `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q4_DP4A64_LAYERS` means *no filter* (every layer), not *none* - setting it to `""` to mean "no dp4a" enables dp4a on **all 48 layers** including the 5 normally excluded, and measures incumbent max KL **0.19363** vs strict, **3.2x** the 43-layer scope's 0.06017. The exclusion set is load-bearing. **No default, flag or dispatch changed.** Limits: one model, one quant, one host (gfx1151, not the gfx1100 default hardware), one profile, one fixture family; the 2.06x remains a microbenchmark projection, not an end-to-end measurement. **ROUTE REMOVED (`20260930T`): THE FLIP WAS LOCALIZED, THE SCOPE SPACE WAS EXHAUSTED, AND NO SCOPE CLEARS THE ENVELOPE AT AN ACCEPTABLE COST.** A 9-tier bisection on the case that carries the outlier (`heldout_mixed_review`, both arms vs the strict teacher) localizes the flip to exactly **`{0,2,5,6,8,9,10,11} ∪ [13,18)` = 13 layers**: the three flip carriers `t1_lo`/`t2_a`/`t3_a` intersect at that set and every disjoint set is clean. `t0_none` is a perfect control - dp4a on no layer makes both arms the exact chain, measuring `max_kl = 0.00000` with zero flips. **A counter-intuitive result:** `t_all48` (all 48 layers) is flip-CLEAN while the production 43-layer scope flips, so the 5 excluded layers (1,3,4,7,12) are what CREATE the flip, the opposite of the exclusion's intent. **A limit that constrains how the bisection may be used:** `max_kl` is NOT monotone in the layer set (0.00000/0.05319/0.25861/0.19748/0.11596/0.13123/0.24654/0.10379 at n=0/4/9/13/17/17/26/48) - the 9-layer `t2_b` reproduces nearly the full 0.26 magnitude with NO flip, so the max-KL outlier does not localize by layer-set bisection; only the top-1 flip does. **The full 18-case suite then exhausts the scope space** (both arms vs strict, 1494 rows, one harness): 43L production fails max **0.261188** (5.2x over); 48L fails mean+max (0.128120); 35L fails mean+p99+max (0.104060); 17L fails max (0.115957); and **the only passing scope, 30L, passes every bar with 1.84x-3.10x margin and dominates the production default on all five axes** (mean 0.000428 vs 0.001054, max 0.027240 vs 0.078490, top-1 0.995314 vs 0.994645). **But the 30L scope costs a MEASURED +1.426 ms/step = +3.10% of decode kernel time** (46.061 -> 47.486, per-kernel join over the decode ROCTX windows; both arms valid, token/state exact, lifecycle closed). Decomposition closes: -2.079 (dp4a owner 5.415 at 43L -> warp 3.336 at 30L) + 3.556 (13 more exact layers, 1.485 -> 5.041) = +1.477 vs measured +1.426. **The decision-relevant correction: the warp is not what costs time.** At a fixed 30-layer scope the warp SAVES 0.493 ms/step against the same scope without it, so the +1.426 is the price of the SCOPE and there is no configuration where the warp pays for the numerics it needs. **And the gate that retained the flag never covered the failing case:** its docstring cited 996 shared-chain teacher-forced rows passing with 2.6-4.4x margin, but that fixture does not contain `heldout_mixed_review`, which is exactly where the warp fails - the coverage gap this row's own action cell had predicted. **The production default, for contrast, PASSES the admission gate with 4-8x margin** (mean 2.44e-4 vs 1e-3, p99 3.67e-3, max 6.14e-3 vs 5e-2, top-1 0.9911, determinism 3/3, clean isolation), and the 18-case suite it fails is already recorded as a stress fixture whose bars cannot rank arms. **Therefore both runtime routes and both flags are DELETED** (`HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_MOE_DECODE_WARP` gate/up and `_DOWN`); the kernel definitions stay registered for direct use by the probes. every node inherits the enclosing `moe:layers.N.expert_gate` role. **The split is therefore done by dispatch signature, not by marker:** `scripts/qwen4exp_de2_shared_expert_attribution.py` groups the 1,749 replayed MoE-graph rows by `(Grid_Size_X, Workgroup_Size_X, VGPR, LDS)`, attaches the real kernel name from the twin restricted to graph-replayed rows (no HIP launch API row), and classifies each signature against the exact GGUF payload shapes. **The 24.5504 ms/step family decomposes exactly:** routed gate+up **14.0126** (47 k/st), routed down **5.4436** (43), routed selected misc 1.3444, **shared gate+up 1.2939** (96 k/st, Q8_0 out=640), **router GEMV 1.2389** (48, F32 out=512), **shared down 0.5185** (48, Q8_0 out=2560), router select 0.3310, **shared gate weight 0.1239** (48, F32 out=1), convert/weighted-sum mixed 0.1164, **shared combine 0.0671** (48), **shared silu_mul 0.0587** (48), misc 0.0014. **The shared expert is 2.0621 ms/step = 3.80% of the 54.2181 ms decode kernel sum** (weight-bearing gate+up+down+gate weight = 1.9363), **inside the row's own [1.36, 2.544] bracket** and near its upper half - so the attribution gap is closed, not merely bounded. **The split is sound because the shapes are unique:** the model has exactly 96 `[2560,640]` Q8_0 tensors (the shared gate and up), and the graph has exactly 2 pack8 out=640 nodes/layer; the only `out=1` GEMV is `ffn_gate_inp_shexp` and the only `out=512` GEMV is the router `ffn_gate_inp` (251.66 MB/step). **Three published numbers reproduce as self-checks:** 14.0126 vs 14.013, 5.4436 vs 5.444, and shared combine 0.0671 vs 0.067. **What this does to the action:** fused gate+up targets `shared_gate_up` 1.2939 ms/step at 129.4 GB/s vs `shared_down` 0.5185 at 161.5 GB/s, so gate+up is the slower half and the fusion target is right; the perfect-fusion ceiling is **2.0621 ms/step = 3.80%**, so a realistic recovery stays under 1% and DE-2 still ranks ~4x below DE-1. **No speedup claimed and no kernel built.** One model, one quant, one host, one live count, one window, diagnostic per-kernel times.
