Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-de3-output-pair-screen/README.md` **X-4'S DEVICE SPLIT IS NOW AVAILABLE (`20260929T`) - this row was parked "after `X-4`" and that dependency is satisfied.** One `code-p4096` decode transition at live count 4097, 3 snapshot-restored repetitions, `rocprofv3 --runtime-trace --kernel-trace --marker-trace --kernel-rename`, attributed to semantic roles by the established join plus the `--kernel-rename` fallback for graph-replayed kernels. **Decode kernel time is 54.218 ms/step** over 1,771 kernels/step and 740 roles, **100% attributed**, and the 30 families sum to the total exactly. **The dense/linear family is 23.166 ms/step = 42.73% of decode kernel time**, against the row's "24.73 ms/token" anchor. Within it the largest members are `linear:attn_qkv` 4.658 (8.59%), **`linear:root.lm_head` 2.980 (5.50%) in a single kernel per step**, `linear:attn_gate` 2.890, `linear:ssm_out` 2.842, `linear:attn_q` 1.815, and the hyper-connection pair `hc_ffn_down` 1.771 / `hc_attn_down` 1.768. **The hyper-connection members alone are 6.911 ms/step = 12.75%**, which the row's action does not mention. **THE DECODE OWNER IS NOW MEASURED, AND IT IS THE SINGLE LARGEST DECODE KERNEL (`20260929T`).** Re-attributed from the post-promotion decode capture (`benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-de1-dp4a-e2e/`, kernel rows sliced to the `qwen4exp_decode_live4097_rep{0,1,2}` ROCTX windows, divided by 3): **`gguf_k_pack8_prefill_out_kernel<float, float, 8>` is 24.005 ms/step over 1,491 dispatches = 50.6% of the 47.390 ms/step decode kernel sum** - larger than every other kernel combined below it. Its dispatch shape in decode is `grid=(out_packed, rows)` with `rows=1`, `workgroup=128x1x1`, **VGPR 48, LDS 512**; six distinct `grid.x` values appear (163840 / 98304 / 40960 / 10240 / 8192 / 5120), i.e. it serves several different projections, so this is a FAMILY of dense GEMVs sharing one generic kernel rather than one tensor. **This confirms the row's premise and replaces its historical '24.73 ms/token' linear figure with a current, kernel-level one.** **LEAD, NOT YET ESTABLISHED:** `hipengine/kernels/hip_gfx1100/quant/gguf_q8_0_pack8_gemv.py:6-7` documents `gguf_q8_0_pack8_gemv_decode_*` as "a drop-in replacement for the decode-shaped `gguf_k_pack8_prefill_out_kernel<unsigned short, ...>`", and the running kernel's third template argument is `8` (the q8_0 qtype slot) - **but that decode-shaped owner is not registered anywhere in the `hip_gfx1151` tree**, so decode is reaching the generic k-quant pack8 kernel instead. Whether the decode-shaped owner is faster at this shape, and whether it is numerically admissible for this quant, is **NOT measured** and is exactly the row's first bounded experiment. **No bandwidth claim is made here** - the byte total for these projections has not been established, so the 50.6% is a share of kernel time, not an efficiency figure. One model, one quant, one live count, one host.  **THE 24.005 ms/step SPLITS INTO EIGHT REAL SHAPES, AND THE `Grid_Size_X` TRAP IS NOW DOCUMENTED (`20260929T`).** **`Grid_Size_X` in the `rocprofv3` CSV is TOTAL WORK-ITEMS, not workgroups** - dividing by the 128-wide block recovers the tensor widths, and the identity is self-checking: `3973120/128 = 31040 = 248320/8` is the lm_head, whose 675.43 MB in 2.969 ms is **227.5 GB/s, at the 221 GB/s roof**, independently reproducing the known comparator. **Per shape (ms/step, dispatches, us each, workgroups, out_features):** 10240/`1280` **6.822, 402, 50.9** (28.4%); 2560/`320` **4.333, 291, 44.7** (18.0%); 320/`40` **3.601, 291, 37.1** (15.0%); 248320/`31040` **2.969, 3, 2969.1** (12.4%, lm_head at the roof); 6144/`768` **2.867, 108, 79.6** (11.9%); 12288/`1536` **1.820, 36, 151.7** (7.6%); 640/`80` **1.286, 288, 13.4** (5.4%); 512/`64` **0.307, 72, 12.8** (1.3%). The top four are 74% of the family. **What this changes:** the family is NOT one slow kernel - the lm_head member is already AT the roof, so the headroom is concentrated in the 10240, 2560 and 320 shapes. **NOT ESTABLISHED and explicitly not claimed:** per-shape bandwidth. The byte total needs a tensor->shape->quant map (the DE-2 pricing script is the template), and without it a 50.9 us dispatch at out=10240 cannot be charged - assuming a bits-per-weight figure would be exactly the unsound estimate this ledger has already had to retract once. **Also NOT established:** that the unregistered decode-shaped `gguf_q8_0_pack8_gemv_decode_*` would be faster or numerically admissible; the running instantiation is `f32_f32_out` while that kernel is `bf16_bf16_out`, so they are not a straight swap.  **MEASURED: THE HEADROOM IS LAUNCH STARVATION, AND IT IS A CLEAN MONOTONIC CURVE (`20260929T`).** Cross-referencing the eight widths against the shipped GGUF (all eight families are **GGML type 8 = Q8_0**, consistent with the `QTYPE=8` instantiation) turns the shape split into a bandwidth curve. The dispatch count is `tensors x 3 reps`, so five shapes have an **exact** byte total (dispatch count equals tensor count); the other three are upper bounds only. **Exact shapes - (out_features, workgroups, GB/s, % of 221 roof):** 320/`40`/**93.8**/**42.4%**; 640/`80`/**130.0**/**58.8%**; 2560/`320`/206.0/93.2%; 6144/`768`/209.8/95.0%; 12288/`1536`/**220.4**/**99.7%**. **Upper bounds (bytes over-counted, so true rate is lower):** 10240/`1280`/<=201.5/<=91.2%; 248320/`31040`/227.5 (this one is the known lm_head comparator, not over-counted); 512/`64`/not usable. **The relation is monotonic in grid size and saturates at roughly 320 workgroups**, which is the signature of a grid too small to fill the GPU rather than of a slow kernel: the same code is at 99.7% of roof when given 1536 workgroups. **Headroom, priced against 95% of roof:** out=320 saves 1.99 ms/step, out=640 saves 0.49, out=512 saves about 0.17 - **about 2.65 ms/step, 11.0% of this family and roughly 4.9% of the 54.6 ms/step decode kernel sum**. **Next action (not yet done):** the fix this implies is split-K on the small-out shapes, raising the workgroup count above the ~320 saturation knee without changing arithmetic order per output. **Not claimed:** that split-K achieves it, or any end-to-end number; and the 10240/512 shapes stay unresolved until their tensor subsets are pinned, since the byte upper bounds there cannot support a rate claim.  **ALL EIGHT SHAPES NOW EXACT, AND THE STARVATION RESOLVES TO `out_features <= 640` (`20260929T`).** The tensor map closes exactly: every observed dispatch count equals its tensor count, with no remainder - out=10240 is 97 `[320,10240]` + 37 `[2560,10240]` = **134**; out=2560 is 48 `[640,2560]` + 48 `[6144,2560]` + 1 `[2560,2560]` = **97**; out=320 is 97 `[10240,320]`; out=640 is 96 `[2560,640]`; out=6144 is 36 `[2560,6144]`; out=12288 is 12 `[2560,12288]`; out=512 is 24 `[2560,512]`; out=248320 is 1 `[2560,248320]`. All are Q8_0. **This supersedes the previous entry's upper bounds** - out=512 was previously unusable because all 84 tensors sharing that width were summed instead of the 24 that are actually Q8_0 and dispatched, and out=248320 summed both lm_head candidates. **Exact table (out_features, in_features, tensors/step, MB/step, ms/step, GB/s, % of 221 roof, workgroups):** 10240/320+2560/134/1368.3/**6.822**/200.6/90.8%/1280; 2560/640+6144+2560/97/892.7/**4.333**/206.0/93.2%/320; **320/10240/97/337.7/3.601/93.8/42.4%/40**; 248320/2560/1/675.4/**2.969**/227.5/102.9%/31040; 6144/2560/36/601.6/**2.867**/209.8/95.0%/768; 12288/2560/12/401.1/**1.820**/220.4/99.7%/1536; **640/2560/96/167.1/1.286/130.0/58.8%/80**; **512/2560/24/33.4/0.307/108.9/49.3%/64**. Family total 4477.3 MB/step in 24.005 ms = **186.5 GB/s = 84.4% of roof**. **The discriminator is the grid, not the aspect ratio:** every shape at 320 or more workgroups lands at 90.8-102.9% of roof, and every shape below 100 workgroups lands at 42.4-58.8%. out=320 (in=10240) has the *largest* K of any shape and is still the worst, which rules out arithmetic intensity as the explanation and leaves grid size. **Priced headroom at 95% of roof, all three byte-exact:** out=320 saves 1.991 ms/step, out=640 saves 0.489, out=512 saves 0.148 - **2.628 ms/step, 10.9% of this family and 4.8% of the 54.6 ms/step decode kernel sum**. **The in-tree code already solves this for prefill and not for decode:** `gguf_linear.py` carries a `tile16_row_threshold` heuristic whose comment is "Keep enough column blocks to fill the device at short/narrow shapes", but its guard is `rows > 1`, so decode (`rows == 1`) never reaches it. **Two candidate routes, neither yet measured:** (a) select a registered variant with a finer column tile or split-K at these three shapes; (b) the `_gemv_decode_dispatch` rewrite - which already covers `gguf_q8_0` and would map `pack8_gemv_f32_f32_out` to `pack8_gemv_decode_f32_f32_out` - currently **silently falls back**, because the decode family ships only `bf16_bf16_out` and `fp16_fp16_out` and the running instantiation is `f32_f32_out`. **Also noted:** `HIPENGINE_GGUF_GEMV_DECODE` defaults off and the qwen4exp runner never sets it, so that rewrite is inactive on this path for two independent reasons.  **SCREENED: THE BLOCK-SIZE LEVER IS WORTH 1.490 ms/step AND THE SIGN SPLITS EXACTLY ON `out_features` (`20260930T`).** `scripts/qwen4exp_de3_starved_shape_screen.py` sweeps each shape the way the engine sweeps it - one distinct device weight per real tensor in the step, so the working set matches the trace and nothing is L3-resident. **A first attempt got this wrong and is recorded as such:** it timed a single 3.48 MB weight across 35 re-reads and reported 326.9 GB/s, above the 221 GB/s roof and therefore invalid, because the whole weight was L3-resident while the real step streams 4477 MB. **At `threads=128` the corrected screen reproduces the trace rate at every comparable shape** (248320: 225.1 vs 227.5 GB/s; 12288: 215.0 vs 220.4; 2560: 202.6 vs 206.0; 320: 92.4 vs 93.8), which is what makes the 256 column citable. **Result (best-of-12, two runs agreeing within 0.5%):** 320x10240 3654.8 -> 2469.2 us (**-32.4%**, 92.4 -> **136.8 GB/s**); 640x2560 1375.0 -> 1108.5 (**-19.4%**, 121.5 -> **150.8**); 512x2560 270.5 -> 206.1 (-23.8%). Against that, 10240x2560 +1.1%, 248320x2560 +1.5%, 12288x2560 +1.3%, 2560x6144 -1.8%, 6144x2560 -1.9%. **The split is exact and follows `out_features`, not size: every shape at `out_features <= 640` gains 19.4-32.4% and every shape at `out_features >= 2560` loses 1.1-1.9%.** Gating at 640 nets **+1.490 ms/step = 6.2% of the 24.005 ms family = 2.71% of the 54.603 ms/step decode kernel sum**. The competing variant `gemv_f32_f32_out` was measured at all eight shapes and is decisively worse (41-53% of roof, insensitive to `threads`), so pack8 is the right family and this is not a variant-selection miss. **Blocker, stated plainly: this is a T1/T2 arithmetic change, not an exact one.** 256 threads per 8-output workgroup means 32 threads per output instead of 16, so the K-reduction tree differs; measured divergence is max relative **2.6e-7** (float32 rounding level) at both shapes. It therefore needs the production numerical envelope before promotion. Artifact: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-de3-threads-lever/`. `threads` is validated to {64,128,256}.  **MEASURED IN THE ENGINE AND IMPLEMENTED (`20260930T`).** `HIPENGINE_GGUF_PACK8_GEMV_WIDE=1` was run through the profiled decode harness on a stable tree; `route_env` confirms the override reached the measured process (`bound_route_env` is the pre-override snapshot by design, so its `null` is expected). **Engagement is exact and doubly proven.** In the kernel trace the three narrow shapes move from `wg=128` to `wg=256` with **identical workgroup counts** (40/80/24 - same grid, wider block): grid 5120->10240 with 291 dispatches, 10240->20480 with 288, 8192->16384 with 72, and no other shape changes. Independently, the emitted token is unchanged (198 in both arms) while `state_sha256` differs (`418c3374...` -> `540dd166...`), which is the exact signature of a T2 association drift rather than a wrong answer. **Measured win, per-kernel join vs the DE-1 promoted baseline:** out=320 **3.601 -> 2.446 ms (-32.1%)**, out=640 **1.286 -> 1.046 (-18.7%)**, out=512 **0.307 -> 0.241 (-21.5%)**; narrow total **5.194 -> 3.733 (-28.1%)**; family total **24.005 -> 22.590 ms/step (-1.416, -5.9%)**, i.e. **2.59% of the 54.603 ms/step decode kernel sum**. The saturated shapes moved only +0.1% to +0.8%. **The screen predicted 1.490 ms/step and the engine measured 1.416 - within 5%**, which is the screen's own validation. **Rule safety was closed across the whole gated range**, not just the three shapes the engine happens to use: a sweep of 48/128/256/320/384/512/640/768 shows 320 and 640 reproducing their wins (-32.5%, -19.4%) while every `in_features=512` shape is L3-resident and neutral within 1% - those are launch-bound at about 11 us per dispatch, which is also their real condition in a model whose whole narrow working set is 2.5 MB, so 256 threads neither helps nor hurts them. **Implemented** as `_pack8_gemv_decode_threads` in `gguf_linear.py`, gated on grid size rather than quant scheme (the reason is occupancy), applied on both `launch_gguf_linear` and `launch_gguf_linear_raw_ptr`, and only when the caller supplied no block size. It is **default OFF** behind `HIPENGINE_GGUF_PACK8_GEMV_WIDE` with a `docs/REFACTOR.md` removal condition, 20 unit cases, and a dispatch-cache safety check: `_DISPATCH_RESOLVE_CACHE` stores `(abi, fn, layer, quant, variant)` and the block size is resolved *after* the cache read, so the env flip is never replayed from cache. **Remaining blocker before promotion: the production numerical envelope.** `token_exact`/`state_exact` in the decode harness are repeat-agreement checks (determinism), not accuracy, so they do not close this; the applicable gate is the calibrated teacher-forced mean/p95/p99/max KL and top-1 envelope. The gate's default model does exercise the route (84 Q8_0 tensors at out=512 and 96 at out=48), so the gate is not vacuous.  **FIRST GATE RUN VOID, AND THE DIAGNOSIS IS A HARNESS-SCOPE FINDING (`20260930T`).** The adapted c1 threads gate (model Qwen3.6-35B-A3B) returned `kl_mean = 0.0`, `kl_max = 0.0`, `top1_agreement = 1.0` over 450 rows with `strict_logits_sha256 == candidate_logits_sha256` and all 18 prompts flagged `strict_and_candidate_bytes_equal`, i.e. **the two arms were bit-identical and the candidate route never ran** - a green quality status in that situation is vacuous, so the result is discarded rather than reported. Two contributing errors are recorded: my shell reported `EXIT=0` because `$?` followed a tail pipe, not the gate, which actually returned `blocked`; and the run's `provenance.dirty` was true with `execution_affecting_dirty: false` and `dirty_paths: []`, i.e. dirtiness from other workers' untracked artifacts, which `measurement_valid` rejects on the raw `dirty` flag. **Root cause, measured with a route probe rather than assumed:** `scripts/qwen4exp_de3_route_probe.py` wraps the predicate and runs a real decode. The predicate IS reached (549 calls) but only ever sees `bf16_hidden_bf16_out` at out=32 (480 calls) and `t16_gemv_decode_bf16_f32_out` at out=248320 (9 calls). **That model never dispatches the `pack8_gemv_*` family at all**, so the `pack8_gemv_` guard correctly rejected every call and the predicate returned a wider block **0 times**. **Consequence: a KL gate run on that model cannot exercise this change, and the void result says nothing about the candidate's numerical quality - only that the wrong harness was used.** **The applicable gate is `scripts/qwen4exp_layer2_profile_gate.py`** - it qualifies a Qwen4Exp candidate against its strict parent on the same 18-prompt/450-row protocol, and it runs on the model whose decode actually owns this family.  **GATE PASSED ON THE APPLICABLE HARNESS AND THE LEVER IS PROMOTED TO THE DEFAULT (`20260930T`).** `scripts/qwen4exp_layer2_profile_gate.py --candidate pack8_gemv_wide_256` on Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-UD-Q4_K_XL (head `d49dc011c`, tracked tree clean, `measurement_valid = true`) **engaged the route**: `strict_logits_sha256 = 7b4385b7...` vs `candidate_logits_sha256 = 6094c507...`. Every binding numerical gate passes with 2.8-4.1x margin - `kl_mean` **0.000287** vs 0.001, `kl_p95` **0.001425** vs 0.005, `kl_p99` **0.004831** vs 0.02, `kl_max` **0.018131** vs 0.05, `top1_agreement` **0.99778** vs 0.99, `hard_gates_passed = true`, `requires_outlier_review = false`, `eligible_for_automatic_admission = true`, `rows_over_review_boundary = []`. All four categories pass their 0.97 per-scope top-1 floor: code 0.99333, general_en 1.0, general_ja 1.0, mixed_ja_en 1.0. Determinism (`repeat_determinism.passed`, 3 runs, 0 mismatches), the state repeat gate, and the lifecycle gate (all 83.5 GB freed, 0 active allocations) all pass. **Scope is confirmed precisely:** the `prefill_last` scope scores `kl_mean = 6.9e-14` and `max_abs_logit_delta = 2.9e-06` - pure float noise - so the lever touches only decode, exactly as designed, and prefill is untouched. `teacher_nll_delta = -0.00042`, i.e. the candidate is marginally better on teacher NLL, not worse. **Two review items were inspected and judged benign rather than waved through.** (1) The single top-1 mismatch (teacher step 13 of `heldout_code_rate_limiter`) is a near-tie: `strict_margin = 0.0248`, `strict_top1_candidate_rank = 2` (the candidate ranked the strict winner second), `topk_overlap = 1.0` (identical top-5 set), `kl = 0.000626` - 32x below the 0.02 review boundary - and it sits inside the flip-eligible set (`top1_mismatches_outside_flip_eligible = 0`). (2) The single free-output divergence emits `from typing import Dict, Deque, Tuple` where strict emitted `from typing import Dict, Deque`; both produce the same working `SlidingWindowRateLimiter`, and `candidate_repeat_exact = true` so it is deterministic. **Disposition:** `_pack8_gemv_wide_threads_enabled()` now defaults on; `HIPENGINE_GGUF_PACK8_GEMV_WIDE=0` restores the 128-wide launcher default for rollback or bisection. The unit test file was updated to the new contract (default-on, explicit falsy spellings opt out) and the surrounding bundle is green (1023 passed). |

routing telemetry is `R10` **X-4'S DEVICE SPLIT IS NOW AVAILABLE (`20260929T`) - this row was parked "after `X-4`" and that dependency is satisfied.** One `code-p4096` decode transition at live count 4097, 3 snapshot-restored repetitions, `rocprofv3 --runtime-trace --kernel-trace --marker-trace --kernel-rename`, attributed to semantic roles by the established join plus the `--kernel-rename` fallback for graph-replayed kernels. **Decode kernel time is 54.218 ms/step** over 1,771 kernels/step and 740 roles, **100% attributed**, and the 30 families sum to the total exactly.  **The governing new number for this row is that DECODE IS NOT KERNEL-BOUND**: the unprofiled wall for the same transition is median **153.0 ms** (spread 1.30x), so the kernels are only **~35%** of a decode step and **~65% is host/launch overhead**, with 48 graph launches carrying 1,771 kernels. **This is the number DE-5 was missing**: the serving-wrapper host time the row describes as "~3.6 ms/token above the runner loop" is measured here at a much larger scale - roughly **99 ms/step of non-kernel time** against 54.2 ms of kernels.   **CORRECTED (`20260929T`): the "~65% host/launch overhead" DOES NOT HOLD, and the row's stated target is measured negligible.** Two independent defects. **(1) Cross-run subtraction:** the 153.04 ms wall comes from `decode-warmup.json` (`profile: false`, `role_markers: false`) while the 54.218 ms kernel sum comes from `decode-profiled.json` (`profile: true`, `role_markers: true`), whose OWN wall median is **225.79 ms** (min 82.0, max 228.1) - not 153.0. Subtracting a kernel sum measured in one instrumentation state from a wall measured in another is not a decomposition. **(2) Same-host history contradicts it:** `benchmarks/results/2026-09-13-framework-qwen4exp-strix-journey-baselines.json` measures the SAME host (`machine_id 55ea6c509d0b49eea8de7094a1023668`) and SAME model (`unsloth-Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-UD-Q4_K_XL`) at `arms.he-ar.summary.cases.code-p4096.decode_tok_s.median` = **19.134 tok/s = 52.26 ms/token** (decode-only, full serving request). So: true step **52.26 ms**, x4 kernel sum **54.218 ms (1.04x)**, x4 wall **153.04 ms (2.93x)** - **the kernel sum agrees with the independently measured step and the WALL is the outlier**, the opposite of the note's reading. A 2.93x regression would be a headline event; the rollup records only improvements in that window (09-26 F16 staging +3.4%, 09-27 full-attn decode-context +2.11%, 09-28 QK unroll +2.65%) and no decode regression of any size. **(3) The row's target is negligible:** the per-step census, identical across all 3 reps, is `memcpy` 3 calls / **10,440 B**, `memcpy_async` 12 / 6,144 B, `memset` 1 / 16 B, `graph_launch` 48 - the ENTIRE synchronous-copy budget is **10.2 KiB**, ~47 ns at the documented ~221 GB/s roof and ~15 us even charging ~5 us of launch cost per call, i.e. **~0.03%** of a 52.26 ms step. There is no win there. The sampling half is not exercised at all (`capture_logits=False`, `token_id_resident=True`). **(4) The row already said this:** its mechanism cell reads "Layer graphs are measured negative: the launch host time is already overlapped" - the same conclusion, from a direct measurement, contradicting the appended 65% claim. **Verdict:** the 65% figure should not drive work; the row's original scope stands and is the wrapper's ~3.6 ms/token (logits D2H, sampling, bookkeeping) = **~6.9%** of a 52.26 ms step. **The harness, not the engine, needs the follow-up** - most likely `snapshot_restore_between_repeats: true` interacting with the QSA indexed-sparse path's rewindable index state (hypothesis, untested). **No new measurement was taken** - all figures quoted from pre-existing artifacts.  **RE-DERIVED AND THE OPEN FOLLOW-UP IS MEASURED (`20260930T`) - the wrapper cost is confirmed at ~3 ms/token and the harness anomaly is localized to the `reset()` path.** The prior correction closed with 'the harness, not the engine, needs the follow-up - most likely `snapshot_restore_between_repeats: true` interacting with the QSA indexed-sparse path's rewindable index state (hypothesis, untested)'. That follow-up is now measured by `scripts/qwen4exp_de5_wall_split.py` on `code-p1024` at the production boundary (`capture_logits=False`, `token_id_resident=True`), which does **not** use the snapshot mechanism: the only state rewind is `runner.prefill()`, whose `reset()` clears state, memsets each attention KV cache, resets the index states and drops `_ple_hash_states`. **Host time is 2.7-3.3 ms/token** (512 transitions, 3 loops: wall 52.76 / 54.01 / 53.78 ms/token, host 2.69 / 3.34 / 3.05) - the row's own ~3.6 ms/token, i.e. **~5-6% of a step, not a large per-token cost**. Steady state is flat at **49.8-50.5 ms/token (~19.8-20.1 tok/s)** from step 32 on, drifting +1.2 ms over 480 steps (context growth). **The reproducible anomaly is a per-prefill transient of ~1.5-1.8 s in the first ~32 steps**, absent in the warmup (first loop after model load) and present in every repeat: first-eighth bands 145.48 / 144.42 / 145.99 ms decaying through ~65 ms to a flat ~49.9. **The row's untested hypothesis is therefore reproduced on the `reset()` path and RELOCATED out of the x4 harness.** Four mechanisms were tested and excluded: **unsynchronized prefill tail** - prefill is 4.2-4.8 s and a second `device_synchronize()` immediately after it returns **0.00-0.01 ms**, so nothing is queued; **queued device work** - same result; **clock throttling** - `pp_dpm_sclk`/`pp_dpm_mclk` sampled every 5 ms as sixteenth-bands are **flat 2900 MHz / 1000 MHz in every band of every loop**; **pure host blocking** - the busy mean is 82.0% versus 95.4% with an idle, so the GPU idles ~11.5 ms/token, but 32 steps of pure host blocking would require ~37% idle, making the transient a **mix**. **A 5 s idle between the prefill and the decode removes the transient entirely but is NOT a fix**: no-settle `4.208 + 512x53.94/1000 = 31.8 s` versus settle `4.193 + 5.0 + 512x50.50/1000 = 35.1 s`, a net **+3.3 s**, so the work is relocated rather than removed and idling must not be adopted. **Path-independence checked:** the probe was first written with the runner default `capture_logits=True` (which synchronizes, copies full-vocab logits D2H and argmaxes on the host); corrected to the device-argmax boundary the numbers agree within noise (production 54.01 / 53.78 versus diagnostic 53.94 / 53.79) and **the transient is present on both**, so it is a decode-path property, not a logits-capture artifact. **Still OPEN:** no mechanism inside `reset()` is isolated - `state.zero()`, the per-attention KV memsets, `index.reset()` and the `_ple_hash_states` clear are all candidates and none is individually excluded. **Limits:** one case, one prompt, one model, one quant, one host, one profile; the driver does not document the busy counter's averaging window so busy-derived shares are approximate and the per-step wall series is the primary evidence. **No performance claim, no promotion, no default changed.**  **RESET EXONERATED AND THE TRIGGER SHARPENED (`20260930T`) - the transient is not in `reset()` and not in the first loop.** `scripts/qwen4exp_de5_reset_split.py` timed `reset()` and each component with a sync on both sides: whole reset **1.14 ms (warmup) / 2.23 ms (rep1)**, against a ~1.5 s transient - **three orders of magnitude too small to be the owner**. Components: `state.zero()` 0.54/0.56, KV memsets over 12 attention states 0.44/1.10, `index.reset()` over 12 index states 0.02/0.06, `_ple_hash_states` clear 0.00/0.01 ms. The KV memsets move **31,457,280 B in 0.44-1.10 ms against 0.143 ms predicted at RT-4's 220 GB/s**, so that rate model holds at this scale. **The run also revised the trigger.** Every earlier run had the warmup flat (~53-57 ms first-eighth); this probe inserts an extra prefill before the measured decode (`prefill #1` -> `reset()` + components -> `prefill #2` -> decode) and the warmup then showed the transient too (first-eighth **146.80**, bands 146.8 / 61.6 / 52.5 / ...), as did rep1 (146.74, bands 146.7 / 97.5 / 58.2 / 49.6 / ...). **So the rule is not 'warmup versus repeat' but: the FIRST prefill+decode after model load is fast, and every subsequent one carries the transient.** That excludes anything depending on decode context or on loop count, and points at something the first prefill establishes and each later prefill invalidates. **The prefill workspace path was checked and is clean:** `_prefill_workspace_selection` is built once at construction (6606/6667/6689) and closed at `close()` (7953-7956), so there is no per-prefill alloc/free there. **NOT YET ISOLATED:** the mechanism - a graph cache, a residency or page-table effect, or allocation churn are the remaining candidates. **VOID TEST:** an allocation-churn probe sampled `runtime.memory_info()` before/after each prefill and through the first 40 decode steps; the method returned `None` on this runtime, so churn is **untested, not excluded** (the call itself was cheap at 0.002-0.023 ms, so it errored rather than being slow). **Limits:** one case, one prompt, one model, one quant, one host, one profile. **No performance claim, no promotion, no default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-de5-wrapper-wall/README.md`. **MECHANISM ISOLATED AND THE ROW CLOSES (`20261001T`): the transient is a HOST CPU PACKAGE CLOCK CLAMP, not engine host code.** The instrument this harness never had is the host CPU clock (`scaling_cur_freq` across all 32 cores; every prior run sampled only `pp_dpm_sclk`/`mclk`, flat at 2900/1000). Measured: every post-prefill decode runs its first ~2 s with **all 32 cores pinned at the 2.0 GHz base clock** - step ms by eighth **159.9 / 87.1 / 57.7 / 49.6 / 49.3 ...** against max-core MHz by sixteenth **2000 x6, then 2032 / 3578 / 4130 ...** - while the warmup decode of the same loop reaches **5.09-5.12 GHz** within ~0.7 s (it starts at 3.2-3.5 GHz) and steps at a flat 52 ms. **How deep the decode starts tracks how much of the prefill ran clamped, not the prefill's length:** the warmups' prefills are clamped for **0.65-0.78** of their samples and their decodes start at 3.2-3.5 GHz with 57-60 ms steps, while the repeats' prefills are clamped for **0.97-0.98** and their decodes start at exactly **2000 MHz** with 162 ms steps. The clamp is **established during the prefill** (its own second eighth reads 2000, and the warmup's prefill shows it engaging ~1 s in) and **scales with it** (p512's 1.99 s prefill: transient 102.3 ms and a ~1.5 s clamp; p1024's 4.05 s: 162.1 ms and ~2 s). **The 5 s settle arm is the causal confirmation:** with `--settle-ms 5000` the clock is back at boost at the first decode step and the step series is **49.35 49.14 49.22 49.42 49.37 49.39 49.41 49.41** - flat from step 0, which is why that arm's +3.3 s net cost is a wait rather than a fix. **The named candidates are excluded by direct counters (graph capture, allocation churn) or made unnecessary (residency/page-table), and the host-side suspects are excluded as well:** graph capture (**48 MoE captures in the warmup's first 16 steps, 0 in every band of every later decode**; capture costs ~100 ms in total), allocation churn (**0.00 MiB in every band of every rep** - the warmup prefill allocates 167,772,176 B and frees 52,428,808 B, every later prefill allocates and frees **0 B**, and the transient is present in exactly those reps), and GC (**0 collections in the transient bands**; with `gc.disable()` the transient is unchanged at 151.1 / 84.3 / 58.0 against 152.4 / 78.2 / 54.8 ms). Cgroup throttling is excluded too (`nr_periods 0`, `nr_throttled 0`), and with a sync per step the device wait is **0.03-0.14 ms in every band** while the host call half carries the whole transient (153.8 -> 49.4 ms), with process CPU time tracking the wall (193.0 -> 81.1 ms/step) and `cProfile` showing the same function mix at ~1.5x per-call cost - the same work at a lower clock. **This also corrects the earlier reading of this row's own numbers: the steady state is not host-submission-bound** - 49.3 ms/step while the package is still at the 2.0 GHz clamp and 49.3 ms/step after it returns to 3.9-4.5 GHz, and 49.35 ms flat in the settled arm at 5.0-5.1 GHz, so over a 2.5x clock range the step does not move and the device is the limit above the base clock, and `call_ms ~= 49.4` with `wait_ms ~= 0.03` is device-paced blocking inside the launch calls, not host CPU. **The trigger is the prefill's own load and not any single load class:** a control probe (`scripts/qwen4exp_de5_clock_burst_probe.py`, 5 s arms) leaves the package at **5117 MHz for a one-thread CPU burst, 5117 for a host memory-traffic burst, 3536 for a 474 GiB D2D burst, 5118 for CPU+GPU and 3555 for memory+GPU** - none reaches base. The one consistent trend is that GPU load alone suppresses the boost to ~3.5 GHz without clamping, the shape of a shared package power budget. **So this is a platform power-management response to the prefill, not an engine host-code defect**, there is no in-tree fix, and the settle workaround stays net-negative. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-01-qwen4exp-de5-clock-clamp/`. **Limits:** one host (integrated APU, not the gfx1100/W7900 default hardware), one model/quant/profile/case family; the clock cannot be pinned without root, so the causal link is the settle arm plus the clock/step-time correspondence rather than a forced clock; `cpuinfo_avg_freq` is a per-CPU attribute, not a package average; the burst control does not include a compute-heavy GPU kernel loop, so the prefill's own GPU power profile remains the untested candidate trigger. **No performance claim, no promotion, no default changed.** |

Within-arm repeatability 0.03%/0.04%; a bare 8-byte D2H copy is 0.009 ms. **So host enqueue and device execution are within ~1% of each other and the wall is `max(host, device)`: a host-only or device-only change is capped at roughly half its nominal saving.** At p512 the step is device-limited and there is almost no remainder to attack; at p4096 the remainder is the target. | Host-profile the bench's own decode loop at p4096 (not the probe's - see the limit) and attribute the 10.56 ms/step before choosing between launch consolidation, per-step host work, or a deeper cause. (2) The **23.8 ms/step readback cost** first measured was a position confound: the two arms ran at different sequence positions on one growing sequence and so differed in KV state and in which PLE rows had to be faulted in. **Limits:** `wall - kernel_sum` is a difference of two differently measured quantities and the profiled capture's own note records that the profiler serializes launches and inflates absolute times, so read 46.19 / 47.13 as the device floor and the **wall growth** as the finding; the probe's absolute levels (54.7 vs 47.7 ms/step at p512, 84.6 vs 57.7 at p4096) are inflated because it never warms the PLE page cache and runs both cases in one process, so its within-arm deltas are sound and its absolute levels are not bench levels; one host (`gfx1151`, not the gfx1100 default hardware), one model, one quant, one profile, two depths rather than a curve. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-decode-readback-split/`, `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-decode-capture-mode/`, worklog `20261004T084710` and `20261004T083532`. **CORRECTED (`20261004T`, later the same day): THE GROWING REMAINDER IS NOT HOST WORK, AND `--host-profile` CANNOT RANK HOST WORK.** Three measurements replace the host-side reading. (1) **Two ablations of comparable host work, and they disagree in sign at p4096:** the build-resolution memo removed ~2.3 ms/step and measured **+2.53%** (base spread 0.73%), the layer-scope env memo removed ~2.2 ms/step and measured **-0.45%** (base spread 0.15%, candidate below both baselines). A ~2 ms/step host ablation does not reliably move this wall. (2) **The host CPU does not slow down with context.** `scripts/qwen4exp_host_speed_vs_context_probe.py` inserts a fixed, context-independent host workload into the decode loop: **70.816 -> 71.478 us/step (+0.93%)** from p512 to p4096 while the wall grows **+37.5%**. Running under GPU load costs ~5% against an idle GPU and that penalty is flat with depth, so the unified-memory starvation mechanism is refuted. (3) **`--host-profile` tottime is not a valid ranking basis.** Its own inflation grows from 5.9% at p512 to 8.7% at p4096, which inflates every function roughly in proportion and produces a misleading '25% more time for 3% more calls' pattern. **Call counts and caller attribution are exact; tottime is not.** **What is left: the +10.04 ms/step wall growth is device-side gaps or device-side work outside kernel durations.** The kernel sum grows +2.0%, the host CPU is flat, host call counts grow 2-4%, and the launch count grows only 4.7% (1,729 -> 1,811 per step) against a 21% wall growth - so it is not launch *count* either. `X-4` is consistent: the kernel sum is stable to 1.0% while the step span varies 2.8x and the median inter-kernel gap swings 23x across repetitions (53.60 / 58.46 / 2.28 us). **This restores a launch/transition lever, for a device-side reason rather than the withdrawn host-enqueue one.** **The 14,293 environment lookups per decode step are real and were removed (exact, non-regressive), but they are not worth the 24.8% of profiled host time the profile charged them.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-host-speed-vs-context/`, `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-layer-scope-env-memo/`, `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-decode-host-profile/`, worklog `20261004T092503`. **DRIFT-FLOOR CAVEAT (`20261004T`): the two walls this row subtracts from were captured in a different session from the kernel sum, and a sequential `code-p4096` comparison on this host drifts 8%.** Interleaved A/B/A/B/A/B in one session repeats to **0.05-0.48%**, but five sequential fresh-process runs of the same harness varied by **8.0%** on decode and the session's *first* run was the outlier (chunk 512 prefill **262.41** first against **249.76-249.83** in every later run, including the interleaved session's). The walls here (47.656 at p512, 57.694 at p4096) are the thermal packet's chunk-1024 readings (20.98 and 17.33 tok/s); p512 reproduces at 0.02% (**20.985**) but p4096 reads **18.930**, **8.7% higher** - at or inside that drift. **So read the 10.56 ms/step remainder as indicative, not measured, and re-derive it from wall and kernel sum captured in one interleaved run.** The `+2.0%` kernel-sum growth and the `X-4` inter-kernel-gap swing are unaffected (same-session within-run comparisons). Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-chunk-decode-trade/`, worklog `20261004T100141`. **~~THIRD CORRECTION~~ RETRACTED (`20261004T`): the kernel sum is NOT pre-promotion, so there is no apples-to-oranges subtraction and this row's original remainder figures stand.** The correction claimed the `46.1927`/`47.1298` kernel sums came from `X-4`'s `2789c19d6` capture (2026-09-29) while the walls came from the 2026-10-04 thermal packet, with `DE-1`'s dp4a promotion and `DE-3`'s block-size lever (both 2026-09-30) landing in between. **Both halves of that are wrong.** The kernel sums come from `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-thermal-protocol-and-llamacpp-basis/bench-decode-rep-trace.json` - a **2026-10-04** artifact captured from this same canonical bench - and **its family breakdown contains `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_q8_1_dp4a_silu_logical128_t64`, which is `DE-1`'s promoted owner and cannot appear in a pre-promotion capture.** **So the kernel sum and the wall are the same configuration, the subtraction is sound, and the original figures stand: ~1.47 ms/step at p512 and ~10.56 at p4096, growing ~7x.** The retracted text's downstream claims - that the p512 remainder is ~10.1 ms/step, that the launch/transition lever is available at short prompts, and the `-8.660 ms/step` promotion adjustment - are all withdrawn. **What survives is independent of the kernel sum and is in the fourth correction below: the wall tracks kernel time 1:1, so `DE-11`'s "capped at roughly half its nominal saving" is still refuted, and per-launch transition cost is still excluded at 0.0004 ms.** **Method note:** the retracted claim was an inference from commit dates and depth-independence, not a measurement, and it was wrong precisely because the artifact's own contents (a kernel name) were the evidence that would have settled it and were not checked first. Worklog `20261004T104420` records the retraction. **FOURTH CORRECTION (`20261004T`): THE WALL IS NOT `max(host, device)` - A KERNEL-TIME SAVING REPRODUCES 1:1 IN THE WALL, SO `DE-11`'s "CAPPED AT ROUGHLY HALF ITS NOMINAL SAVING" IS REFUTED.** Interleaved A/B/A/B at `code-p512`, chunk 1024, one process per arm, 1 warmup + 3 repetitions: `HIPENGINE_GGUF_PACK8_GEMV_WIDE=1` (default) **20.983 tok/s = 47.657 ms/step** against `=0` **20.325 = 49.201**. Turning off a lever worth a measured **-1.416 ms/step of kernel time** (`DE-3`) costs **1.545 ms/step of wall - 109% of the prediction**, with every arm separated and within-arm spread 0.02%. **The wall behaves as `kernel_sum + fixed_remainder`, so a millisecond removed from the decode kernels is a millisecond removed from the decode wall, and kernel optimization is a direct decode lever.** **This does NOT settle the remainder's magnitude:** the A/B is consistent with both this row's kernel sum (46.19, remainder 1.46) and the correction above (37.53, remainder 10.13), because the two readings differ only in the assumed kernel sum and both predict the lever-off wall to within 0.13 ms. **A same-session kernel sum is still required.** **And per-launch transition cost is now excluded:** one graph launch costs **0.0004 ms** (`scripts/qwen4exp_graph_launch_cost_probe.py`, N=2000, 5 runs), so the 48 per-layer graph launches are **0.018 ms/step** and the graph wrapper costs 0.079 ms/step - two to three orders below what this row's next-step text needed. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-wall-tracks-kernel-time/`, worklog `20261004T104420`. **FIFTH CORRECTION / SAME-SESSION PAIR (`20261007T`): ESTIMATED REMAINDER GROWTH IS 3.15x UNDER THE MEASURED PROTOCOL, NOT THE HISTORICAL CROSS-SESSION 7x.** Wall and kernel sum captured in **one interleaved session** (counterbalanced `A/B/A/B -> B/A/B/A`, one process per arm, cooldown to <=50 C, chunk 1024, warmup 1 + 3 reps wall / rep 1 kernel, `code-p512` and `code-p4096`, production, HEAD `7d97832d3`, gfx1151): p512 wall **47.700** (47.744 / 47.655), kernel sum **44.242** (44.228 / 44.256), remainder **3.457 ms/step**; p4096 wall **55.946** (55.844 / 56.048), kernel **45.050** (45.048 / 45.053), remainder **10.896 ms/step**. Within-wall-arm measured-repetition spread is 0.03-0.60%; producer/depth run-median spread is 0.01-0.36%. Wall grows **+8.246 ms/step (+17.3%)**, kernel **+0.808 (+1.8%)**, remainder **+7.439 = 3.15x**. **The p4096 remainder reproduces this row's 10.56 within 3%, but the p512 remainder is 3.46, not 1.47, and the p512 step is 92.8% kernel, not 96.9%.** The difference is entirely in the kernel sum: this session's p512 kernel sum is **1.95 ms/step lower** (44.24 against 46.19) while the wall is unchanged (47.70 against 47.66). **The historical lever state is unknown.** The dominant `gguf_k_pack8_prefill_out_kernel` is **2851.6 ms** over 128 steps at the default (wide=1), **3039.9 ms** at wide=0 and **3015.8 ms** in the historical trace; total p512 kernel sum is **45.76** at wide=0 versus **44.24** at wide=1. The lever-off family time is within 0.8% of the historical value, supporting a `DE-3` lever-state hypothesis, but it does not prove a lever-on wall was subtracted from a lever-off kernel sum. Changed production dispatch and session variation remain competing explanations. **Method:** `scripts/qwen4exp_canonical_ar_bench.py hipengine` under `rocprofv3 --kernel-trace`, sliced by the bench's own `phase_windows_ns["decode"]` (rocprofv3 dispatch timestamps are `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, the same clock as `perf_counter_ns`); the kernel sum is the total `DurationNs` of dispatches contained in that window over 128 steps, via `scripts/qwen4exp_de11_paired_decode_wall_kernel.py`. The profiled arm's own wall is 48.9 / 56.3 ms/step (2.5% / 0.6% above the unprofiled wall), so profiler inflation is small. **Limits:** one host (gfx1151, not the gfx1100 default), one model, one quant, one profile, one category, two depths, chunk 1024, two wall runs and two kernel runs per depth; the `DurationNs` sum assumes no inter-kernel overlap; the wide=0 attribution is a reproduction (3039.9 against 3015.8, 0.8% apart) and not a record of the ledger capture's environment (the ledger walls are head `8c6f2bc94`, manifest `a55324f9...`; this session is head `7d97832d3`, manifest `be32e719...`, so the production dispatch changed too). **Review (`20261007T030945`):** joins reject missing/mismatched host, source head, manifest, model path, fixture, decode-step and protocol identities across runs; raw profiler bench artifacts and generated-ID repeatability are verified. The remainder is an estimate from separate unprofiled wall and profiled kernel measurements, not a direct host-gap measurement. No production/default change. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-07-qwen4exp-de11-paired-decode-wall-kernel/`, worklog `20261007T030458`, corrected by `20261007T030945`. |

## 6. MTP open work

**State.** Explicit, default-off, capped at 1K context. Serial exact target
verification is the only admitted verifier. The retained ten-prompt serial
provider is exact and reaches **0.7407x** true AR, regressing every category
(`20260902T092733`). Every verifier that batches target rows has either changed
a token or been slower (§7J).

**What the fast engines do differently.** gufo's decode arithmetic is designed
to be **width-invariant**: verification "keeps the same quantization and
reduction as single-token decoding", its integer-WMMA Q8 verifier "preserves
K8 partials/FMA order and reduces each result once" through 48 rows, and its
batched decode matches isolated execution exactly at C2-C8. That is what lets it
verify several drafts in one pass. Ours differs: the multirow prefill path and
the c1 decode path use different kernels and arithmetic, so a batched verifier
built from prefill primitives fails the state RED (`20260902T082732`).

| ID | Mechanism | Evidence | First bounded experiment | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MT-1 | **Width-invariant rows≤8 verifier.** One target pass over the draft rows that uses the c1 decode arithmetic per row, so every row equals what serial decode would have produced | The pooled deferred-head verifier is exact but its 0.172 ms head saving cannot pay for the transaction (−3.52% complete, −12.7% public); the missing piece is a body pass whose cost grows sub-linearly with rows while keeping c1 arithmetic | Pick the two largest decode owners (`DE-1`, `DE-3`) and build rows≤8 variants that reproduce the c1 kernels' per-output reduction bit for bit; RED against serial `target.step` at rows 2-8 before any timing | BODY PASS FOUND — already exists and is sub-linear; independent diagnostics below remain open **BODY PASS FOUND - ALREADY EXISTS AND IS SUB-LINEAR (`20260929T`).** The stated blocker was "a body pass whose cost grows sub-linearly with rows while keeping c1 arithmetic". **Both halves are already satisfied for the q8_0 linear family (DE-3) by an existing registered kernel**, so no new kernel is needed for that half. **Arithmetic (RED first):** two links were already tested but never COMPOSED - `test_q8_0_pack8_f32_matches_scalar_gemv_bits` (c1 decode == scalar GEMV, rows=1) and `test_q8_0_f32_rowbatch32_matches_scalar_prefill_bits` (rowbatch == scalar GEMV, rows=37). Link 2 is checked at a SINGLE width and neither asserts **batch-composition invariance**, which is what a width-invariant verifier actually needs. New RED-first test `test_q8_0_coltile8_rowbatch4_is_width_invariant_to_serial_c1[2..8]` compares `gguf_q8_0_gemv_coltile8_rowbatch4_f32_f32_out` at width `rows` against `rows` separate single-row `gguf_q8_0_pack8_gemv_f32_f32_out` launches, `assert_array_equal`, no scalar-GEMV intermediary: **7/7 widths pass**. **Cost (q8_0 pair at attn_qkv 2560 -> 10240, median of 30 after 5 warmups, sync both sides): rows=1 82.1 vs 65.9 us (**0.80x**); rows=2 94.7 vs 108.2 (**1.14x**); rows=4 116.1 vs 193.6 (**1.67x**); rows=8 198.8 vs 367.8 (**1.85x**).** Per-row cost falls **82.1 -> 24.8 us (3.3x)**; 8x the rows costs **2.42x** - clearly sub-linear. **rows=1 is slower**, so the pass must not be used at width 1. **Caveats:** NOT an end-to-end verifier speedup (kernel pair only; MT-1 needs the whole target pass sub-linear and this is the DE-3 component); **does NOT cover DE-1** (MoE expert GEMV pair, 11.3 ms/token) - a separate link in the same chain; part of the 1.85x is **launch-count reduction, not throughput** (the serial arm issues `rows` launches); no MTP end-to-end run. **Scope change:** the row assumed we must BUILD rows<=8 variants; for the q8_0 linear family that build is already done and in the prefill path today (rows=1024 in the p4096 prefill census). Remaining work is composing it into a verifier body pass and measuring the COMPLETE pass - a different and smaller unit than the row assumed. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-mt1-rowbatch-cost/README.md` **COMPOSED AND MEASURED (`20261007T`) - THE COMPLETE BODY IS NOT c1-EXACT, AND THE BLOCKER IS THE rows>1 DISPATCH, NOT THE LAYER STRUCTURE.** The remaining unit was "compose it into a verifier body pass and measure the COMPLETE pass". **The composition exists and is measured.** The strict decode QSA mixer is rows==1-only (`run_qwen4_exp_dense_qsa_token_mixer` rejects every width 2..8), but the rows>1 body that the prefill chunk already uses - `run_qwen4_exp_qsa_prefill_layer` (QSA), `run_qwen4_exp_gdn_layer` (GDN, shared with decode) and `run_qwen4_exp_ple` - was composed into a non-resetting verifier body with one batched head (`scripts/qwen4exp_mt1_batched_body_probe.py`). **On synthetic layer fixtures the rows>1 owners are bit-exact vs serial c1**: rows>1 QSA attention (rows=6) output/KV/index raw keys bit-exact, full QSA layer (rows=4) output/KV bit-exact, GDN layer (rows=3) output/conv/recurrent state bit-exact (`scripts/qwen4exp_mt1_verifier_body_probe.py`). **On the real target the composed body is NOT c1-exact.** Qwen3.8-Flash-Next UD-Q4_K_XL, production profile, toy chain `1..rows`: top-1 agreement **1.0** at every width 2/4/6/8, but hidden max abs delta **0.3125 / 0.3125 / 0.5176 / 0.5176** and logits max abs delta **1.0818** at all widths, with **row 0 already diverging** (hidden 0.1055, logits 0.5148). Row 0 of a batched body should equal one serial step, so the divergence is in the **rows>1 production dispatch**, not cross-row accumulation: the prefill chunk wraps the rows>1 pass in `raw_k_prefill_rowbatch_session`, the q8 MMQ prefill context and the wide-F16 activation context, so the model's non-q8_0 dense projections (Q4_K/Q5_K/Q6_K) and the batched attention/MoE run prefill-shaped kernels whose per-output reduction differs from the c1 decode GEMV. **The q8_0 rowbatch is width-invariant; the other quant paths are not.** **Disposition:** the composed body is diagnostic only - it is not in the engine and not wired into the provider (the prototyped `verify_target_block_batched_body` was measured and removed, because shipping a body that returns divergent hidden/logits is a correctness footgun). **MT-1's remaining unit is therefore arithmetic, not wiring**: the rows>1 dense/MoE/attention dispatch must reproduce the c1 per-output reduction per row at every width 2..8 before a batched body can carry the exact accepted prefix. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-07-qwen4exp-mt1-verifier-body-boundary/` (README, `probe.json`, `batched-body.json`). Limits: synthetic fixtures are small-shape; the real-target comparison is one toy token chain, one model/quant/host; top-1 agreement is reported only to localize the divergence, not as the gate. |
| MT-2 | **Capacity beyond 1K** | Provider, draft runner and request checks all enforce 1,024 tokens; the 4K MTP suite cannot run | Audit allocation/state accounting beyond 1K independently; enable only after a paying verifier plus capacity/quality/state gates | OPEN for diagnostics; admission blocked |
| MT-3 | **Adaptive / cost-aware draft depth** (gufo default adaptive 1-7; halo-box PR #102 learns verify cost per width online) | Depth budget fitting was blocked by the 1K cap and the absence of a paying verifier | After `MT-1`/`MT-2`, fit on a disjoint partition, never on the evaluation prompts | BLOCKED **BLOCKER RE-CHECKED AFTER `MT-1` (`20260929T`) - STILL BLOCKED, ON `MT-2`.** The row waits on "`MT-1`/`MT-2`". `MT-1`'s body pass is now found (rowbatch 2-8 is width-invariant, 7/7 widths `assert_array_equal`, and sub-linear at 8x rows costing 2.42x), but `MT-1` records that its remaining unit is **composing that into a verifier body pass and measuring the complete pass**, which is not done. **`MT-2` ("capacity beyond 1K") is still open**, and this row needs both. The row's own constraint is unchanged and still binding: fit on a **disjoint partition, never on the evaluation prompts**. |
| MT-4 | **Cheaper draft head** (halo-box PR #101 vocabulary subset: draft step 3.13 → 1.42 ms on Qwen3.6-35B-A3B; our compact-head rung) | Even deleting our 3.153 ms full head left serial MTP at 0.964x AR, so the head is not the blocker today | Re-open after `MT-1` changes the economics | BLOCKED on `MT-1` **BLOCKER RE-CHECKED AFTER `MT-1` (`20260929T`) - STILL BLOCKED, AND NOW FOR A PRECISE REASON.** The row says "Re-open after `MT-1` changes the economics", and `MT-1` has since found its body pass. **But `MT-1` explicitly records that its result is NOT an end-to-end verifier speedup**: it is the kernel pair only, and it "does NOT cover DE-1 (MoE expert GEMV pair) - a separate link in the same chain", with part of the 1.85x being launch-count reduction rather than throughput. So the economics this row waits on **have not changed**: the row's own reason for closing is "even deleting our 3.153 ms full head left serial MTP at 0.964x AR, so the head is not the blocker today", and a sub-linear body pass that has not yet been composed into a verifier pass does not alter that. **What would re-open it**: `MT-1`'s remaining unit - composing the rowbatch pass into a verifier body pass and measuring the COMPLETE pass. Stay closed until that lands. |
| MT-5 | **Prefill-side MTP cost** (gufo: one-row MTP prefill lag keeps 320 KiB of hidden state instead of replaying a final chunk; final-tile catch-up restricts the predictor to the last aligned tile) | Unmeasured for us | Measure our MTP prefill catch-up cost at 1K first | MEASURED — prefill catch-up cost, 8 KiB/token per array, 0.176 ms at 1K **MEASURED (`20260929T`).** `scripts/mtp_prefill_catchup_cost.py`, CPU-only host-side, 9 reps after 2 warmups, `hidden_size` 2048 (Qwen3.6-35B-A3B `text_config.hidden_size`). At 1K: **8.00 MiB retained by the caller + 8.01 MiB created by the catch-up = 16.01 MiB total, 0.176 ms**. Linear at **8 KiB/token per array**: 4096 -> 64.01 MiB / 2.503 ms, 16384 -> 256.01 MiB / 9.973 ms. Reference approach (one lagging row): **8,192 B = 8.0 KiB, constant**. **Decomposition, because conflating the two terms would overstate it:** `retained_hidden_bytes` is NOT caused by the catch-up - it is the prompt hidden rows the caller already holds for `draft.prime_prompt`, and any priming approach pays it. `catchup_peak_bytes` IS the catch-up's own marginal cost: the right-shifted `concat([zero, hidden[:-1]])` copy, avoidable in principle by having the consumer read with a one-row offset. So the catch-up itself costs **8.01 MiB and 0.176 ms at 1K**, not 16 MiB. **Time is not the issue** (0.176 ms at 1K, 9.973 ms at 16K, small against a prefill of the same length); **memory is, and it scales** - at 8 KiB/token per array a 128K context holds **1 GiB retained + 1 GiB copied**, where the reference holds 8 KiB. The gap is **structural, not a tuning miss**: the reference keeps one lagging row and replays the final chunk, we materialize the whole prompt hidden state plus a shifted copy, so closing it means changing how the draft is primed. Limits: host-side only (excludes the prefill, the draft model and all device work); bytes exact, milliseconds are medians on one host; `performance_claim: false` - a cost measurement, not a speedup. Next: whether the shifted copy can be dropped via a one-row offset in the consumer, removing the 8 KiB/token second array without touching the priming contract. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-mtp-prefill-catchup-cost/README.md`|
| MT-6 | **Compact recurrent rollback** (gufo: one full state plus exact FP32 update operands, ~147 MiB/session at seven drafts) | Our rejection-depth RED grid passes; rollback memory cost at depth unmeasured | Charge rollback bytes in the context-capacity ledger once `MT-2` opens | OPEN, after `MT-2` |
| MT-7 | **Benchmark protocol** | Speedup claims need a true no-MTP AR baseline from the same protocol on the full mtp-bench category suite (AGENTS.md anti-gaming rules) | Keep; no single-prompt or repetitive-only rows | Standing rule |
| MT-8 | **Duplicate replay forwards and dirty-destination rollback** (`E6`) | External review records rollback-slot fixes that avoided extra target forwards; our cost at each rejection depth is not inventoried | Count target/draft forwards, restored bytes and allocations per accepted token at every rejection depth, including all-reject and full-accept. Audit existing REDs before adding dirty-destination full/partial restore and shared-weight no-double-count tests. Remove redundant replay only when transaction/control gates pass; price the complete cycle | **DIAGNOSTIC COMPLETE (`20261007T`).** The CPU-only four-token accounting screen covers all rejection depths plus full-accept. Target forwards are 1/2/3/4/4 for all-reject/reject-after-1/2/3/full-accept; draft forwards are 4 in every case. The provider stops at the first mismatch and restores the draft by cursor trim; restored bytes, restore allocations and per-cycle allocations are all zero, with one shared resident-weight attachment not charged per cycle. No duplicate target replay or dirty-destination full-state restore exists in this route, so no production change is justified. `performance_claim: false`. Artifact: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-07-qwen4exp-mt8-rollback-replay-accounting/accounting.json` | Reopen only with a transaction redesign that introduces distinct state/KV/recurrent ownership or a real dirty-destination restore path. |

`MT-1` pursues a width-invariant verifier because it simplifies exact
verification; it is not the only admissible production design. A distinct
width-specific T1/T2 verifier may be screened under §3.1, with its declared
acceptance algorithm unchanged and exact accepted-prefix/rollback ownership.
Numeric state differences alone are not an ownership failure. Such a candidate
must pass the full numerical/task gate and full-suite true-AR economics before
admission; an algorithm or acceptance-policy change is a separate experiment.
Likewise, capacity accounting and `MT-5`/`MT-8` diagnostics can proceed before a
paying verifier exists. Enabling larger-context MTP remains blocked on the
combined capacity, state, quality and economics gates, not just removing a cap.

## 7. Tried and not retained, by functional block (the registry)

Rows here are **not** to be re-run unchanged. Outcome classes:

- **accuracy** — failed the numerical or task gate;
- **perf** — exact or admissible but slower or neutral on the complete owner or whole model;
- **state** — failed determinism, state or parity;
- **withdrawn** — promoted, then disabled by the September 14 recovery (`49ffa3cb5`, `PRODUCTION_ARITHMETIC_RECOVERY_FLAGS`) because the composition failed max KL 0.0546;
- **dead** — measured not to address the bottleneck;
- **blocked** — stopped by a structural or evidence prerequisite.

Promotions that are live are listed per block for context; their evidence is in
STATUS and the cited entries.

### 7A. Dense projections

Live: `dense_wide256` at **13-47** (`20260916T205304`, extended `20261004T`),
F16 activation staging (`20260926T143853`) - **confirmed engaged by symbol census
and priced at +4.09% prefill end to end (`20261004T132957`)**, exact Q8 wave-scale
prefill (`20260905T234311`), its software-pipelined leaf (`20260929T083913`,
`DP-8`), and its DPP wave reduction (`20261004T`).

Canonical protocol: 12 cases, 1548 rows, 128 decode steps, chunk 1024, 3 repeats, determinism passed on all 12 prompts, teacher `coltile8_rowbatch4_f32_f32_out`, WMMA and dense_wide cleared in both arms. 0-11 also fails (mean 1.274e-3, 10 rows over review). **The failure is not a 0-15 boundary artefact and narrowing does not fix it**: `DP-8`'s ladder shows 0-7 worse than 0-15 (mean 1.354e-3 vs 1.177e-3) and 0-7 worse than 0-47, so the drift is concentrated in the earliest layers rather than cumulative, and every scope with marginal value fails. **The same mechanism passes at 16-47** (mean 3.236e-4, p95 1.629e-3, max 1.610e-2, top-1 0.99483, no rows over review) **Its value where it does pass is now measured rather than estimated: the 13-15 composition saves ~90 ms = 0.6% of prefill** (two runs, 4096 rows, chunk 1024, 900 staged launches against 0 in the baseline and a one-for-one replacement of the exact-chain leaf; `DP-8`). That is the deepest passing scope, and it is a narrow one: the same anchor charges 3262.5 ms of exact-chain dense-family time to layers 0-15, so the region where the value would be large is exactly the region that fails the envelope - and no number is offered for it, because this packet's own non-monotonicity forbids the extrapolation. | `20260929T083913`; artifacts `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-q8-staged-f32-dense-prefill-layers-gate/artifact-layers0-15-4cat.json`, `...-layers16-47-4cat.json` | A different layer set. The *tile* conclusion stands unchanged (every registered coltile/rowbatch instantiation was swept and the production one wins), but "occupancy at the register-only maximum" should not be quoted as a property of this leaf, because it is what makes an occupancy-shaped retile look impossible when it is merely unmeasured. The VGPR is dominated by the 32 accumulators plus the two pipelined operand stages (2 x (COL_TILE + ROW_BATCH) = 24 floats). | `20260915T182013`, `20260831T124221` | — |
| Wave reduction: DPP instead of `__shfl_down`, and the launch order | perf | **DPP IS RETAINED - BIT-IDENTICAL AND 1.5-1.7% (`20261004T`).** The five `__shfl_down` levels compile to `ds_bpermute_b32`, one LDS round trip each: 128 of them per thread plus 32 `ds_swizzle_b32` and 113 `s_waitcnt` (mostly `lgkmcnt`), against only 55 FMA-class instructions in a 378-instruction static stream - the reduction is about 40% of it for 32 accumulators. `h7c_reduce_wave_accumulators_dpp`, already in the file for the q6_k prefill kernels, folds the same pairs in VALU (`v_permlanex16`, `v_mov_dpp` row shifts 8/4/2, `v_add_f32_dpp row_shl:1`) with no LDS access. **The tree is the same tree** - 16, then 8, 4, 2, 1 - so it is an implementation swap: bit-identical on the raw `uint32` bits at eight shapes and against the wave-scale grandparent, and 0.0 max-abs-diff on five real layers. **378 -> 309 static instructions at IDENTICAL VGPR (76) and occupancy (25 waves/CU)**, and 15.8936 / 15.8694 / 15.8689 / 15.8438 / 15.8465 ms against the parent's 16.1395 / 16.1421 / 16.1266 / 16.1177 / 16.1191 on layers 0/9/22/34/46, **mean -1.64%, all five winning**. Promoted as the preferred leaf behind `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q8_DPP_REDUCTION` (default on), `is_registered`-guarded so the chain degrades DPP -> pipelined -> wave-scale parent -> unscaled exact parent; the engine-level state gate passes with five hashes identical across three arms and the DPP leaf's call count 0 / **1072** / 0, so engagement is recorded rather than assumed. **The launch order is the negative: `ROW_FIRST` swaps the grid axes to keep one 21.8 KB weight slice resident across the row sweep instead of streaming all 27.85 MB per row batch against a 32 MB MALL. It is free - identical VGPR, identical instruction count, bit-identical output - and 2.1-2.9% SLOWER on all five layers.** If the weight re-read were the binding cost this could not lose; sequential column-major streaming is better because it is what the hardware prefetches. **Third independent refutation of the traffic reading of this family**, after the GR up retile (halving a modelled 90.3% re-read costs 16%) and `HC-3` (removing 4.2% of modelled traffic costs 2%). **So the 6.2x issue gap this leaf sits inside is not closable by any locality change.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-q8-prefill-dpp-reduction/`, `worklog/entries/20261003T220950.002513Z` | `20261004T` | The operand representation - `dense_wide256` at 6.74x on this same operation is registered and blocked only on a production gate |

| Constant-accumulator row reuse RB8/H1; equal-accumulator RB2/H4; GR-specific MMQ64x128 | perf | RB8/H1 loses on both real weight banks; RB2/H4 not repeatably positive; 64x128 rejected | `20260906T075517`, `20260906T080204`, `20260906T021059` | The LDS-staged weight path (`HC-1`) **WIDENING IS NOW DEAD IN THE FORM THAT MATTERED TOO, AND BY A MEASURED MECHANISM (`20261004T`).** The rows above are constant-accumulator retiles. The untried form - `ROW_BATCH` 8 with `HIDDEN_TILE` 2, which keeps every operand load count per FMA at the parent's and halves only the weight decodes per output (8 decodes per k-step per thread for 64 FMAs instead of 32) - is **0.833-0.844x** against the production wave-scale parent on two real Q8_0 banks at `rows` 512 and 1024, 20 order-balanced pairs per case, both order strata agreeing, and `ROW_BATCH` 16 is **0.430-0.437x**. All arms are **bit-identical** to the parent (`gate` and `mixed` as raw `uint32`, 32 case rows, twice per case), and the screen also reproduces the parent's recorded production time (6.35-6.39 ms at rows=1024 against the attribution's 6.529 ms). **The mechanism is occupancy, not spills**: `acc[ROW_BATCH][4][2]` is a direct register cost, and a static census of the compiled profile gives VGPR 72 / 130 / 256 with 28 / 15 / 8 waves per CU (87.5% / 46.9% / 25.0%) for widths 4 / 8 / 16, scratch 0 throughout. **This also refutes the cost attribution that motivated the lever**: if 90.3% of the kernel's traffic were a DRAM-served 256x re-read, halving it could not cost 16% and quartering it could not cost 2.3x. The re-read is cache-served and the kernel is latency-bound at 87.5% occupancy; its real cost is the per-output structure (a 128-way reduction tree per output, a per-row-batch decode, and a 2.5-iteration K loop), which is what `HC-1`'s staged tile removes. That correction matters for how `HC-1` is ever re-argued, and it does not reopen it. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-10-04-qwen4exp-gr-row-batch-width/`, `worklog/entries/20261003T213357.974883Z` | A machine class where 130 VGPR still buys 32 waves/CU, or a body change that lowers the accumulator cost |

| Journey tiled/DPP (`R01`) / multi-column (`R02`) restorations | accuracy (task) | **`R02` PROMOTED TO PRODUCTION (`20260930T`) - MEASURED 1.34x-1.68x FASTER THAN THE DPP ROUTE IT REPLACES, at NMSE ~1.8e-14 (fp32 round-off), with the production numerical gates passing; the correction below is what unblocked it.**  **CORRECTED (`20260930T`): THE ATTRIBUTION WAS WRONG - `R02` DID NOT FAIL THIS.** This row combined `R01` (tiled/DPP) and `R02` (multi-column) under one evidence entry - `20260914T201148`, which is **`R01`'s** entry - and carried `R01`'s Japanese failure across to `R02`. **The `R02` artifact contradicts that.** `benchmarks/results/2026-09-15-journey-gdn-multi-restoration/README.md` records the full 18-prompt/594-row short gate **passing** (mean KL 1.0724e-04, p95 3.9221e-04, max 9.67834e-03, 593/594 top-1) and the canonical depth gate **passing every declared limit** (mean KL 8.92215e-05 <= 1e-3, p95 4.206676e-04 <= 5e-3, p99 1.2666802e-03 <= 2e-2, max 4.4548289e-03 <= 5e-2, top-1 777/780 = 99.615% >= 99%), and states outright: **"The candidate does not reproduce the single-column Japanese speculative-decoding error."** `R02`'s binding finding is a **different prompt** - `heldout_general_en_quant`, where the candidate "conflates native AMD INT8 capability with library support and FP16 emulation" - and the artifact scopes it itself: "This one paired finding is **not proof of worse expected quality or a GDN kernel defect**." **So `R01` failed the Japanese complete-EOS task; `R02` did not.** They are separate candidates with separate entries and separate findings, and this row's single citation covered only the first. The top1 differences `R02` does record (3 of 780) are explicitly **in-envelope**: "Three top1 differences are permitted by the production envelope; exact generated IDs are not the admission criterion." **The removal premise this row fed into `docs/REFACTOR.md` rested on the misattribution and has been corrected there.** `20260914T201148` (R01), `2026-09-15-journey-gdn-multi-restoration` (R02) | A passing predeclared task review | **THE PROMOTED BINDING WAS UNREACHABLE AND THE ROUTE RAN ZERO TIMES IN PRODUCTION (`20260930T`).** `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_GDN_TILE16_VARIANT` is read only inside the runner's `if colwarps_prefill:` block (`hipengine/runtime/qwen4_exp_runner.py:6038-6047`), and the binder bound it under `production and quant == "gguf_ud_q4_k_xl"` - but the UD quant **is** the arithmetic-recovery profile and `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL` is one of `PRODUCTION_ARITHMETIC_RECOVERY_FLAGS`, so the UD binder sets that flag to `"0"` and closes the very gate the variant feeds. The variant was named exactly where it could never dispatch and cleared exactly where the gate is open (`gguf_q4_k_m`), so the promoted route ran **zero** times in every production profile; the condition predates this promotion, so the DPP route it replaced was equally unreachable and the earlier "the env binding is the live path" claim was wrong for both. **Measured with `scripts/qwen4exp_gdn_multicolumn_route_probe.py`** (wraps the registered tile16 callables and the selection predicate, then runs a real prefill at rows=512, Hk16/Hv48/Dk=Dv=128, production): before the fix the predicate was reached 15/15 returning True while the env var read inside it was `''` and multi-column dispatches were **0**; after the fix the env var reads the multi-column variant and dispatches are **30**, with other tiled dispatches 0. The binding now tracks the gate it feeds (`!= "gguf_ud_q4_k_xl"`), and two unit tests that pinned the old unreachable contract were rewritten to pin the corrected one. **The 2026-09-15 artifact above remains valid and is what validates the route** - its candidate environment carries `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL: "1"` alongside the multi-column variant, which is the configuration the route needs; it does not by itself cover the q4_k_m production profile this fix now enables, so a new candidate `production_gdn_multi_q4km` (empty env override, `count_registered_dispatch=True`) is the gate that does. **Also corrected: the 2026-09-30 `gdn_multi_column` artifact is vacuous for this route** - its candidate env sets only the variant on a UD base profile whose gate is closed, and it records `dispatch_count_mode: "not_counted"` with `candidate_dispatch_calls: null`; its differing logits hashes come from the strict-vs-production base profile, not from the candidate. **THE REPAIRED BINDING NOW DISPATCHES, THE ROUTE IS EXONERATED, AND THE PROFILE IT RUNS IN IS THE DOCUMENTED UNRECOVERED COMPOSITION (`20260930T`).** Three results from `production_gdn_multi_q4km` and its attribution control `production_gdn_tiled16_q4km` (18 prompts, 24 decode steps, 3 repeats, chunk 512, BF16 KV, gfx1151, machine `55ea6c509d0b49eea8de7094a1023668`). **(1) Engagement: 1350 dispatches** of `qwen4_exp_gdn_prefill_tiled16_multi_f32`, against **0** before the repair - the route is no longer inert in the profile whose column-warp gate is open. **(2) The gate FAILS, and the control fails identically and slightly worse**, so the failure is not this route's: multi max KL **0.05958** and `prefill_last` mean **1.333e-3** against tiled16's **0.06645** and **1.475e-3**, with the same five scope failures and the same single over-review row (`heldout_general_ja_speculative` step 12, top-1 unchanged, strict margin 0.9091, top-5 overlap 1.0). Both arms ran 1350 dispatches of their own kernel and produced different candidate logits hashes while using the **byte-identical strict teacher** (`strict_logits_sha256` `e5ae994e...`, the value the UD gates recorded), so the arms differ only in the GDN prefill owner. **(3) The failure is the documented pre-existing condition, not a new one:** `2026-09-14-q8-prefill-numerics` measured this composition at max KL **0.054642** with prefill-last mean/p95 also failing, and its Conservative Recovery section states the decision in advance - *"The Q4_K_M profile, model files, KV ABI and other backends are unchanged"* - because the recovery zeroed the fifteen arithmetic selectors for `gguf_ud_q4_k_xl` only. **The gate also exposed a provenance defect this row's promotion introduced, now fixed:** both q4_k_m arms recorded `candidate_manifest_sha256` `a47225d0...` while different kernels dispatched underneath, because the GDN selection's variant was conditional on a `dpp` parameter true only for the quant that also passes `recovery=True` - and the recovery pass replaces tiled variants by name before the manifest is built, so that branch never reached a manifest while its `dpp=False` counterpart reached the q4_k_m one unchanged. The row is now unconditional and the unused parameter is gone: `gguf_q4_k_m` moves `a47225d0... -> 50da0d06...` and declares `qwen4exp_gdn_tiled16_multi_prefill`, matching the env the runner reads, while `gguf_ud_q4_k_xl` stays byte-identical at `a55324f9...`, so no UD certificate is invalidated. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gdn-multicolumn-q4km/gate.json`, `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gdn-tiled16-control/gate.json`, worklog `20260930T141619`.

## 8. Measurement rules for this model

1. **The 1x authority is `role-analysis.json` `exact_role_kernels`**
   ([`2026-09-17-qwen4exp-per-role-cost`](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-17-qwen4exp-per-role-cost/)).
   The raw `rocprofv3` CSV there is **two repetitions**; three artifacts
   published 2x counts before this was corrected on 2026-09-27.
2. **`rocprofv3` `Grid_Size_X/Y` are work-items, not grid dimensions.** The
   traced `(192, 1021, 1)` QSA launch is a `(6, 1021, 1)` grid.
3. **Classify complete semantic owners, not names alone.** The QSA family's
   largest historical member is a full-attention kernel. Conversion may be
   in-kernel or staged depending on the manifest; the F16 staging promotion
   makes the old “no separate conversion” reading stale. Reconcile symbols
   with role ranges and include fused work without double-counting.
4. **Do not add the A/B savings** in STATUS §2.4; they are separate
   experiments on different compositions.
5. **Rank by recoverable complete-request milliseconds.** A leaf ratio finds a
   row; it never ranks one. Many §7 rows are leaf wins that lost the whole
   model.
6. **A passing KL envelope beside failing top-1 requires localization.**
   Near-tie router amplification is one supported mechanism, not a universal
   diagnosis. Check `flip_eligible_rows`, route choices and the first divergent
   numerical/state boundary; do not waive top-1 or blame every miss on routing.
7. **Production candidates do not need bit-exactness**; the binding contract
   is [`EXECUTION-PROFILES.md`](EXECUTION-PROFILES.md). A byte-identical change
   is cheaper to justify, not more admissible.
8. **Competitor rows carry their basis.** Same-host fixture rows and
   author-reported rows never share a ratio. `llama-bench` prompts are
   synthetic tokens, so their MoE routing and PLE locality are not our
   fixture's (confound C3).
9. **Three prefill-window denominators are in use, and they are not
   interchangeable.** A percentage of "the window" means one of:
   **22,698.6 ms** (`role-analysis.json` at `725794c3f`, the 1x authority of
   rule 1, 9,652 kernel rows - MR-1, QA-5); **16,319.9 ms** (the 2026-09-28
   `qwen4exp-current-attribution` capture at `2789c19d6`, which is the
   reconciliation capture X-3's status names); and **15,742.0 ms** (that same
   capture's family-taxonomy union, which excludes time outside the taxonomy -
   HC-7). The spread between the largest and smallest is **44%**, so a share
   quoted against one base is **not** comparable to a share quoted against
   another. This is not an error in any row: each is internally consistent and
   each states its own basis. It is a reading hazard - when comparing two rows'
   shares, **check the denominator first**, and when quoting a new share, name
   the base. Rule 1 remains the ranking authority; the other two exist for
   reconciliation against the interval union and the taxonomy.

10. **A status cell must be read to its end before a row is worked.** Cells here
   have grown past 5 KB (DE-2 is 5,524 characters; MX-1 7,165), and the most
   recent conclusion is appended **last**, so a truncated read returns the
   row's *opening premise* while hiding the result that superseded it. This is
   not hypothetical: reading the first 2,200 characters of DE-2 on 2026-09-30
   produced a "the blocker is unresolved" premise for a row that was already
   fully priced, and the iteration spent on it had to be recorded as a skip.
   **Read the whole cell, or `tail` it, before forming a hypothesis** - and when
   a hypothesis looks surprisingly unaddressed for a high-ranked row, check the
   cell's end first. Related: an appended note that re-derives a figure the
   cell already bounds more tightly is a regression in the record, not an
   addition, so compare against the existing numbers before writing one.
11. **A raw `rocprofv3` kernel CSV from a mixed capture is not a decode
   measurement.** The X-4 directory's `x4full_kernel_trace.csv` (and its
   byte-identical `x4decode_` twin) holds the **whole capture - 18,941 rows,
   15,309 ms - including the prefill**; the decode attribution uses only the 3
   marker-scoped windows inside it (5,313 rows, 163 ms). Filter through
   `_read_marker_windows` + `_filter_kernels_by_windows` first. The failure is
   quiet because the contaminated number lands near the prefill's own, so it
   reads as corroboration: the router-logits kernel totals **616.5 ms
   whole-trace and 0.0 ms decode-only** (0.0 because the in-window rows are
   renamed to roles, below). Corollary: **`_annotate_kernel_roles` rewrites
   `kernel` to the role for every selected row** - all 5,313 of 5,313 carry a
   `qwen4exp_role:` name and none retains a kernel name - so **a name-to-kernel
   join is impossible inside any decode window this harness produces**, and a
   scoped marker is the only route to a sub-family split.

## 9. Cadence

- Re-rank after every retained change and after `X-3`. A promotion
  invalidates the family table for its own family and any family whose work it
  moved.
- Update [`QWEN4EXP-STATUS.md`](QWEN4EXP-STATUS.md) in the same commit as a
  promotion that changes it, and this ledger when a row changes state.
- A rejected row moves to §7 with its class, evidence, entry and reopen
  condition in the same commit that records the rejection.
- New candidates enter through this ledger, not through a new campaign
  document. External source reviews add rows here and cite the pinned commit.

For example, historical performance-campaign `R6` is decode attribution;
journey `R06` is GR-down arithmetic. Journey `R29` is comparator compatibility,
not missing dense-scope qualification; journey `R30` is final-stack comparison,
not just harness coverage accounting.
