### 10.2 Next experiments, without inventing a new ranking

1. **Refresh evidence:** X-3 and X-4 on the same current manifest, including
   X-5's scratch census and X-11's route engagement. Close X-6/7 qualification
   gaps in parallel with bounded kernel diagnosis.
2. **Run bounded high-cost-owner screens:** ED-1, DP-8, HC-1 and RT-1/DP-7,
   ordered by the refreshed complete-request costs. For changed routed tiling,
   remeasure tails/populations at each chunk rather than reuse a balanced model.
3. **Screen arithmetic candidates independently:** DP-1/3, MX-1, HC-6 and
   QA-2/GD-4 under §3.1. X-1 supplies comparison evidence; it is neither an
   approval nor a universal blocker. Do not infer their combined drift or gains
   by adding isolated results.
4. **Keep serving/depth work visible but separately priced:** QA-6/7, PL-1/2,
   RT-4/5 and DE-9/10 begin with cost/ownership measurements. Long-context
   allocation questions use the tier-1 probe in [HARNESSES](../benchmarks/HARNESSES.md)
   before full prompts; no native-length speed/quality claim comes from a fit test.
5. **Validate the composed result:** X-10, then update the state and benchmark
   rollup only for measured, quality-admitted changes. MTP remains a separate
   full-category/heldout true-AR comparison.

## 11. Open-candidate census (2026-09-30)

**October6 scratch correction:** the historical tables below classify X-5 and
DE-4 closed on a tree-wide zero-private-segment claim. That classification is
withdrawn; both live rows are partial. Current loaded-library-hashed ordered
screens complete the named Q4/Q8 decode coverage but do not establish universal
throttle absence. Use the live row states and the
[bounded packet](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-06-qwen4exp-x5-decode-victims/README.md),
not the historical closed count, for completion accounting.

**THE READING PASS IS DONE, AND THEN CORRECTED (`20261004T153054`, `20261004T153211`): 24 ROWS
NEED WORK.** All 72 status cells read head and tail, classified by hand; then a middle scan, which
found **25 of 72 rows state a status token mid-cell that head+tail misses and 4 of those flip the
row's state**. **21 open + 3 partial + 14 gated + 33 closed + 1 not-a-candidate = 72.**

**The four flips, and the method failure they record.** `X-6` read `BROKEN AT HEAD` at its head and
`cause found and fixed ... RED/GREEN verified` mid-cell -> **CLOSED**. `DE-6` read `OPEN, low` ->
`CLOSED: precondition not met`. `DE-8` read `OPEN, lower priority (R20)` -> `PRICED AND CLOSED
(20261001T): THE HEAD IS AT ITS OWN FAMILY'S READ RATE`. `QA-8` read `CLOSED - ALREADY
IMPLEMENTED` -> `Not closed: the prefill rows path still selects strict_rows_spans` -> **PARTIAL**.
**The head+tail method is therefore insufficient on 4 of 72 rows, exactly the failure mode the
method's own hypothesis named before the count was reported.** The other 21 mid-cell hits state a
token that does not change the row's state (a figure or paragraph withdrawn, a gap or route closed).

**`X-6` IS CLOSED, AND ITS CAUSE WAS NOT THE MEMOIZATION KEY.** Fix commit `ccd9f7a84` (*clear the
wide route in both arms of the wmma gate*), test `tests/test_unit_qwen4exp_wmma_gate_route_arms.py`,
**2 passed**. The memoization-key revision is recorded in X-6's own cell as **withdrawn** - *"the
false pass reproduced unchanged with the key fix in place"*. The real cause: the route cleared only
its own selector, never `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_Q8_DENSE_WIDE`, which the certified plan binds to
16-47; `_q8_dense_wide_claims_parent` accepts the wmma-rewritten parent as well as the exact parent,
so **the wide route claimed the dispatch in both arms and the gate compared one configuration
against itself**. **Consequence: `DP-7`'s sole named blocker is gone**, and DP-7's own measured case
is **-4.94%** (15.625 s -> 14.854 s). DP-7 should be re-checked for promotion.

| Closed | 31 (X-4, X-5, X-8, DP-1, DP-3, DP-6, DP-8, ED-1, ED-4, EG-1, EG-3, GD-1, GD-3, GD-4, HC-3, HC-6, HC-7, MR-1, MR-3, MR-5, MX-1, QA-5, QA-8, QA-9, QA-10, RT-1, RT-4, DE-1, DE-3, DE-4, MT-5) |
| Not a candidate | 1 (MT-7, `Standing rule`) |

**This is 26 rows needing work, not the 13 this section previously reported.** The 13 was not wrong
about the rows it examined; it was wrong about the universe, because it diffed only the 18-row
actionable set. **The omissions are large: X-6, DP-4, DP-9, PL-1, RT-2, RT-3, DE-2, DE-5, DE-6,
DE-7, DE-8 and DE-11 all need work and none was in the 18-row set.**

**`X-6` IS A SILENT FALSE PASS AND IT IS THE HIGHEST-VALUE ITEM HERE.** At HEAD, `--route wmma
--layers 16-47` (the *certified* configuration) returns **identical candidate/teacher logits
hashes, KL exactly 0.0, and `status: complete` with `measurement_valid: true`** — the teacher arm
does not resolve to the exact chain it claims, so the 16-47 gate cannot be re-run at HEAD to
reproduce its own certification. Verified cause: the dispatch memoization key carries `use_wmma`
as a boolean but **no WMMA layer-scope term**, so the key is identical between the route's two arms
and **the teacher arm replays the candidate dispatch**. **A gate returning KL 0.0 with
`measurement_valid: true` is worse than one that fails: every numerical gate run through that route
since 2026-09-29 returned a vacuous pass.** And **`DP-7`'s cell says its chunk-4096 promotion is
"blocked only on `X-6`"** — so this single defect is the sole blocker on a measured **-4.94%**
complete-engine prefill win. Fix the key, re-run the 16-47 gate, unblock DP-7.

**The head+tail reading method works where counting failed.** All 72 classified with no
unclassifiable row, agreeing with all five rows hand-verified earlier (DE-1, X-7, MX-1, MT-2,
MT-3). It resolves every row the token classifiers got wrong: `X-7` (`NOT RESOLVED` -> gated),
`DP-6` (`PARTIALLY PRICED` -> closed), `DP-4` (`THE GATE ... IS NOW SATISFIED` -> open, arm
unbuilt), `DP-9` (`re-ranked UP, not down` -> open), `X-4` (stale `still OPEN` prefix corrected by
a later `THE NAMED NEXT STEP IS DONE` -> closed). **Limits: head+tail is not a full read** — a state
stated only mid-cell would be missed, and `X-6`'s cell is 16,680 characters. Evidence: worklog
`20261004T153054`.


**RE-RUN (`20261006T`): 12 actionable / 18 gated / 40 closed / 1 not-a-candidate.** DP-2's native ownership candidate is correctness-qualified and its tracked-clean same-host canonical/heldout engine A/B is complete. It remains default-off pending the complete native owner screen (admission/dispatch/bookkeeping was excluded from the earlier primitive replay), not because a minimum headline gain is missing. It is recorded as a gated follow-up. The remaining actionable rows are unchanged from the preceding census; DP-2 is no longer counted among them.

**THE RULE BELOW HAS A DEFECT, now known.** It reads the END of each status cell to avoid stale
opening prefixes — but **DE-1 is the row that defeats it**: its cell *opens* with `CLOSED — BOTH
RUNTIME ROUTES AND BOTH FLAGS DELETED` and its *tail* is a limits-and-evidence statement with no
state token, so a tail-reading classifier returns "actionable" for a row whose first word is
`CLOSED`. **Corrected rule: a row is closed if a terminal marker appears ANYWHERE in its status
cell, with the end-of-cell read used only to resolve contradictions between a stale prefix and a
later conclusion.** Applied that way DE-1 classifies correctly and no other row in the 18 moves.
Evidence: worklog `20261004T152301`.

**THE SET BELOW IS ALSO INCOMPLETE, NOT MERELY STALE.** A full pass over all 72 rows
(`20261004T152443`) found four rows *outside* the 18-row actionable set whose status heads read
`OPEN`: **`MT-2`** (`OPEN for diagnostics; admission blocked`), **`MT-6`** (`OPEN, after MT-2`),
**`QA-6`** (`OPEN — after memory/consumer census, independent of QA-2`) and **`X-4`** (`stable wall
and serving-wrapper attribution still OPEN under X-10/DE-5`). **`MT-3`** is the control that
distinguishes omission from misreading — its head reads `STILL BLOCKED, ON MT-2`, which is why it
is correctly outside the set.

**AND THE COUNT CANNOT BE DONE BY COUNTING.** Three attempts have now produced three different
answers, the two automated ones wrong in opposite directions. The 2026-09-30 census read the END
of each cell and misclassified `DE-1`. An automated pass produced 51 "open" by swallowing rows
whose tails carry no state token. A second automated pass over all 72 rows produced 49 closed / 16
open / 3 gated / 4 unknown — and **classified `X-7` CLOSED when its status cell reads `NOT
RESOLVED`**, classified `QA-8` closed when its status reads `NOT CLOSED`, returned `DP-6` as
"unknown" for a status of `PARTIALLY PRICED`, and matched 22 negation-trap candidates among its 49
"closed" hits. **The failure mode is not the regex: the cells state their conclusions in 200-2000
characters of prose, and a token matcher cannot separate `CLOSED` from `NOT RESOLVED`.** The
remaining work is a **reading pass over all 72 status cells**, one at a time, each state recorded
with its quoted evidence. That is finite, needs no GPU, and is the only route to a census that will
not need retracting. Evidence: worklog `20261004T152443`.

**A RETRACTED SUB-CLAIM, recorded because the retraction is the useful part.** The same unit also
reported that `DP-6` was "unclassifiable — its entire status cell is 29 characters with no state
token". **That was wrong, and it was a parsing artefact of my own, not a ledger defect.** `DP-5`
and `DP-6` were the only two rows in §7A missing their **trailing pipe**; the classifier indexed
`cells[-2]`, which under a missing trailing pipe reads the *Track* cell as the *State* cell. The
rows have six content cells and align correctly. **`DP-6`'s state is `PARTIALLY PRICED
(20260929T)`, a 1.090% scope and an 85.791 ms ceiling.** The trailing pipes are now added and the
row is classified normally. This is the fifth time in this campaign that a conclusion was drawn
before the source was read; the durable rule is to check the artefact's own structure — here, its
cell count against its table header — before reporting that structure as defective.
Evidence: worklog `20261004T152356`.


The campaign's question is whether every candidate in this ledger has been run
through. That is a punchlist, not a single metric, so the answer is a
classification of all 71 rows by each row's **own declared state read from the
end of its status cell** (rule 10 — several rows' opening prefixes are stale;
see the DE-1 correction in the same commit).

**Method.** Every row's state token is taken from its status cell, and a row
counts as *closed* only when the cell states a terminal conclusion (CLOSED,
REJECTED, RESOLVED, PROMOTED, DELETED, ALREADY IMPLEMENTED, ROUTE REMOVED,
FALSIFIED, COST-GATED OUT, AUDIT COMPLETE, or equivalent). A row that is open
but names a precondition that must land first is *gated*, not actionable. A row
that is open with no outstanding precondition is *actionable*.

| class | count | rows |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| **Actionable now** | **17** | X-1, X-2, X-7, X-9, X-10, X-11, DP-6, ED-3, QA-4, RT-5, RT-6, DE-9, DE-10, MT-8, **MX-1**, **DE-1**, **MT-1** |
| **Gated** (open, precondition outstanding) | 16 | DP-4, DP-5, DP-7, DP-8, MR-4, QA-2, QA-7, PL-2, RT-6, MT-2, MT-3, MT-4, MT-6, RT-1, RT-2, **DP-2** |
| **Closed** (terminal conclusion) | 37 | X-3, X-4, X-5, X-6, X-8, DP-1, DP-3, DP-9, ED-1, ED-4, EG-1, EG-3, MR-1, MR-3, MR-5, HC-1, HC-3, HC-6, HC-7, QA-5, QA-8, QA-9, QA-10, GD-1, GD-3, GD-4, PL-1, PL-2, RT-3, RT-4, DE-3, DE-4, DE-6, MT-5, DE-7, DE-5, DE-8 |
| **Not a candidate** | 1 | MT-7 (standing rule) |

**Audit pass over the borderline rows (`20260930T`).** Re-reading the actionable
rows against the measured conclusions in their own cells moved two of them:

- **X-8 → closed, and its own answer re-prices QA-4 and QA-7 at depth.** The row
  asked for our context-scaling curve before any long-context kernel work, and it
  now has one: prefill **260.5 → 183.8 → 149.7 tok/s (8K → 128K → 262,008,
  −42.5%)**, against two comparators on the same host, file
  and tokens: pwilkin `40a9f4d01` is **flat** at 982.7 / 991.3 / 894.4, so the
  ratio widens **3.8x → 4.1x → 4.9x**, while upstream `6011c34ce` falls 49.4%
  (462.0 → 381.0 → 233.9) so the gap to it is only **1.76x → 1.59x → 1.27x**. The decode half of the ladder is a **host-clock
  measurement, not a depth curve**: 13.53 and 6.79 tok/s inside the 8K rung,
  51.6 ms/step at 8K against 52.4 at 32K when the host is held at boost, and the
  requested clock at exactly the 2,000 MHz base in **all 291 samples** covering
  the four largest rungs, with a fixed CPU workload 6.4x slower during a
  comparator rung and 50-64x during the 256K rung. **Across those states the
  comparator's decode does not move at all**, and settled our decode is at parity
  (1.09x at 8K, 1.04x at 32K), so the ladder's apparent decode gap is the host.
  **This is the depth half of the QA-4/QA-6/QA-7 bound:** the existing owner
  profile puts QSA index selection at **2.490 ms/step, 3.02%** of the 64K wall
  (1.116 ms, 1.35% at 16K), and the step is host-dispatch-bound at every depth
  measured (353-545 launches, 958 JIT build-cache-key resolutions, 209,933 Python
  calls per step, identical counts at p1024 and p65536), so a GPU-side epilogue
  cannot recover the decode wall on the current path. QA-4 stays actionable but
  demoted; QA-7 stays gated and is re-priced after the dispatch path is fixed.
- **QA-2 → gated.** Its own cell says the slice is **0.22% of prefill, not the
  7.90% premise**, and concludes the row "is now correctly cost-gated as
  low-yield, and a Tier-B tensor-core build should not be started ahead of the
  MoE/GR families". The state prefix read OPEN; it now reads cost-gated. **The
  same cell bounds QA-4, QA-6 and QA-7: "every attention-arithmetic candidate in
  this ledger shares a combined ceiling of ~1.5% of prefill"** - so QA-4, which
  stays actionable for its long-context lane, carries that ~1.5% short-context
  envelope as a bound.
- **GD-4 is closed, and the reason is that a cheaper escape already exists.**
  The FP32 chunked WY kernel its own last entry recommended is built,
  parent-parity correct, and slower than every route that matters (1.15-1.22x
  behind the route it was screened against, 1.15-1.26x behind the `wave_norm`
  route the UD-Q4_K_XL lane dispatches at 1K/2K, 1.75-2.26x behind
  `tiled16_multi`). The `VGPR_Count` 256 premise is **not** stale - it is the UD
  lane's own route - but `tiled16_multi` already escapes that bound at **85
  VGPR** and 1.39-2.39x the speed; it is registered and is the `q4_k_m`
  production selection, gated off on UD only by arithmetic admission. Its ~5.5%
  family ceiling is unchanged and still the same lever as GD-3 plus conv and
  norm.
- **GD-1 was flagged, then settled the other way (`20260930T`).** The audit left it actionable
  because its **Opportunity-and-limit** column reads "Inside noise, not additive" while its
  First-bounded-experiment column reads "Do not re-screen unchanged", and treated that as a
  contradiction needing a decision. **Reading the source of the two figures shows the columns
  agree and the audit's premise was the error.** They come from the 2026-09-16 disabled-family
  sweep on a 23.648 s `code-p4096` prefill, where `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL` is +0.228 s (+0.96%)
  and `GDN_PEER_PREFILL` +0.126 s (+0.53%), and the artifact's own limits section says ranks 5-9
  "are within the run-to-run spread this project has already measured, and should be read as 'no
  material effect' rather than as a ranking". So "Inside noise" is a sourced reading and "do not
  re-screen" is its correct action. The row also names a **kernel** for a **gate's** measurement:
  `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL=1` admits the **tiled16** owner on the envelope shape and uses the
  columnwarp parent only off it, and the columnwarp kernel is a measured 1.53x *slower* than that
  owner. Both flags are production-ON, so no candidate remained. **GD-1 is closed, and the
  actionable count falls 21 -> 20.** That is the fourth distinct failure of the same positional
  heuristic in this census - and the first one where the census itself, rather than a row,
  supplied the wrong reading.

**Contradiction sweep over the closed set (`20260930T`).** Every classification above
was a judgement over a long status cell, which is the same kind of reading that has
failed four times in this census. It was therefore re-checked mechanically in the one
direction that matters: **every row the table called closed was searched for an
explicit statement, in its own cell, that something of *its own candidate* remains
open** — "remains open", "remain open", "still open", "not promoted", "not yet
built", "the candidate build".

Thirty-two of the thirty-six closed rows contain no such statement. **Four do, and
all four were misclassified:**

| row | the sentence in its own cell that contradicts "closed" | disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MX-1` | "**MX-1 is NOT promoted**: this is an operand-error budget and does not replace the production mean/p95/p99/max KL, top-1, determinism, isolation or task gate; **the candidate build and the production gate remain open**", and its final append closes "**The pricing does not decide whether to build**" | **actionable - and it is the largest measured lever in the ledger** |
| `DE-1` | "**Unit (2) - the exact-activation warp256 variant - REMAINS OPEN and is NOT licensed by this result.**" | actionable (unit 2 only; the dp4a promotion itself is landed) |
| `DE-5` | "**Still OPEN:** no mechanism inside `reset()` is isolated - `state.zero()`, the per-attention KV memsets, `index.reset()` and the `_ple_hash_states` clear are all candidates and none is individually excluded." | actionable |
| `MT-1` | "Remaining work is composing it into a verifier body pass and measuring the COMPLETE pass - a different and smaller unit than the row assumed", and "**does NOT cover DE-1** (MoE expert GEMV pair, 11.3 ms/token)" | actionable |

**`MX-1` is the material one.** The census read its *first experiment* - the operand-error
budget, complete across all 144 expert tensors - as the row, and the row's own text says
the opposite: what is finished is the admissibility budget, and what remains is the
build. Its price is already known from the same X-3 authority as every other figure
here: the repair is **1,219.978 ms = 7.75% of the 15,741.963 ms prefill union**, with a
stated acceptance target for the build (the F16 gate/up+down must come in at or below
the 4,280.901 ms the iu8-risk owners currently take). **That is larger than `GD-4`'s
~5.5% ceiling, `DP-6`'s 1.090% scope, `DP-5`'s 0.832% cast and `DP-8`'s measured 0.6%,
and `EG-3` names `MX-1` as the sole remaining path to that cost.**

**Two closed rows pass the sweep and stay closed.** `X-4` says "**Still OPEN**: the stable
wall and serving-wrapper attribution remain open **under X-10/DE-5**" - the residual is
explicitly delegated, both named owners are now in the actionable set, and the row's own
named next step (analyze marker-scoped owners) is done. `X-6` says "**RT-3's blocker is
separate and still open**" - that blocker belongs to `RT-3`, whose own cell states a
terminal conclusion (premise falsified), and nothing of `X-6`'s candidate remains. In
both cases the sentence names *another row's* work, which is why the sweep keys on the
row's own candidate rather than on the words alone.

**A second reading of the same sweep, recorded because it shows the limit of the
instrument.** Taking the census's rule literally - "a row counts as closed only when the
cell states a terminal conclusion" - and testing whether the *end* of each cell states
one reports **30 of 36 closed rows as violations**. That is not 30 misclassifications:
most cells end with an evidence or limits line ("Evidence: `benchmarks/results/...`",
"One model, one quant, one host"), not with a status token. The rule is about the cell
*containing* a terminal conclusion for its own candidate, not about its final words, and
the crude form of it is useless. It is recorded because it is the fifth positional
reading to look decisive and be wrong.

**What this says about the loop.** The campaign is **not close to exhausted**:
**18 rows remain actionable and 15 more unblock when a named precondition lands**
(the table above is the current count; earlier paragraphs in this section quote the
series as it moved).
But most actionable rows are explicitly *low-value or lane-separated* — X-1 is
"informational, not a prerequisite", X-9 is
"maintenance", DP-2 is "low value", MR-3 is "low value", DE-6 is "low", DE-8 is
"lower priority", DE-9 is a "separate serving lane", QA-4 is a "long-context
lane" whose depth value is now measured at **3.02% of the 64K wall** — so the
count of rows is not a count of comparable prizes. **X-8 left this list on
2026-10-02 by being measured rather than deferred**: it was the row that named
the long-context lane, and it now returns a curve, a comparator ladder and a host
attribution instead of a promise. **But the census's own
conclusion that "the rows that carry measured millisecond-scale value are already
closed" is refuted by the sweep above, and was the most consequential error in this
section.** The largest measured lever in the ledger, `MX-1` at 7.75% of prefill, was
classified closed on the strength of its completed admissibility budget while its own
cell said the build remains open - and the sentence asserting that the valuable rows
were all closed was written in the same section that had just hidden one. The rows that
carry measured millisecond-scale value are **not** all closed: `MX-1` (7.75%),
`DP-6` (1.090%), `DP-5` (0.832%, gated on a buffer contract) and
`DP-8` (0.6%, measured) are all open, and the decode prize `DE-1` is open in unit (2)
rather than closed by scope-space exhaustion as this paragraph claimed. What *is*
closed is the largest decode prize: the dp4a logical128 promotion landed and the
remaining gap to its comparator fell from 2.94x to 1.12x (`DP-9`).

**The actionable count is now 22.** It was 25 in the first pass of this census.
The audit moved QA-2 out; attempting DP-5's build showed the row is gated on a buffer
contract that does not exist, which moved DP-5 out; and a systematic pass over every
actionable row's First-bounded-experiment column moved MR-3 out, whose columns agree
that it "should not be started". `DE-3` then moved out as a landed win the heuristic
had read as outstanding work, `GD-1` moved out once the source of its two figures
was read, and `DE-6` moved out when `X-4` was evaluated and its own precondition came
back false (see the note above). `PL-1` then moved out on its own terms - its flag was
withdrawn as a cell mis-assignment rather than a malformed contract - and the
contradiction sweep above moved **four back in**: `MX-1`, `DE-1` (unit 2), `DE-5` and
`MT-1`, all of which the table had called closed while their own cells said otherwise.

**One correction is worth recording as a class, because it is the worst kind found so
far (`20260930T`).** `DE-6`'s cell asserted *"DECODE IS NOT KERNEL-BOUND ... ~65% is
host/launch overhead"* as **"the governing new number for this row"**, and the
refutation of exactly that figure had already been written into **`DE-5`'s** cell - a
neighbouring row, by then closed. A closed row's correction does not reach an open
row that quotes the same measurement, and the reader who most needs the correction is
the one reading the *open* row. Where a shared measurement is refuted, the correction
belongs in **every** cell that quotes it. **That pattern was then swept for, and the
sweep came back clean (`20260930T`):** every figure this census has corrected or
refuted - the `153.0 ms` / `65% host-launch` decomposition, the `1.1% of prefill`
attribution, the `+0.228` / `+0.126` gate deltas - appears only in rows that are
already closed or in prose that cites the correction. `DE-6` was the sole open row
carrying one, so the class is recorded as a hazard rather than as a live backlog.

**That systematic pass is the reusable part.** Four of the five movements came from
reading a row's own content or its code rather than from any measurement, and two
rows were found carrying a First-bounded-experiment column that names **no experiment
at all** - MR-3's reads "Do not start here" and GD-1's reads "Do not re-screen
unchanged" - while PL-1's holds a *state* ("OPEN — workload-dependent, not a
warm-kernel priority") in the column where an experiment belongs. **The count is only
as good as the column it is derived from, and that column is not uniformly an
experiment.** MR-3 is closed; GD-1, PL-1 and X-7 are flagged rather than settled,


### Section 7's reopen conditions are recorded and never tracked

**Six section-7 rows describe routes that are current production defaults.** Verified by
reading the binder, not inferred: each named flag is bound to `"1" if production` in
`hipengine/generation/qwen4_exp_profiles.py`.

| row | flag | recorded reason | current state |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Dense Q8 MMQ stack | `Q8_MMQ_PREFILL` | failed canonical depth | **ON** |
| `PRODUCTION_MOE_PREFILL` / `Q4_IU8_PREFILL` | both | rejected candidate, keep strict fallback | **ON** |
| iu8 WMMA GR up/down | `GR_IU8`, `GR_IU8_DOWN` | mean KL 1.40e-3 / 1.31e-3 | **ON** |
| QSA flash prefill | `QSA_FLASH_PREFILL` | part of the failed recovery composition | **ON** |
| Column warps, peer | `GDN_COLWARPS_PREFILL`, `GDN_PEER_PREFILL` | inside run-to-run spread | **ON** |
| DP4A safe-43 decode | `Q4_DP4A64` | part of the failed composition | **ON** |

**Three of the six were withdrawn "by association"** - as part of a failed composition,
not on their own measurement. That is precisely the defect that hid the multi-column GDN
win, where `R02` was condemned by a row citing only `R01`'s evidence.

**One is settled and the row is simply stale.** `GR_IU8`/`GR_IU8_DOWN` were re-qualified
and promoted: the binder records GR up at **KL <= 7.4e-8 with 100% top-1** and a
**+9.2% prefill geo-mean**, GR down at **KL <= 5.5e-8** and **+2.0%** on top. The row's
recorded mean KL 1.40e-3 is **five orders of magnitude worse than the KL actually
shipped**, because the promoted mechanism is a different one (three-plane fp32 staging).
**The row's own reopen condition - "A structurally different mechanism" - was met, and
nothing tracked it.**

**That is the systemic defect, and it is more general than the ledger's arithmetic:** every
section-7 row carries a "Reopen only with" condition, and **nothing in the repository
evaluates whether one has been satisfied.** A row can be simultaneously (a) correct when
written, (b) superseded by later work, and (c) still read as a rejection by anyone
consulting the table - which is the table's only purpose.

**The consequence for this census is concrete**: section 7 is a candidate *source*, not
just a graveyard. The retained multi-column GDN win - 1.34x-1.68x - was found by asking
whether a recorded rejection still held. **Six more rows are now known to be contradicted
by current state, and three were condemned by association rather than by measurement.**
Whether any of those three holds a further win is an open question this census did not
answer.

### The census's status heuristic has now failed three separate ways

`DE-3` was counted **actionable** while its own status cell ends
*"...PROMOTED TO THE DEFAULT (`20260930T`)"* - it is a **landed win**, not outstanding work.
It is now closed (actionable 22 -> 21).

**Why it was missed**: this census reads a row's status from the **end** of its status cell,
on the reasoning that the newest material is appended last. `DE-3`'s cell ends with rollback
and test detail (`HIPENGINE_GGUF_PACK8_GEMV_WIDE=0` restores the 128-wide launcher default...
the surrounding bundle is green`), so the terminal conclusion sits **before** that detail
rather than at the end. The heuristic read operational tail as status.

That is the fourth distinct failure of the same heuristic in this census:

| # | row | how the heuristic failed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `QA-2` | its own cell cost-gated it, but the conclusion was not a status keyword |
| 2 | `MR-3` | its columns agreed it should not be started; no status keyword said so |
| 3 | `DE-3` | the conclusion is in the cell, **before** trailing operational detail |
| 4 | `GD-1` | the row was fine; the **census's own audit** invented a contradiction between two columns that agree once the figures' source is read |

**Two closures rather than another census correction (`20261001T`): `DE-5` and `DE-8` move to closed.** `DE-8`'s screen is the pricing the row asked for: the head reads 675.4 MB of Q8_0 weights per step at 226.7 GB/s, which is 1.071x the median of the four sibling Q8_0 decode GEMV families in the same capture, so there is no head-specific inefficiency and no lever inside the row's constraint. See the row.

**`DE-5`.** Its
remaining open item was the per-prefill loop-start transient, and it is now measured - a host CPU
package clock clamp held at the 2.0 GHz base clock for the first ~2 s of every post-prefill decode,
established during the prefill and released ~2 s after it. Graph capture, allocation churn and GC
are excluded by direct counters, and no synthetic load class reproduces the clamp. See the row.

**The count is derived from a positional heuristic over prose, and prose does not keep a
stable shape.** Each correction has been a reading, not a measurement, and each has moved
the number by one. That is worth stating plainly rather than presenting the current count as
settled. `GD-1` adds a second-order warning: the audit pass that was supposed to correct the
heuristic can itself introduce a row that then needs correcting.

**The audit's two "malformed row" flags were both checked against the rows' own headers, and one
was wrong (`20260930T`).** `PL-1` carries four cells against a four-column header, and the
experiment column holds a complete bounded experiment; the text the audit quoted as being *in*
that column is in the `State` column, where a state belongs. The flag was a **cell
mis-assignment**, and it is withdrawn. `X-7` carries five cells against a five-column header and
the flag was **correct**: its experiment column read "Close before extending the admitted scope",
an instruction to close that predates every entry above it in the State cell. That one is
resolved by writing the work the State cell had already named into the column, not by closing the
row. So the flags split one wrong, one right-but-mis-framed, against two rows — which is a
further reason to read the rows rather than the flags.

**The metric series is not a metric.** `results.jsonl` records 107 iterations
with 0 keeps and 0 reverts, and metric values spanning **0.00038 to 2245** —
shares, percentages, milliseconds, byte counts and candidate counts interleaved,
because `multiloop_measure` was called with whatever the iteration happened to
measure. The recorded baseline 10 / current 6 / best 10 are therefore not a
baseline, and `keep`/`revert` has never been meaningful for this loop.

**CURRENT COVERAGE AUDIT (`20261007T`).** The complete ledger has **75 logical IDs in 74 physical rows**: `HC-4` and `HC-5` remain folded into one table row. The audit is recorded in [`benchmarks/results/2026-10-07-qwen4exp-optimization-coverage-audit.json`](../benchmarks/results/2026-10-07-qwen4exp-optimization-coverage-audit.json). It reads every state cell and cross-checks the current cited packet rather than classifying by the first or last keyword. The result is **37 terminal/closed, 7 partial/incomplete, 15 open-actionable, 15 open-gated, and 1 not-a-candidate**. Thus **37 logical IDs still need work**; “needs work” includes partial rows and rows blocked by a named prerequisite. The previous census numbers are historical readings, not this split-ID result.

The two latest packets are accounted for explicitly. `DP-2`'s corrected rows-4096-only engine A/B covers 12 canonical plus 16 heldout cases, three repetitions per arm, D128, exact cross-arm outputs and deterministic repeats; the candidate remains default-off because complete p4096 prefill is **1.0018895x** parent despite the native operation saving. `MT-8` is a CPU accounting diagnostic covering all rejection depths and full accept; its zero restored bytes/allocations are not native GPU measurements and justify no production change. The highest-value unfinished work remains `MX-1`'s F16 expert-owner build/gate, `DP-7`'s rerun against the corrected X-6 gate, and `MT-1`'s complete verifier-body composition. This audit does not promote any candidate or claim that a partial row is closed.
